# Nashr: system architecture (as implemented)

Baseline written from the code on branch `claude/cool-tu-144390` (HEAD `4959f7e`), 2026-09-18. Media generation (kie.ai, Higgsfield, Vercel Blob, video) was added on 2026-09-29 from the code of that change, and brought up to date the same day with its review fixes (whole-task writes through `updateTask` under `tasklock`, the two-step refund marker, rate windows set with EXPIRE NX, the chat tool's render deadline, https on every download hop, a placeholder that never covers artwork). Main's changes of the same day were merged in: steps 1 and 2 of the agent-team upgrade (routines and their cron, per-agent caps and usage, decision records, feedback into prompts, the per-agent reviewer and the judge's automatic fails) and the shared-link onboarding fix (`onboard/confirm`, `onboardok:`). Phase 1 of Reem, the secretary agent (the owner's own to-dos, the computed agenda, `/api/secretary`, four free chat and MCP tools, the My to-dos page, the brief block and the overdue follow-up), was added on 2026-09-29 from the code of that change (spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-29-secretary-reem-phase1.md`), with its review fixes of the same day (links set by chat and MCP only at creation and only to a pending task, the idle-scope purge, a proposal ending a snooze, the open cap on restore, the current local date in every agent's prompt), then the five fixes from a comparison with the Taskuary repository (a chase clock for what others owe, one alert per overdue item spaced over days 0, 1, 3 and 7, notification markers claimed with SET NX, a time zone per brand, an agent's "done" shown as a claim next to a fact checked in code), then merged with main's teams change (Reem's routes, tools and page follow the role table: members write with `todo.write`, the owner and admins close with `todo.close`; each audit line names the acting login), and the `file:line` references into the files they moved re-derived. It describes what the code does, not what it should do. Paths are relative to the repository root. `file:line` references point at the first relevant line. Teams (seats and roles) were added on 2026-09-29 and merged with the media change the same day: sections 2.3 to 2.5, 3, 4(b) and 4(f) and 5 describe them. Step 3a of the agent-team upgrade (one generation pipeline `generate-batch.ts`, who acted `actor.ts`, governance history with restore, prompt-version stamps and the per-agent activity log) was merged with teams the same day; the line references in the rows and steps both sides had changed were re-read from the merged code. Main's Reem change (#225) was then merged into step 3a the same day, and the line references in the rows and steps both sides had changed were re-read from that merged code again. Pricing phase 1 (2026-09-30) added `pricing.ts` and `task-costs.ts` and changed `auth.ts`, `brands.ts`, `credits.ts`, `onboard-confirm.ts`, `schedule.ts`, `rate.ts` and `admin-stats.ts` (section 2.5), the weekly cron and the daily cron's onboarding hold sweep (section 2.6) and the keys `user:{email}` (`deal`, `onboardPaid`), `rate:brandadd:*`, `brandslock:{email}`, `schedule:{scope}` and `onboardlock:{scope}` (section 3). Main, with Reem's phase 1, was merged into pricing phase 1 on 2026-09-30: the route count (section 2.4) was recounted on the merged tree, and the `file:line` references into files both changes touched were re-derived from the merged code. On 2026-09-30 every `path:line` reference in this file was re-read against main at `2c71b74` and corrected where the code had moved, and the passages that re-reading showed out of date were brought up to the current code; when that correction (#229) was merged into pricing phase 1, the references into files pricing phase 1 changed were re-derived from the merged code. Main's pricing phase 1 (#228) and docs fixes (#229) were then merged into step 3a on 2026-09-30: the route count (section 2.4) was recounted on the merged tree, and every `path:line` reference into a file either side had changed was derived again from the merged code (step 3a's `generate-batch.ts` charges the chat's drafts, so the pricing rows name it). Step 3b part R (the cross-agent review: `crossReview` in `noor.ts`, `Governance.crossReview`, `Task.crossReview` and `TaskLite.xflag`, the `xreview` usage kind and the `cross_reviewed` activity kind) was added on 2026-09-30 from the code of that change; it adds no route and no key. Step 3b part O, handoffs with Majed as lead (`src/lib/handoffs.ts`, the chat tool `propose_handoff`, the routes `handoffs/[id]`, `handoffs/[id]/run` and `handoffs/[id]/decline`, the keys `handoff:`, `handoffs:` and `handofflock:`, `Task.parent` and `source: "handoff"`, the `<lead_brief>` block, the three `handoff_*` activity kinds and the chat's handoff card), was added the same day on top of part R. Its item 5, Majed's opt-in auto-run (`src/lib/handoff-auto.ts`, run by the routines cron; `Governance.majedAutoHandoff`; `isAutonomous` in `brand.ts`; the keys `handofftry:` and `handoffhold:`), was added after it the same day; it adds no route. Step 3a, with main's security gates (#227), pricing phase 1 (#228) and docs fixes (#229), was then merged into step 3b on 2026-09-30: the route count (section 2.4) was recounted on the merged tree, and every `path:line` reference that one side wrote into a file the other side had changed was mapped to the same line of the merged code; `flows.md` citations of step 3b's sections moved from 30-32 to 33-35 and those of step 3a's from 27-29 to 30-32. Phase 2 of Reem (2026-09-30: reminders at a set time, fired by an Upstash QStash tick with the daily cron as fallback, the walk through the list `secretary/walk`, the evening wrap `cron/evening`, and agents' handoff notes, which are not Majed's handoffs; spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-30-secretary-reem-phase2.md`) was added from the code of that change, and main (steps 3a and 3b) was merged into it the same day: the route count (section 2.4) was recounted on the merged tree, and the `path:line` references in the rows and steps both sides had changed, and main's references into the files phase 2 moved (`chat.ts`, `brand.ts`, `account.ts`, `mcp.ts`), were re-derived from the merged code. Pricing phase 2 (2026-10-01: the chat allowance, the owner's "continue on credits" switch, the $0.35 per-message ceiling and the history guard; spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-29-pricing-design.md` section 6 rules 1 and 2) added the route `billing/chat-overflow` (section 2.4), the modules `chat-budget.ts`, `chat-allowance.ts` and `usage-view.ts` and changes to `pricing.ts`, `chat.ts`, `credits.ts`, `usage.ts`, `auth.ts`, `account.ts` and `admin-stats.ts` (section 2.5), the keys `usage:{email}:{YYYY-MM}:chat_msgs`, `usage:{email}:{YYYY-MM}:chat_over`, `usage:{email}:{YYYY-MM}:chat_cred` and `chatm:{email}:trial`, the `rate:chatovf:{email}` bucket and the `user:{email}` fields `chatAllowance`, `chatOverflow` and `chatCarryMicro` (section 3), and steps 1-3, 6 and 7 of section 4(c), whose step 2 now also names the chat burst limit it had left out; every `path:line` reference into a file that change touched was mapped to the same line of the changed code. Its review fixes of the same day are in too: nothing is held before a message on credits (the gate checks the balance is at least `chatOverflowHold()` and `settleChatOverflow` bills the metered cost after the reply, at most that, into the new `chat_cred` counter), `ChatStatus` gained `counted` and `overflow.credits`, the first call's thread is shortened by `fitFirstCall`, a call cut off at `max_tokens` is never acted on, a workspace too large for one message gets its own reply and is not counted, the reply no longer carries `usd`, and the `chat.ts` line references that change moved were derived again. Reem phase 3 A1 (2026-10-03: the brand's work calendar, one chase rule and contacts who are away; spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-02-secretary-reem-phase3a-design.md`) added the routes `secretary/away`, `secretary/away/proposals/[id]` and `secretary/calendar` (section 2.4), the modules `secretary-calendar.ts` and `secretary-away.ts` and changes to `secretary.ts`, `secretary-agenda.ts`, `secretary-evening.ts`, `notifications.ts`, `chat.ts`, `seasons.ts` and `zones.ts` (section 2.5), and the keys `sec:cal:{scope}`, `sec:away:{scope}`, `secawayprop:{scope}:{id}`, `secawayprop-done:{scope}:{id}` and `secawayprops:{scope}` (section 3; rule 489 checks every `SEC_KEYS` family against this table); the `path:line` references into `chat.ts`, `noor.ts`, `schedule.ts`, `notifications.ts` and `ChatPanel.tsx`, which it moved, were derived again from the code.

## 1. Summary

Nashr is a single Next.js 16 app-router application (TypeScript, React server components, no separate backend) deployed on Hostinger as a Node.js web app (since 2026-10-08; on Vercel before, retired 2026-10-10, rules 987-990). It serves a bilingual (Arabic default, English) marketing site and an authenticated app under `/{lang}/app` in which a small-business owner, and since 2026-09-29 the admins and members the owner invites into the account, talk to eight AI agents (Noor marketing, Sami sales, Razan finance, Faris growth, Dana reporting, Majed executive, Layan product, Reem secretary; `src/lib/agents.ts`). Seven of them produce content "tasks"; Reem owns no task type and keeps the owner's own to-do list instead (`src/lib/secretary.ts`), for free. Agents call Anthropic models through `@anthropic-ai/sdk`, produce content "tasks" that land in an approval inbox, and can publish approved items to third-party platforms through connectors. All state lives in one Upstash Redis database reached through a thin adapter (`src/lib/db.ts`); when no Redis credentials are present the adapter silently falls back to a process-local in-memory `Map` (`memory`, `src/lib/db.ts:183`). There is no SQL database, no queue and no background worker: long work runs inside the request (route `maxDuration` up to 300 s), after the response via Next `after()` (`judgeLater`, `src/lib/judge.ts:192`, and the opportunistic media sweep `maybeSweep` in `src/lib/media/jobs.ts`), or in the GET cron jobs listed in `crons.json` (hPanel cron jobs on Hostinger) and one Upstash QStash schedule (Reem's tick, `/api/cron/secretary`, since 2026-09-30). Images and video come from fal.ai, kie.ai and Higgsfield and are copied to a Vercel Blob store (section 4(e)). Usage is metered in credits held on the owner's user record, shared by everyone in the account, and, since 2026-10-01, chat in messages: a monthly allowance per plan for the whole account, past which chat pauses unless the owner chose to continue on credits (`src/lib/chat-allowance.ts`, section 4(c)); who may do what inside an account is one role table (`src/lib/roles.ts`). The same agent capabilities are exposed to external AI clients through an MCP endpoint (`/api/mcp`) protected by personal bearer tokens or by a built-in minimal OAuth 2.1 authorization server; through it an owner's own Claude or ChatGPT subscription can also read the brand rules, check a draft and save it into the approval inbox without spending credits (`save_draft`, `src/lib/external-drafts.ts`; action `drafts`).

## 2. Component map

### 2.1 Edge / routing

| Component | File | Role |
|---|---|---|
| Proxy (Next 16 replacement for middleware) | `src/proxy.ts:22` | Matcher (`:64`): every path except `/_next/static/…`, exactly `/_next/image` and exactly `/favicon.ico` (the exclusions are anchored, `/((?!_next/static/\|_next/image$\|favicon\.ico$).*)`, so a look-alike such as `/favicon.icox` or `/_next/staticx/a` still meets the lock; it was only `/`, `/app` and `/:lang/app/:path*` until the staging lock, rules 674 and 677). Because the proxy runs on every `/api/*` request, Next clones and buffers every non-GET body before the route runs, so a route's own size cap, auth and rate limit all come after that read: a route's cap bounds what the route keeps and parses, never what the server reads. `experimental.proxyClientMaxBodySize` in `next.config.ts` caps the buffer at 6 MB (rule 774; Next's default is 10 MB), and past it the route gets the body cut short, never an error. The largest legitimate body is `POST /api/performance` (a CSV of at most 2,000,000 characters sent as JSON, at most 6,000,000 bytes); a future route that takes more must raise the cap and its test (`tests/staging.test.ts`). The same buffering reaches the webhook and cron routes, so their 64 KB caps refuse a larger body only after Next has read it; the exempt paths of `CROSS_SITE_EXEMPT` cannot be left out of the matcher, because the lock must see every path. On staging (`isStaging()`, `APP_ENV=staging`) a path outside `STAGING_OPEN_PATHS` without a valid `thakeen_staging` cookie (`hasAccess`) gets a bare 404 before anything else (`:28-30`), and every response the proxy handles carries `X-Robots-Tag: noindex, nofollow` on staging (`:54`; the static assets, `/_next/image` and `/favicon.ico` do not pass through it). Everywhere, and alone off staging: an `/api/` request gets the cross-site check (`:33-37`, `crossSiteRefusal` at `src/lib/csrf.ts:132`; rules 690-693): a POST, PUT, PATCH or DELETE that another site started is refused with 403 `{"error":"cross_site"}`, the paths of `CROSS_SITE_EXEMPT` are skipped, and every other request passes on untouched; `/` is redirected (307) to `/ar` or `/en` from the `thakeen_lang` cookie, then `Accept-Language`, default Arabic (`:12-20`, `:38-44`); `/app`, the installed app's start URL, the same way to `/ar/app` or `/en/app` (`:38-44`, rule 681); a path matching `^/[^/]+/app(/|$)` only has its pathname copied into the request header `x-pathname` (`:45-49`, the one reader is `src/app/[lang]/app/layout.tsx`); every other path passes through untouched (`:50-52`). It imports only `next/server`, `csrf.ts` (which imports only `origin.ts`), `deploy-env.ts` and `staging-access.ts`, never the store (`tests/staging.test.ts`). It performs no user authentication: the cross-site check reads who started a request, never a session. |
| Security headers | `next.config.ts` `SECURITY_HEADERS` | On every path: HSTS (2 years, subdomains), `nosniff`, `Referrer-Policy`, `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, `Permissions-Policy`, the enforced CSP (`frame-ancestors`, `base-uri`, `form-action`, `object-src` only; rule 428) and since 2026-10-12 a full `Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only` (rule 1341: `default-src 'self'`, Turnstile allowed, inline scripts and styles allowed, images and media from https), which only logs to the browser console. `poweredByHeader: false` drops `X-Powered-By` (rule 1340). `public/.well-known/security.txt` names hello@thakeen.com and expires 2027-10-01. |
| Rewrites | `next.config.ts:16-23` | `/.well-known/oauth-authorization-server[/*]` to `/api/oauth/meta`; `/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource[/*]` to `/api/oauth/resource`. |
| Robots and sitemap | `src/app/robots.ts:5`, `src/app/sitemap.ts:13` | Live: robots disallows `/api/`, `/ar/app/`, `/en/app/` and names the sitemap. Every sitemap URL carries hreflang alternates, its Arabic and English twins and the Arabic one as `x-default` (rule 1876); the two home pages set their own canonical and the same three alternates (rule 1859). On staging (`isStaging()`) robots disallows `/` for every agent with no sitemap line and the sitemap is empty (rule 677, `tests/staging.test.ts`); both are built statically, so `APP_ENV=staging` must be set before the staging build. |
| Install to home screen (PWA, 2026-10-08, rules 681-684) | `src/app/manifest.ts`, `src/app/apple-icon.png`, `public/icons/`, `src/lib/pwa.ts`, `src/app/[lang]/layout.tsx` (`appleWebApp`), `src/components/app/InstallHint.tsx` | Static manifest at `/manifest.webmanifest`, linked from every page: «ذكين \| Thakeen», Arabic and rtl, standalone, `start_url` and `id` `/app`, scope `/`, theme paper and background ground, icons 192, 512 and maskable 512, plus the 180 apple-touch-icon and the apple-web-app tags. The icons are a placeholder drawn «ذ» (`scripts/make-pwa-icons.mjs`) until the founder supplies a logo. No service worker, so nothing is cached on the device; push is phase 2 (spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-08-pwa-install-design.md`). |
| llms.txt (2026-10-08, rule 698) | `src/app/llms.txt/route.ts` | A plain-text index of the public site for language models: one H1, the summary (Arabic first), then the footer's columns as `## ` sections of `- [name](url): note` links. Every word is existing site copy and every link a path the sitemap lists on `appOrigin()`, so nothing under `/app`, `/api`, the founder console, OAuth or the partner dashboard. `force-static`: prerendered at build, no handler runs per request, so it reads no request, session or Redis and carries no rate limit. Robots does not disallow it; the proxy, which sees every path since the staging lock, passes it on untouched off staging and locks it on staging like every page. |
| Share cards (2026-10-01, #45) | `src/app/[lang]/layout.tsx` (`generateMetadata`), `src/app/[lang]/opengraph-image.png` (+ `.alt.txt`), `src/lib/page-meta.ts` | `metadataBase` from `appOrigin()`; `openGraph` (site name, type, locale) and `twitter` (`summary_large_image`) with no title or description, so Next fills og:title, og:description and the twitter card from each page's own title and description; one static 1200×630 share image (`/{lang}/opengraph-image.png`, bilingual "ذكين \| Thakeen" card), drawn by `scripts/make-share-image.mjs` (2026-10-08: lemon card, the long-kaf wordmark set like `Wordmark.tsx` in Thmanyah with `salt` on the kaf, the slogan from `SLOGAN.line1`; the font stays in `src/app/fonts`, only the pixels reach the PNG) inherited by every page under `[lang]`. Since 2026-10-01 (founder decision) the card and the layout's default title and description (`SITE_DEFAULT` in `page-meta.ts`, what the homepage shows and shares) carry the public name and the founder's slogan, read from the homepage h1's `SLOGAN.line1` in `src/lib/home.ts` (never a second literal): the title is «ذكين. الذكي المكين.» / "Thakeen. Smart. Steadfast.", the description the slogan followed by «وكيل تسويق مبني للخليج.» / "A marketing agent built for the Gulf." (masculine in Arabic, to agree with ذكين; the footer's feminine tagline is Noor's and stays in the footer), and the image's two lines are the Arabic and English slogan. No agent's tagline and no model name. Public pages build their metadata with `pageMeta(lang, path, {title, description, bilingual?})`: title, description, canonical and hreflang only. A page must not set `openGraph` or `twitter`: Next merges metadata shallowly, so it would replace the layout's object and drop the image (`tests/share-meta.test.ts`). |
| Cron schedule | `crons.json` (read by no host; the hPanel cron jobs match it; `vercel.json` until 2026-10-10) | See 2.6. |

### 2.2 Marketing site (public, `src/app/[lang]/*`)

Root layout `src/app/[lang]/layout.tsx` (static params for `ar`, `en`). Pages: home `page.tsx`, `agents`, `agents/[agent]`, `ai-marketing-agent`, `affiliates`, `affiliates/terms` (the partner programme terms, 2026-10-01), `blog`, `blog/[slug]`, `customers`, `enterprise`, `for/[industry]`, `help`, `help/[topic]` (one page per help topic with its answers' `#slug` anchors and FAQPage JSON-LD, 2026-10-18, rules 2140-2145), `pricing`, `privacy`, `terms`, `resources`, `tools`, `updates`, `compare` (comparison by kind; the old `vs/[competitor]` addresses answer 308 to it from `next.config.ts`, rule 1742). Content comes from static modules (`src/lib/i18n.ts`, `site.ts`, `changelog.ts`, `pages-compare.ts`, `pages-industry.ts`, `legal.ts`, `help.ts`, `blog.ts` reading `content/`). Public forms post to `/api/support` (`src/components/ContactForm.tsx:15`, `HelpCenter.tsx:17`), `/api/affiliates` (`AffiliateForm.tsx:18`) and `/api/tools/{tool}` (`ToolForm.tsx:16`). No component posts to `/api/waitlist` any more (only the route itself references it). Outside the language tree, `/llms.txt` (2026-10-08) indexes these pages for language models (2.1).

The homepage (`page.tsx`) is prototype D (rule 516): `Cover` (the masthead, the `Lineup` of the team, then the signup form, trial line and freshness chip under the team, rules 1830 and 1892), `Rules`, `Day`, `Gallery`, `Steps`, `Plans` and `HomeFaq` from `src/components/home/`, with `Decor`'s seams between them; the site nav shows `HOME_NAV`, one anchor per section. Copy — the slogan, the eight-agent cast plus the owner, their questions, the rules, the day strip, the seven steps, the questions — comes from `src/lib/home.ts`; every price on `Plans` comes from the dictionary's pricing block (`src/lib/pricing.ts`). The doodles and seams are CSS only: they move under `prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference` and stop while `html[data-motion="off"]`, which the page's pause button in the lineup sets through the module-level store in `src/components/home/motion.ts` (WCAG 2.2.2), so the page stays a server component. The lineup's figures take turns showing their thought on their own (rules 1832-1835): silently, one at a time, never under reduced motion, while paused, off screen, in a hidden tab or while a visitor holds a figure; `motion.ts` also holds the one reduced-motion hook the lineup and the scenes' slider share. There are no server calls from the homepage components; every call to action is a link to `/{lang}/app/signup` (the agency plan to `/{lang}/enterprise#contact`). `Gallery` (UI/UX phase 5b, rules 1220-1227, `docs/system/tests/1220-uiux-phase5b.md`) is the live UI gallery: a `Tabs` row over three panels (chat, starter plan, inbox) drawn by the app's own `ChatBubble`, `ApprovalCard` and `PlanCard` with made-up data for an unnamed roastery, each panel labelled «مثال توضيحي» / "Illustrative example". Its copy and samples live in `src/lib/home-gallery.ts`, not `home.ts`. The components run with `demo`, so Approve, Undo and Add change only the card and send nothing; the server passes the client only the approval card's slice of the app dictionary (`approvalDict` in `src/lib/approval-dict.ts`). It is not in `HOME_NAV`. The homepage's dictionary game component (`Dictionary.tsx`) and its start form (`StartForm.tsx`) were deleted on 2026-10-12 (nothing imported either; the app's first-login game is `DictionaryStart.tsx`). `src/components/Landing.tsx` and `Sections.tsx`, an earlier homepage, and `Story`, `Truths` and `QuestionFlip`, the previous one, were deleted.

### 2.3 App shell (`src/app/[lang]/app/*`)

`layout.tsx` resolves the session (`currentUser()`), redirects anonymous visitors to login unless the path ends with one of `/login /signup /forgot /reset /admin /join /verify` (an owner who has not verified the address reads as signed out; the layout then redirects them to `/{lang}/app/verify` instead, rule 754), renders the sidebar (the acting person's name, email and role; Members for everyone; Referrals and MCP by role; Partner (since 2026-10-01) for the owner of an account with `User.partnerCode`; the console link by `isFounderActor`), trial banner and limit dialog (paying and caps offered to the owner only), and an impersonation banner when the `thakeen_impersonate` cookie exists. Since 2026-10-08 the signed-in branch also renders the install hint (`src/components/app/InstallHint.tsx`, rule 684) under the trial banner; signed-out pages (login, signup) never show it. Pages: inbox (`page.tsx`), `chat`, `todo` (My to-dos, Reem's list, since 2026-09-29), `conversations`, `generate`, `calendar`, `files`, `tasks` (schedule), `governance`, `activity` (the per-agent activity log, first page rendered on the server), `integrations`, `mcp`, `billing` (since 2026-10-01 with the chat card, `ChatAllowanceCard`: messages used of the allowance, when they renew, how long they last at this pace, and the owner's "continue on credits" switch), `refer`, `partner` (the partner page, 2026-10-01), `reports`, `notifications`, `profile`, `brands`, `inspo`, `help`, `start`, `onboarding`, `team` (Members, 2026-09-29), `join` (public: accept an invite, 2026-09-29), `login`, `signup`, `forgot`, `reset`, `verify` (the 6-digit email code and the change-address form, 2026-10-08), `admin`. Owner-only and admin+ pages render read-only for lower roles from `can()` (`permissions.md` section 2). The OAuth consent screen lives outside the language tree at `src/app/oauth/authorize/page.tsx`. Since 2026-10-08 the Team, Files, Governance and Wishlist panels ask in the app's own confirmation dialog (`src/components/app/ConfirmDialog.tsx`: `useConfirm`, a native modal `<dialog>`; rules 695, 697) instead of the browser's `confirm()`, and the password inputs of sign-up, login, reset, join and the profile's change-password form are `PasswordField`s (`src/components/app/PasswordField.tsx`) with a show toggle that never submits (rule 696). Account deletion on the Profile page asks in `DeleteAccountDialog` (the account's email typed and the password, rule 964). Since 2026-10-11 (UI/UX phase 1, rules 1080-1098) the shared primitives live in `src/components/ui/` (`Button` and the pure `buttonClass`, `Field`/`Input`/`Textarea`/`Select`, `Switch`, `Badge`, `Callout`, `Toast`, `Dialog`/`FormDialog`/`usePrompt`, `Time`/`Money` over `src/lib/format.ts`; `docs/Design_System.md`), and this layout wraps both branches in `ToastProvider` so any panel under it can call `useToast()`; the sign-in, sign-up, forgot and reset forms, the brand form, brands list, account deletion and integrations are built on them, and `eslint-rules/thakeen-ui.mjs` warns on the patterns they replace. Since 2026-10-08 (plan Task 7 of the model routing spec) the chat header carries the model picker (`ChatModelPicker`) and the brand page, under the form, the "Default model for content" field (`BrandModelField`), both in `src/components/app/ModelPicker.tsx`: they read `GET /api/models` and write `PUT /api/models` (`flows.md` section 8, step 15.7).

Since 2026-10-11 (UI/UX phase 3, rules 1140-1158, branch `claude/uiux-phase3`) the account pages live in one settings area, `src/app/[lang]/app/settings/` (`layout.tsx`: `SettingsNav` as a start-side column on a wide screen; on a phone the index lists the sections and each section page starts with a link back). Sections, from `SETTINGS_SECTIONS` in `src/lib/settings-nav.ts`: `account` (name, language, the owner's phone), `security` (password change with the sign-up rules live; sign out other devices), `notifications` (preferences per type and channel; the feed stays at `/app/notifications`), `brand` (the brand form plus the social handles, value proposition and sitemap the old profile page held), `brands`, `members` (was `team`), `billing`, `connections` (was `integrations`), `governance`, `danger` (log out, delete the account). The old pages `profile`, `team`, `brands`, `billing`, `onboarding`, `integrations` and `governance` are gone; `src/proxy.ts` answers each old path with a 307 to its section, query string kept (`legacySettingsPath`), for one release. Each settings card has its own Save (`SettingsCard`, `saveEnabled`). The billing page sums up plan, credits, seats and the next date, shows `PlanCard`s (current plan in words, prices in ink, the VAT note), the owner's payment history from the payments already recorded, and on a Paymob return polls `GET /api/billing/status` (`PaymobReturnStatus`). `ProfilePanel.tsx` is gone; its credit card is `src/components/app/settings/CreditUsageCard.tsx`, the month's usage `src/lib/month-usage.ts`.

### 2.3a Founder console (`src/app/[lang]/admin/*`, 2026-10-09)

Its own route tree and layout (rules 870-872): `layout.tsx` renders `AdminNav` (`src/components/admin/AdminNav.tsx`: the twelve sections (Integrations added 2026-10-13, rule 1540, branch only; Mail preview added 2026-10-16, rule 1964, branch only), the new-leads badge, «العودة للتطبيق» for a founder signed in as themselves, "end key session" for a key session, a menu button under `lg`) only behind `isAdminCookie()`; every page calls `requireAdmin()` (`src/lib/admin-gate-server.ts`, a plain 404 otherwise, except a well-formed key-session cookie whose session ended: the page then renders the ended notice with its link to `?login=1`). The layout's metadata is `noindex, nofollow`, and the wide tables scroll inside an `overflow-x-auto` box at phone width. Sections and links come from `src/lib/admin-gate.ts` (`ADMIN_SECTIONS`, `adminHref`, `ADMIN_LEADS_SEEN`). `/{lang}/admin?login=1` shows the key form without the shell. The old `/{lang}/app/admin` redirects here with its query; a key session that ended links to `/{lang}/admin?login=1` (`adminEndedHref`) and the impersonation stop returns to `/{lang}/admin/users`. Each page renders one section component from `src/components/admin` (rule 874: `Overview`, `Users`, `Usage`, `Billing`, `Quality` with `GoldenPanel` and then `AgentScopePanel` (rules 1412-1413) under it, `Log`; leads, partners, wishlist and skills render their panel inside `EndedGate`), and each fetches only what it shows; `shared.tsx` holds the copy, the API shapes and the hooks they share.

Second factor (2026-10-12, rules 1370-1384): the layout also renders `SecondFactor` (`src/components/admin/SecondFactor.tsx`) above every section: a banner with "set up" while `secondFactorStatus()` (`src/lib/admin-2fa.ts`) finds no enrolment, a status line with "replace" once there is one, and the code prompt that `adminFetch` opens when a write answers 403 `second_factor` (the write is sent again after a right code). The codes come from `src/lib/totp.ts` (RFC 6238 in `crypto`, no dependency). The setup dialog also draws the `otpauth://` URI as a QR code (2026-10-13, rules 1620-1624): `src/components/admin/QrCode.tsx` renders the SVG elements from `qrPath` in `src/lib/qr.ts`, which encodes the URI in the page with the `uqr` package (0.1.3, no dependencies of its own; the console's only QR dependency); nothing is fetched. `jsqr` (dev only) decodes it back in `tests/admin-2fa-qr.test.ts`.

Staff identity (2026-10-13, rules 1630-1654, branch `claude/staff-identity`, phase 1 = expand; `docs/system/flows.md` section 71): the console has its own login, `staff:{email}`, never a customer account. Pages `login` (`StaffSignIn`: password, then the code, a recovery code or the enrolment with `QrCode`), `login/setup` (setup or reset code typed into the form), `staff` (`StaffAdmin`) and `account` (`StaffAccount`), every word in `src/lib/staff-words.ts`. The layout reads `consoleIdentity()` (staff session first, then the key session, then the founder customer session while that path is open) and renders `ConsoleBanners` (break-glass key session; the expand-only founder path; the first staff login from a key session while none exists). `requireAdmin(lang, here)` now redirects a visitor with none of them to `/{lang}/admin/login?next=` (rule 1652). `src/proxy.ts` sends `x-robots-tag: noindex, nofollow` and `Cache-Control: no-store` on `/{lang}/admin*` and `/api/admin/staff*` (rule 1653). For a staff session `SecondFactor` shows only the code prompt: the code is the person's own.

Agent instructions page (`src/app/[lang]/app/instructions/page.tsx`, 2026-10-10, rule 957; agent skills portal task 10): a server page for every signed-in role (no route change, no new permission). It reads only the caller's workspace `sc(u)` (`getLayer`, `listHistory`, `listFileNames`, `refRecords`, `getBrand`, the plan's `entitlements(u).instructions`) and never the platform layer, and sends `InstructionsPanel` the live set and draft through `publicLayer`, the newest 20 versions as counts only (`summarizeRevision`), `skipped = skippedCount(active, limits)`, the edit base (`editBase`) and, for admin and owner, the price of each test (`testOptionsFor`, from `testQuote`, the function `POST /api/instructions/test` charges by). The panel calls the instructions routes only when someone acts. `InstructionEditor` is the shared row editor (the founder's console reuses it). The pure helpers the browser shares with the server are `instructions-diff.ts` and `instructions-ui.ts`. The sidebar shows the row to every role.

Founder skills console (`src/app/[lang]/admin/skills/page.tsx`, `src/components/admin/SkillsAdminPanel.tsx`, `src/app/api/admin/skills/route.ts`, `src/lib/skills-admin.ts`, `src/lib/sample-brand.ts`, 2026-10-10, rules 958-959; agent skills portal task 11): the tenth console section (`ADMIN_SECTIONS` += `skills`). One route with three methods, each calling `adminActor(req)` once, first (401): GET answers the console state (`consoleState`: the `platform` layer with its edit base, the newest 20 versions as counts, the feed of workspace changes, the pause switch; the page builds its props with the same function) plus `?compare=<revisionId>` (matched against the platform's own history) and `?workspace=<scope>` (read-only live set of a workspace, only when the scope string is one the server's own feed holds, else 404; no draft, no `by.imp`); PUT saves the platform draft (the eight agents, `media`, `reviewer`, `judge`; the `lock` flag; 2000 characters; `base` required, 400 `bad_base` without it, 409 `stale` on a mismatch); POST takes `activate`, `discard`, `restore`, `pause` and `test`. The layer written is the constant `PLATFORM`; nothing in the key comes from the request. Pause (`setPaused`) blanks every workspace block and leaves the platform block (`instructionBlocks`). The test is the customer's two-generation flow on the fictional `SAMPLE_BRAND` with the platform layer only (live set against draft): it imports no `charge`, is metered under the fixed label `skills-test@nashr.internal` and limited to `ADMIN_SKILL_TESTS_PER_HOUR` (30) on one bucket, a server constant. Every founder write pushes `admin:log` with `what: skills.<action>` and small before and after values (versions and counts, never an instruction's text).

UI/UX phase 3 (2026-10-11, branch `claude/uiux-phase3`) adds 3 files and 3 handlers: `account/sessions` (POST, sign out the login's other devices, rule 1150), `notifications/prefs` (PUT, notification preferences, rules 1144-1148) and `billing/status` (GET, the Paymob return poll, rule 1155); `permissions.md` rows 161-163.

Per-route authorization is in `permissions.md`.

#### API route wrapper (2026-10-16, branch only, rules 1900-1906, `flows.md` section 77)

`src/lib/api-handler.ts` exports `handler(options, fn)`, the one place a route's entry checks run, in a fixed order: (1) authenticate (`auth: "user"` is `currentUser()`, `"admin"` is `isAdmin(req)`, `"cron"` is the `CRON_SECRET` bearer compared with `sameSecret`, `"public"` is none) and answer 401 before anything of the request is read; (2) `forbid(u, role)` with an action of `src/lib/roles.ts`; (3) the rate limit (`allow`, bucket `rate:<bucket>:<email | sc(u) | client IP>`, 429 `slow_down` with `Retry-After`); (4) `maxBytes` from Content-Length (413 `too_large`); (5) the query string, then the JSON body, each checked by a zod schema (400 with the first failing field's own code, else `invalid`, else the bare `{ ok: false }`). `fn` receives `{ req, u, scope, input, query, params }`; `scope` is `sc(u)` computed from the session, never from the request. Error codes are the `ApiError` union in the same file, the strings the routes already sent, so the client maps in `src/lib/app-i18n.ts` keep matching. Nothing is caught: a throw is the same 500. `zod` (4.x) is a direct dependency since this change. The static checker in `tests/role-gates.test.ts` reads a wrapped handler's `auth`, `role` and `rate` options as its gates.

Converted so far (the pilot): `files` (GET, PUT, DELETE), `brand` (GET, PUT), `brand/proposals/[id]` (POST), `tasks` (GET), `activity` (GET), `activity/export` (GET), `conversations` (GET, POST, PATCH, DELETE), `wishlist/vote` (POST), `wishlist/suggest` (POST). Status codes and bodies are unchanged (rule 1905); the only difference is that a malformed body that used to reach the handler (a non-string file name, a `null` or array body, a non-string conversation id or title) is now a 400. The other routes still run the same steps by hand and move over in later batches.

### 2.5 Library modules (`src/lib`)

| Module | One line |
|---|---|
| `src/instrumentation.ts` (outside `src/lib`) | Next's `onRequestError` (2026-10-16, rule 1976, branch only): one `log.error("request failed", …)` per server error with the request id, method, path without its query, route file, route kind and React digest; never the message, stack, headers or body. Nothing is sent to a third party |
| `src/instrumentation-client.ts` (outside `src/lib`) | Next 16 client hook `onRouterTransitionStart` (2026-10-17, rule 2005, branch only): a navigation to another path or query on this origin sends the window event `thakeen:route-start` for the top progress bar; nothing else runs before hydration |
| `route-progress.ts` | The progress bar's pure state (rule 2005): `startsProgress(target, here)` and `progressStore` (idle, waiting 150 ms, running, ending 250 ms, self-finish after 10 s), driven by `src/components/ui/RouteProgress.tsx` |
| `url-query.ts` | A list's query in the address (rule 2007): `queryHref`, `historyFor` (push for a choice, replace for typed text) and `canonicalHref` over a validating codec; used by `useUrlQuery` in `src/components/admin/DataTable.tsx` |
| `keys.ts` | The Redis key registry (2026-10-17, rules 2030-2034, branch only): `wsKey` (workspace keys, each takes a branded `Scope` that only `sc(u)`, `workspaceScope` and `storedScope` produce), `accountKey` (login and account keys, by normalized email) and `globalKey` (tokens, ids, indexes). Every builder returns the bytes of the template it replaced. `scopeParam` is the phase-1 boundary for lib helpers whose scope parameter is still `string`; `tests/key-registry-ratchet.test.ts` counts raw key templates and these calls per file, and the counts only go down |
| `stored.ts` | Read validation for stored shapes (2026-10-17, rules 2035-2039, branch only): `parseStored(shape, schema, raw, fallback)` logs `stored shape invalid` with field paths and zod codes only, and returns the fallback; schemas for `brands:{email}` rows, `activebrand:{login}`, `conn:{scope}:{provider}` and `conv:{scope}:{id}`, each `looseObject` and accepting every older shape |
| `empty-copy.ts` | Shared empty-state words per panel, Arabic and English (rule 2002), for `src/components/ui/EmptyState.tsx` |
| `db.ts` | Storage adapter: Upstash REST client (`upstash`, `:73`) or in-memory fallback (`memory`, `:189`), chosen by `pickStore`; a production deployment on any host (`isProductionDeploy` in `deploy-env.ts`, since 2026-10-08; Vercel-only before) without Redis credentials gets a store that rejects every call with `StoreNotConfigured` instead of the memory store (2026-10-01, rule 430), one `Store` type (`:8`): `get/set(ttl)/ttl/del/lpush/lrange/ltrim/lrem/hset/hgetall/hdel/sadd/smembers/sismember/srem/scardMany/mget/zadd/zrem/zcard/zrangeWithScores/zrangeByScore/incrby/expireNx/lock/setNx/setXx/unlock/lockOwned/unlockOwned/getdel/rename/replaceIf` (`scardMany`: every set size in one pipelined request; `mget`: many JSON values in one MGET; the sorted-set commands since 2026-09-29, for Reem's lists, with the memory store ordering by score then member as Redis does; since 2026-09-30 `zadd` takes `{lt: true}` (ZADD LT: adds a new member, moves an existing one only lower) and `zrangeByScore(key, max, limit)` reads the members scored at most `max`, lowest first, on both stores; the ZREM claim `zremClaim` of the first phase-2 commit is gone: the reminder sweep leases with `setNx` instead), plus `pushCapped` and `CAPS`. `sismember` (2026-09-29, teams) is one membership test, the roster check `onRoster` in `team.ts`. `expireNx` (`:42`) is EXPIRE with the NX option on Upstash (`:157-159`): it gives a key a TTL only when it has none and never touches the value. `lock` is SET NX with a TTL; `unlock` is an unconditional DEL (`:169`). `setNx` (since 2026-09-29) is SET key value NX EX in one command: the notification markers are claimed with it, so of two overlapping writers exactly one wins; the memory store checks and writes with no await between. `setXx` (2026-09-30, Majed's handoffs) is SET key value XX EX in one command: it overwrites a value only while the key exists, so a writer that read a handoff record before it was deleted (pushed off its capped index) cannot bring it back (`save` in `handoffs.ts`); the memory store checks and writes with no await between too. Since 2026-10-05 three more single-command primitives serve the token rules: `getdel` is GETDEL (read and delete in one command, so of two overlapping callers exactly one gets the value: an authorization code or a refresh record, rules 548-549), `rename` is RENAME (keeps the value and its TTL; false when the source is gone, used to move a pre-hashing MCP token to its hashed key, rule 553) and `replaceIf` is a Lua compare-and-set that writes only while the key still holds the expected string and keeps its TTL (moves `mcptoken-of`); the memory store does the same with no await between. Since 2026-09-30 `lockOwned` is SET NX with a TTL and a random owner token, and `unlockOwned` deletes the key only while it still holds that token (an EVAL compare-and-delete on Upstash), for a lock that a finished run must never free for the next one (`onboardlock:`). The memory fallback honours TTL (`live`, `:198`, since 2026-09-18) and copies values on read and write, as Upstash does. `ttl` (2026-10-01) answers as Redis TTL: seconds left, -1 for no expiry, -2 for a missing key; since then the memory `lock` and `lockOwned` record their expiry too, so `ttl` answers for a lock as it does on Upstash (`writtenBeforeRepairCutoff` in `notify.ts`). `mgetChunked(keys, size = 100)` (2026-10-12, rule 1430): one MGET per 100 keys, four in flight, in key order; `listTasks`, `rebuildIndex` and `listConversations` read their records with it. No `await db.*` sits in a loop outside the reviewed lock retries and `runCronBatch` (`tests/redis-loops.test.ts`, rule 1435). |
| `auth.ts` | `User` type (plus `fairUseLift` (since 2026-10-13, rule 1731, branch only: the founder lifted the fair-use pause for that month, `src/lib/fair-use.ts`), `seats`, `brandCap` (since 2026-10-12, rule 1393: the founder's number of active brands, 1-200, read only through `brandCapFor` in `pricing.ts`; written only by `POST /api/admin/brand-cap`), `teamSessionsAfter`, the partner programme's `referredBy` (partner, code, `at` = link click or signup, `via`, random `ref`), `firstMonthOffer`, `firstPaidOn` and `partnerCode` since 2026-10-01, and the runtime-only `actor`; since 2026-10-01 `chatAllowance`, the founder's override of the plan's chat messages, `chatOverflow`, the owner's "continue on credits" choice, and `chatCarryMicro`, the metered cost of over-allowance messages not yet billed), `Role`, `Actor`, bcrypt password hashing, session cookie `thakeen_session` and `Session` (`{email, at, imp?}`), `currentUser()` (resolves owner, member and impersonation sessions to the owner's account with `actor` set; a member must also be on the owner's roster, `members:{owner}`), `getLogin` (owner or member login for an email; a member record off its owner's roster is no login), scope helper `sc(u)`, re-exports of the plan table (`Plan`, `AgencyDeal`, `PLANS`, `isPlan`, `PLAN_CREDITS`, `PLAN_SEATS`, `seatLimit` from `pricing.ts`, since 2026-09-30; `User.deal` holds an Agency contract, and `getUser` maps the stored plan on read with `planFromStore`, so a retired `enterprise` reads as `agency` and anything unknown as `trial`), revocation stamps (`revokeSessions`, `revokeTeamSessions`), founder checks by actor (`isFounderActor`, `adminActor`, `isAdmin`, `isAdminCookie`). |

| `partners.ts` | The partner programme's store side (2026-10-01): approvals under `lock:partners`, attribution at signup (`signupPartner`, `afterSignup`) and from the billing page (`claimPartnerCode`), the one first-month offer (`openOffer` with the early-partner offer's end, `offerLastDay`, `earlyOfferDue`, `lastDayOfferOwed` within that end, `grantEarlyOffer`), recorded payments and voids under the customer's `withUserLock`, every commission of the customer re-taken by `settle` with the ledger under `lock:partner:{email}` (`recordPayment`, `voidPayment`), payouts with an idempotent id, the VAT paid on top and withholding borne recorded beside them, voiding and the founder's bank confirmation (`recordPayout`, `voidPayout`, `confirmBank`), applications with their terms consent (`listApplications`), the partner's page data without customer PII (`partnerDashboard`, `customerLabel`), payout details (`saveBank`: holder, any country's IBAN, country, optional tax number) and `forgetPartnerData` for `wipeAccount`.
