Since 2026-10-01 (pricing phase 2) the chat: `CHAT_ALLOWANCE` (trial 30 for the whole trial; starter 250, pro 650, business 1,300 a month), `MAX_CHAT_OVERRIDE` (100,000) and `isChatOverride`, `chatAllowanceOf(u)` (a valid founder override, 0 included, else Agency → Business's, else the plan's, else 0), `CHAT_TURN_MAX_USD` (0.14 since chat moved to Sonnet 5.5 on 2026-10-02, 0.35 on Opus before; the most one message may cost in model calls) and `chatOverflowHold()` (that ceiling at the floor rate, rounded up: 5 credits). |
| `entitlements.ts` | `entitlements(u, now?)` (2026-10-08, rule 780, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-08-entitlements-agent-scope-design.md` 3.1): one pure function for what an account's plan includes (`active`, `inactive`, `endsAt`, monthly credits, seats, chat allowance and hourly and daily caps, the video models, the agents). It asks `allowanceOf`, `seatLimit`, `chatAllowanceOf` and `chatCaps` rather than copying them, and reads the founder's dated video grant (`User.grants.video`, `grantActive`); `parseVideoGrant` is the one check of a grant on its way in (`POST /api/admin/plan` `videoGrant`, rule 783) and `isUtcDay` the one check that a grant's `until` is a day that exists. `entitlementView(u, now?)` (rule 784) adds what the founder's console shows for one account: `base` (the plan's own values, `entitlements()` of the record without `deal`, `seats`, `chatAllowance` and `grants`), the seats in use and the chat status (one `Promise.all`, read-only), the credit balance, `resetsAt` (the next 1st of the month, a UTC day, for a paid plan that is not billing-locked, null for a trial and a locked subscription) and the video grant with an `expired` flag. A server module (`rate.ts`, `team.ts` and `chat-allowance.ts` import the db client), so the inbox's client components get the video list as a prop and the console and its table `EntitlementsTable` import only its types. Read by `renderVideo` and the inbox page (rule 781) and by the chat route and MCP `ask_noor` (rule 782), and by the billing page, which reads `entitlementView(u)` once and shows the owner what the plan includes in `PlanIncludesCard` (rule 785; it reads `ent` and `used` and never `grants`). Outside it: whether a model is switched on (`media/config.ts`), the abuse rate limits and the owner's per-agent budgets. |
| `agent-scope.ts` | Agent scope, Part B core (2026-10-10, rules 790, 792, 793, 796; spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-08-entitlements-agent-scope-design.md` section 6): the module every layer uses; it holds no model call of its own (`scopeCheck` calls the classifier in `agent-scope-classify.ts`, rule 791; the verdict words are in the leaf file `agent-scope-verdicts.ts`, which both read, so neither imports the other). `AgentScopeModes` (`prompt` on/off; `input`, `role`, `output` off/shadow/enforce) with `AGENT_SCOPE_DEFAULT_MODES` (`on`, `shadow`, `shadow`, `shadow`), `parseAgentScopeModes(raw)` (a missing key, a non-object or a bad field gives that field its default) and `readAgentScopeModes()` (never throws: a store error gives the defaults); `AGENT_SCOPE_KEYS` (`modes`, `evalLast`, `log(day)`); `VERDICTS` / `isVerdict` / `Verdict` (`in`, `off_topic`, `pro_advice`, `other_business`, `personal`, `other_brand`; re-exported unchanged from `agent-scope-verdicts.ts`); `agentScopeRefusalText(verdict, lang, {brand, otherBrands, classified?})` (the spec 6.3 copy; an empty or blank brand name reads "your business" / «نشاطك», and `other_brand` writes the entry of the owner's own list that equals the classifier's word after trim and lowercase, as the list spells it, else "another of your brands" / «علامة أخرى من علاماتك»: the classifier's text is never shown), `agentScopeSlowText`, `agentScopeRetryText`; `logAgentScope(entry)` (one JSON line in `agent-scope:log:{YYYY-MM-DD}` for the UTC day, `text` and `error` cut to 200 characters, `pushCapped` to `CAPS.agentScopeLog` 5000, then `expireNx` 31 days; never throws, and prints only the error's name); `itemText` / `itemHash` (sha256 of the title, a blank line and the edited body or the body, so a check is tied to the exact text); `otherBrandNames(user)` (one `Promise.all` of `listBrands` and `getBrand(user.email)`, because the default brand's `BrandMeta.name` is ""; the active brand and empty names are left out); `AgentScopeRefused(verdict, reply)`; `scopeCheck(layer, mode, text, ctx, {failClosed?})` (rule 791, 2026-10-10), the one decision every gate asks for: `off` makes no call and no log; the classifier's `paused` error counts as passed with no log; any other error (`timeout`, `api`, `unknown_verdict`) is logged as `verdict: "error"` and blocks only with `failClosed` in `enforce` (a publish, with `agentScopeRetryText`); `in` passes unlogged; any other verdict is logged with the mode and, in `enforce`, blocks with `agentScopeRefusalText` (names from the owner's own brands, never the classifier's text), in `shadow` passes; it never throws, and passes the turn's `ctx.deadline` to the classifier; an enforced input refusal by verdict is counted toward the ceiling (`countAgentScopeRefusal`, rule 795, a failure prints only the error's name). `agentScopePreflight(u)` (rule 795) reads the modes and the ceiling in one round and returns `{modes, slow}`, `slow` "hour", "day" or null and never set outside input `enforce`; a ceiling that cannot be read is null. The chat route and MCP `ask_noor` call it, and `chat()` runs the input gate (rule 794, `flows.md` section 54 item 1). `checkGenerationNotes(user, type, gate, brand, deadline)` (rule 796, `flows.md` section 54 item 2) is the same gate on a generation batch's notes. `generateBatch` calls it (`BatchOpts.notesGate`, set by `POST /api/generate` and MCP `generate_content`) with its own brand read, after its free refusals and before the renewal and the charge: no classifier call, no count and no refusal when the layer is `off` or the notes are empty; a spent ceiling (`gate.slow`) is answered without a call; otherwise the ask is counted against the scope's bound (`agentScopeGenRefusal` in `rate.ts`, 60 an hour and 200 a day; over it enforce answers `slow` and shadow skips the call), and the notes, trimmed and cut at `GENERATE_NOTES_READ` (2000, the characters the generator and the reviewer read, one constant in the leaf file `notes-read.ts` that `noor.ts` and `json.ts` import and `agent-scope.ts` re-exports), go to `scopeCheck("input", …)` with the type's owning agent (`agentForTask`), the brand's name and sector and the other brands' names (a store error there reads `[]`). With it: `"agent_scope"` in `KINDS` (`usage.ts`; the classifier is metered to the global counters only), `MODEL_BUDGET.AGENT_SCOPE_MS` 3 s (`model.ts`) and `CAPS.agentScopeLog` (`db.ts`). Server module (it reads the store and, through `scopeCheck`, reaches the model SDK): a client file may import its types only. Pinned by `tests/agent-scope.test.ts` and `tests/agent-scope-classify.test.ts`. |
| `agent-scope-classify.ts` | The agent-scope classifier (2026-10-10, rule 791): `classify(input, {deadline?, meterAgent?})` never throws and returns `{ok: true, verdict, brand?}` or `{ok: false, error: paused\|timeout\|api\|unknown_verdict}`. One forced `verdict` tool call (`tool_choice`, so no thinking and no cache), `max_tokens` 100, one attempt: the client is `anthropic({timeoutMs: MODEL_BUDGET.AGENT_SCOPE_MS, maxRetries: 0})` (3 s) and the call ends by `until(deadline, {cap})`. `modelState() !== "on"` is `paused` and builds no client; under 500 ms of the caller's deadline left is `timeout` and builds no client; an abort or a timeout error is `timeout`; any other throw is `api`; an answer with no tool call or a verdict that is not one of the six words is `unknown_verdict`. The model is `agentScopeModel()` = `route("agentScope").model`, so `claude-haiku-5-5` today and no model id is named in the file. The owner's text goes in `<owner_text>` (an agent's in `<agent_text>`) with every `<` and `>` in it, the brand names and the sector turned into `‹` and `›`, so nothing inside can close the wrapper; the message also names the agent and its role, the brand and its sector, the owner's other brands and the language. The classifier's cost is metered with `meter(undefined, model, "agent_scope", usage, agent)`: the global counters only (`usage:_all:{month}:kind:agent_scope`, the agent, model and provider families), never an account's. A failed call leaves one fixed `console.warn` line, `agent_scope: classify failed, <class name>` plus `, status <n>` when the error has a numeric status (the day log keeps only the category `timeout` or `api`, so this line is what tells a 400 on every call from a 529 now and then); it never prints the error's message, which can quote the request. It imports the verdict words from `agent-scope-verdicts.ts`, never from `agent-scope.ts` (which imports it). Server only (`SERVER_ONLY` in `tests/client-bundle.test.ts`). Pinned by `tests/agent-scope-classify.test.ts`. |
| `agent-scope-verdicts.ts` | The verdict words of the agent-scope classifier (2026-10-10, rule 791): `VERDICTS` (`in`, `off_topic`, `pro_advice`, `other_business`, `personal`, `other_brand`), `Verdict` and `isVerdict`. A leaf: it imports nothing, so `agent-scope.ts` (which re-exports the three unchanged) and `agent-scope-classify.ts` (which imports them from here) never import each other's module back, and a test that mocks either with `vi.mock(..., async (orig) => ...)` cannot hang on the other. In `SERVER_ONLY` of `tests/client-bundle.test.ts` with the other two (it is pure, so that list can drop it the day a client file needs the words). Pinned by `tests/agent-scope-classify.test.ts`. |
| `agent-scope-eval.ts` | The agent-scope test set and its score (2026-10-12, rule 1412): `AGENT_SCOPE_SET` (the hand-written `agent-scope-set.json` next to it: invented brands, Arabic and English, every agent, owner requests and agent texts), `scoreAgentScope(cases, got)` (pure: recall of refusal-labelled cases, wrong refusals of `in` cases, `passed` at 0.95 and 0.02, an unanswered case counts as not refused) and `runAgentScopeEval()` (the paid classifier once per case, ten at a time, global meter only). Server only; the founder's eval route is its only caller. |
| `notes-read.ts` | `GENERATE_NOTES_READ` = 2000 (2026-10-10, rule 796): how many characters of the owner's notes a generation batch reads. A leaf with no imports; the generator (`noor.ts`), the reviewer's grounding (`json.ts`) and the input gate (`checkGenerationNotes`, re-exported by `agent-scope.ts`) all use it, so the three cuts cannot drift. |
| `scope-refusal.ts` | `scopeRefusalText(j)` (2026-10-10, rule 796): the message of an `agent_scope` (422) or `slow_down` (429) answer of `POST /api/generate`, or null for any other answer. **Client-safe** (no imports): `GeneratePanel` and `ReportsPanel` (the report buttons and the cart-recovery draft) show it as text. |
| `task-models.ts` | Rule 901 (2026-10-09): `TASK_MODELS`, the writer and the reviewer of each task type (exhaustive over `TaskType`), and the three model ids `OPUS`, `SONNET`, `HAIKU`. Imports only a type. Read by `noor.ts` (`generate`, `critique`) and `task-costs.ts` (`estimateBatch`), so the floor test prices what is called; pinned by `tests/model-assignment.test.ts`. |
| `task-costs.ts` | Server and test only (in `SERVER_ONLY` of `tests/client-bundle.test.ts`), 2026-09-30: token assumptions per call (`ESTIMATE_MODELS` for the judge, onboarding and media prompts, `ITEM_SIZE`, `ITEM_OUT_TOKENS`, `ASSUME`) priced through `costUsd`; `estimateBatch(type, {items, credits, cache})` on a cache hit or cold, at the type's own writer and reviewer from `task-models.ts` (rule 901), `draftCredits`, `estimateDraft`, `onboardEstimateUsd`, `mediaPromptUsd`. Read by `tests/pricing-floor.test.ts` (rule 241, which also pins `draftCredits` to `countCost` in `generate-batch.ts`, the charge of the chat's `draft_items`) and, in the app, only by `handoffs.ts` (`autoUnderFloor`: Majed's auto-run starts only types whose cold-cache estimate keeps the margin floor, rule 296). |
| `brand.ts` | `Brand` file, `Task` records (`:47`), task costs and counts (re-exported from `tasks.ts`, `:40`), owner preference notes (`listPreferencesFor` gives one agent's prompts the notes that are not about another agent's work). `saveTask` (`:260`) writes a task with its pending-set, light-index and ad-code entries in one round; creation sites call it with `isNew`. Every change to an existing task goes through `updateTask` (`:342`): under `tasklock:{scope}:{id}` (SET NX, 10 s, 60 tries at 50 ms, then `task_lock_timeout`) it re-reads the task, applies the caller's `mutate` to a copy and saves, so a writer never saves a copy it read before slow work; `mutate` may return null to save nothing, and a task deleted meanwhile is not recreated. Callers: the approve and edit route, the publish route, the chat tool `publish_item`, the judge, the placeholder card and every task write of the media engine. `liteOf` (`:228`) counts an image only when it is not a placeholder card (`:240`). |
| `credits.ts` | Credit ledger under a per-user Redis lock: `charge`, `adjust`, `refund`, monthly `renewIfDue` (since 2026-09-30 it sets the balance to `allowanceOf(u)` from `pricing.ts`: the plan's credits, or an Agency deal's; a trial or an unrecognised stored plan is never renewed). A lock release that fails after the balance was saved is logged, not thrown (`:28`). Since 2026-09-29 a charge carries `{scope, agent, cap}`: it counts the credits against the agent that owns the work in that brand scope (`User.agentSpend`, same save as the balance), refuses with `agent_cap` over the owner's monthly cap before the balance is touched, and `refund` given the charge's result lowers the same counter in the same month only. `agentSpendFor(u, scope)` reads this month's counters. Since 2026-09-30 an added brand's onboarding charge goes through `chargeOnboard` (reuses a held charge, or refuses an ended trial and charges, with the hold `User.onboardPaid[scope]` in the same save), `refundOnboard` (gives the held credits back and drops the hold in one save; nothing held, nothing given), `settleOnboard` (drops it once the brand is saved) and `resolveOnboardHolds` (the daily sweep; since 2026-10-01 `resolveOnceHolds` does the same for the Sunday plan's holds, rule 337, `ONBOARD_HOLD_MS` = one day; it returns each refund as the ledger made it, scope, agent and credits, for the sweep's activity row). Since 2026-10-01 `chargeOnce(email, cost, meta, claim)` charges at most once per claim (the Sunday plan's `weekly:{scope}:{week}`): the hold `User.paidOnce[claim]` is written in the same save as the debit, a run that finds it reuses it, holds older than `PAID_ONCE_MS` (8 days) are dropped by the next `chargeOnce`, and `refundOnce` gives a hold back and drops it in one save, once (rule 333). `renewalDue(u)` is `renewIfDue`'s test, which a copy read before the lock may use to skip the lock (rule 332). Since 2026-10-01 `settleChatOverflow(email, maxCredits, usdMicro, creditMicro)` bills a chat message past the allowance after the reply, in one save under the user lock: nothing was taken before the turn (the gate only checked the balance is at least `chatOverflowHold()`), the turn's metered cost is added to `User.chatCarryMicro`, every whole credit of it at the floor rate is billed, at most `maxCredits` and at most what the balance still holds, and the carry keeps the remainder (always under one credit); cost past either bound is never billed. It does not go through `charge`: chat is no agent's work, so the per-agent caps never count it. A failed or zero-cost turn never reaches it. Since 2026-10-01 (rules 440-454) also the top-up side: `addTopup` and `voidTopup` (the founder console, through `addPaidTopup` and `voidPaidTopup` in `partners.ts`), `saveSettledTopups` (the daily sweep), every debit and refund through `src/lib/topups.ts` (plan credits first, then packs by expiry; a charge's result carries its receipt `from`, kept on an `onboardPaid` or `paidOnce` hold), and `renewIfDue` resets only the month's plan credits. |
| `chat.ts` | Agent chat loop (Anthropic streaming, up to 5 tool turns, `CHAT_MAX_CALLS`), tool definitions and `runTool`, chat history lists; its `generate_content` and `draft_items` go through `generateBatch`. Since 2026-10-01 (pricing phase 2) the turn's model is `opts.model ?? route("chat").model`, resolved once at the top of `chat()` (since 2026-10-08 the chat routes pass the model their `reserveChatTurn` gate carries, so the model called and the messages counted are one value; `CHAT_MODEL` is only the exported default, `DEFAULTS.chat`, `claude-sonnet-5-5`), the thread the model gets is `fitFirstCall(model, trimHistory(thread), …)` (`chat-budget.ts`), and every later call's `max_tokens` is `nextMaxTokens`: what is left of `CHAT_TURN_MAX_USD` after what the turn has cost and an upper estimate of the call's input; when that leaves too little for a useful answer the loop stops and the reply is `CAPPED_REPLY` in the owner's language, and when even the first call has no room, `LARGE_REPLY` (no model call, so the message is not counted). A call ended by `max_tokens` is never acted on: its text is kept with `CUT_NOTE` and its tool calls do not run. `chat()` returns a `ChatTurn` (`ChatMsg` plus `limit` and `usd`, what the turn's chat model calls cost, read only by the routes for the allowance, never saved and never sent to the browser). `chat()` (`:363`) takes the request's one deadline first (`:365`): `opts.deadline` or `deadlineFor(CHAT_MAX_DURATION_S)`, where `CHAT_MAX_DURATION_S` (`:323`, 300) is the `maxDuration` of `/api/chat` and `/api/mcp`; `runTool` passes it to `generate_image`, which starts no render with less than `CHAT_RENDER_MIN_MS` (`:325`, 60 s) left (`:822`), and to `generate_content` and `draft_items`, whose batch skips the cross-agent review with less than 60 s of it left. `system()` (`:229`) puts the current date and time in the brand's zone (`nowLine(Date.now(), zoneOf(brand), weekend)`, `:279`: `NOW (city, UTC offset)`; `zoneOf` falls back to Riyadh, so `NOW (Riyadh, UTC+3)` by default) at the top of the uncached block, so agents work relative dates out from the real calendar. `chat()` also works out who is talking once (`opts.by`, else `byOf(user)`) and hands `runTool` its turn (`ToolCtx`: the talking agent and `by`, which the batches, `update_brand`, `write_file`, `publish_item` and `generate_image` put on their activity rows; since main's security gates were merged in, also `via`, "mcp" for `ask_noor`, which refuses `publish_item`, core-memory `write_file` and `import_catalog`, and `proposed`, the turn's brand-change proposals). `update_brand` records `brand_changed` only when it changed the brand file (a plain field, or banned words added), and `publish_item` records `published` only after `publishOnce` sent the item. `runTool` refuses `update_brand`, `publish_item` and `import_catalog` to a login whose role may not (`TOOL_ACTION`), and `write_file` to a core memory file without `files.core.write`. Since 2026-09-30 (handoffs): `toolsFor(agent)` adds `PROPOSE_HANDOFF_TOOL` in Majed's turns only; `runTool`'s `propose_handoff` is refused in code to any other agent, a turn with no agent, a token or a turn over MCP (`ctx.via`), and a second proposal in one reply, and saves through `createHandoff` (the turn's `ToolCtx` now also carries `ownerMsg` and `conv`); `roleGate` (`roleOwner`, `roleRefusalText`, `src/lib/agent-scope.ts`, rule 798) refuses Majed's `generate_content` and `draft_items` for a type he does not own in every mode, and the other agents' per the founder's role layer (`off`, `shadow` logs `wrong_agent`, `enforce` refuses with a `switch:` card that becomes `ChatMsg.switchTo`), and `runTool` answers `Unknown tool.` first for a tool the talking agent was not offered (rule 799); Majed's chat block (`LEAD_TEXT`, not his persona) says he leads; the reply's `ChatMsg.handoff` names the proposal, and `deliver` (`:78`) saves that reply first and only then marks the proposal shown (`markShown`), so a turn that fails after the tool saved a proposal never makes it eligible for Majed's auto-run (`flows.md` 34, 35). The reply's item cards are built with `chatItemOf`, which carries `xflag` (the agents that flagged an item in the cross-agent review), so a flagged item's card sends the owner to the inbox instead of offering Approve (`flows.md` 33). Since 2026-10-03 (Reem phase 3 A1, rules 478, 485-486): `chat()` reads `sec:cal:{scope}` in its `Promise.all` and passes the brand's effective weekend to `system()`, whose NOW line names the week start, and `system()` reads the seasons on the brand's zone; `toolsFor("reem")` adds `CONTACT_AWAY_TOOL`, and `runTool`'s `contact_away` is refused in code to any other agent, a turn with no agent, MCP and a token, and past 5 cards a reply (`AWAY_CARDS_PER_REPLY`); it counts against `sec:write`, only proposes (`proposeAway`) and collects the card in `ToolCtx.awayProposed` for the reply's `awayProposals`; `history()` gives every brand card and away card its state in one round. |
| `chat-history.ts`, `chat-tools.ts`, `chat-prompt.ts`, `chat-reply.ts`, `chat-gates.ts`, `chat-run-tool.ts` | The split of `chat.ts` (2026-10-17, wave 2a, rules 2010-2015): `chat.ts` keeps `chat()` and re-exports the rest. `-history` (message types, history lists), `-tools` (tool definitions, leaf), `-prompt` (`system`), `-reply` (reply parts, statuses), `-gates` (role, plan and limit gates), `-run-tool` (`runTool`). No part imports `chat.ts` |
| `app-i18n.ts`, `app-i18n-shell.ts`, `-workspace.ts`, `-pages.ts`, `-todo.ts`, `-team.ts` | The in-app dictionary (split 2026-10-17, wave 2a): `app-i18n.ts` joins the five area parts into `appDict` (`Record<Lang, typeof ar>`) and `AppDict`; each part exports `arX` and `enX: typeof arX`, so a key missing in either language fails the type-check. 37 top-level keys, same order as before |
| `deploy-env.ts` | `isProductionDeploy()` (2026-10-08): the one host-neutral answer to "is this serving customers", read by `pickStore` (`db.ts`, rule 430) and `modelState` (`model.ts`, rule 431). Fails closed: `VERCEL_ENV=production` or `APP_ENV=production`, else any production build unless `APP_ENV` is `test`/`development`/`preview` or `VERCEL_ENV` is `preview`/`development`. Also `isStaging()` (rule 674): exactly `APP_ENV=staging`, read at call time; it does not change `isProductionDeploy()`, so staging keeps rules 430-431. |
| `staging-access.ts` | The staging lock's cookie and token (rules 674-675): `STAGING_COOKIE` (`thakeen_staging`), `STAGING_OPEN_PATHS` (the access page in both languages, `/api/staging-access` and `/robots.txt`, exact match), `accessToken(key)` (HMAC-SHA256 of the fixed label `staging-access-v1` keyed by `STAGING_ACCESS_KEY`, hex; null for a missing or empty key) and `hasAccess(cookie)` (`sameSecret` against the current key's token, read at call time; false when the key is missing or empty). The cookie holds the token, never the code. Imports only `node:crypto` and `secret.ts`, never `db`, `rate` or `auth`, so the proxy can use it without loading the store. |
| `model.ts` | The model switch (`CLAUDE_PAUSED`, `modelState()`: `on`, `paused` or `mock`, `ModelPaused`, `PAUSED_REPLY`) and, since 2026-10-01, the only model client factory `anthropic({timeoutMs, maxRetries})` (rule 335), `until(deadline)` (per-call `{signal, timeout}` that ends a call, stream body included, by an epoch-ms deadline; a plain unref'd timer) and `MODEL_BUDGET` (per-call budgets, all inside their routes' maxDuration, rule 336). Since 2026-10-09 (Task 8) `effectiveProvider()` names the provider calls really go to (`straitly` only with `LLM_PROVIDER=straitly` and `STRAITLY_API_KEY`, else `anthropic`; `anthropic()` builds its client from the same `straitlyKey()`) for the metering, and `withDirectFallback` also counts each request it re-sends to Anthropic in the global `usage:_all:{YYYY-MM}:provider:fallback` (`noteFallback`: fire and forget, guarded twice, so a store that fails never delays or changes the retry or what is logged; it imports `db` and never `usage.ts`, which imports this file). The gateway's base is `gatewayOrigin(STRAITLY_BASE_URL)` (`models/gateway-url.ts`), an origin, so the fallback keeps the SDK's path and query and changes only the origin. Server only: it imports the SDK (`SERVER_ONLY` in `tests/client-bundle.test.ts`). |
| `models/catalog.ts` | The model catalog (2026-10-08, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-08-straitly-model-routing-design.md` sections 3 and 6). **Server and test only** (in `SERVER_ONLY` of `tests/client-bundle.test.ts`) because it holds our per-model rates. `MODELS` (deep-frozen) lists each model by exact id with label `{ar, en}`, vendor, `hosting` (`west` or `china`), `tier`, `dialect`, `caps`, `rate` (USD per million tokens: `in`, `out`, `cacheRead`, `cacheWrite`), `auto` (automatic routing may use it; never true for `china`) and `pick` (a customer may choose it). `modelById(id: unknown)` is Map-backed, so `constructor`, `__proto__`, non-strings and unknown ids answer `undefined`; `automaticOf(list)` keeps the entries that are `auto` AND west-hosted (since 2026-10-09; `autoModels()` is `automaticOf(MODELS)`, frozen), `pickModels()`; `pickerView()` is the only thing the browser may be given (id, label, tier, hosting, no rate); `validateCatalog(entries)` lists every defect of a list and is what `tests/model-catalog.test.ts` runs on the real catalog and on lists built in the test. A Claude model's `rate` is the Anthropic list price (the direct-Anthropic fallback pays it, so the margin floor holds on the worst route); a gateway-only model's is list x 0.6. Today it holds only the three Claude 5.5 models (`claude-opus-5-5` premium, `claude-sonnet-5-5` standard, `claude-haiku-5-5` economy; Opus 5 and Sonnet 5 were removed on 2026-10-10, rule 970, so a stored pick of either is `unknown` and runs on the default); the other vendors come after the Straitly spike. Imports only `tiers.ts` and never `usage.ts`, which reads it. `scripts/check-models.ts` compares it with the gateway's `GET /v1/models` by hand (not in `npm run check`). |
| `models/gateway-url.ts` | **Pure, import-free** (2026-10-09, rule 810): `gatewayOrigin(raw)` checks `STRAITLY_BASE_URL`'s value for `anthropic()` in `model.ts` and for `scripts/check-models.ts`, one check for both, and returns the origin (`DEFAULT_GATEWAY_BASE_URL` when empty). https, or plain http to a loopback host (`localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `[::1]`); no whitespace or control character inside (the parser would drop a tab or newline without a word) and no port 0; no path (a `/v1` included: the SDK and the script add their own), query, fragment or user info. Two fixed refusal messages (`INVALID_GATEWAY_URL`, `GATEWAY_URL_NOT_ORIGIN`) that never contain the value. It reads no environment variable; the callers pass the value. |
| `models/tiers.ts` | **Client safe, import-free** (pinned in `tests/client-bundle.test.ts`): `Tier` (`economy`, `standard`, `premium`), `TIER_MULTIPLIER` (0.6, 1, 2: what a task costs in credits on a model of that tier, as a multiple of today's price) and `isTier`. `creditsFor` on the server reads this table (through `tierMultiplier`); the picker in the browser reads only its types, and every multiplier it shows comes from `GET /api/models`, worked out from this same table. |
| `model-picker.ts` | **Client safe** (2026-10-08, plan Task 7; imports only types from `models/tiers.ts`, pinned in `tests/client-bundle.test.ts`): the picker's logic over the `GET /api/models` answer. `PickerRowView` and `ModelsView` (the answer's shape, checked by `readModelsView`), `pickerOptions(view, surface, lang, autoLabel)` (Automatic first, then the rows with a server multiplier for that surface; chat leaves out rows without `tools`), `formatMultiplier` (`x0.6`), `chosenId` (the stored id while listed, else Automatic), `needsChinaConfirm`, `pickBodies` (the confirmation first, then the pick), `sendPick` (the bodies in order, stopping at the first refusal; answers `{why, body}`) and `pickRefusal` (one answer per refusal), `pickedChat` (the `chat` a conversation pick answers, shape-checked; `PickedChat` mirrors `ChatStatus`, both ways checked by the type check), `storedPick` (a chat pick made in the panel, then the open conversation's record from the page, then the listed rows), `pickChanges` (a change against the stored value itself, so a stale id can be cleared), `pickerLocked` (no chat pick while one is saved, while a reply is in flight, or before the list loads), `nextMadePicks` and `nextBrandPick` (the optimistic change and its rollback as pure steps), `NOTE_MS` (how long the refusal note stays), `unitsText` (the chat units sentence, Arabic plural forms). Holds no multiplier, tier table or rate: every number is the server's. |
| `models/route.ts` | **Server and test only** (in `SERVER_ONLY` of `tests/client-bundle.test.ts`), 2026-10-08, plan Task 5: `route(purpose, ctx?)` is the one place that decides which model a call runs on, and no other file under `src` names a Claude model for a call (`tests/model-route-calls.test.ts`). `Purpose` is `chat`, `generate`, `review`, `xreview`, `judge`, `onboard`, `imagePrompt`, `tool` or, since 2026-10-10 (rule 791), `agentScope` (the agent-scope classifier: `claude-haiku-5-5`, not in `OVERRIDABLE`; `agentScopeModel()` in `agent-scope-classify.ts` asks for it); `DEFAULTS` holds today's model for each (chat `claude-sonnet-5-5`, generate and onboard `claude-opus-5`, the rest `claude-sonnet-5`), so this release changes no model, price or reply; `DEFAULTS.chat` and `DEFAULTS.generate` equal `PRICED_AT` (pinned), and `ESTIMATE_MODELS` in `task-costs.ts` is built from `DEFAULTS`. Synchronous, no store, no network. Every automatic choice (a default, the judge's alternative) must be `hosting === "west"` and `auto === true`, checked in this file as well as in the catalog's data: a default that is not throws `default_model_not_auto:{purpose}:{id}`. Only `OVERRIDABLE` (`chat`, `generate`) read `ctx.stored`, a customer's stored pick (supplied by `models/overrides.ts`; `undefined` and `null` are nothing stored): it is used only if it is exactly a catalog id, `pick`, confirmed by the owner when it is not west-hosted (`ctx.confirmedChina === true`), for chat has tool use and, for generation with `ctx.task`, may run that type; otherwise the default runs and `rejected` says why (`unknown`, `not_pickable`, `china_unconfirmed`, `needs_tools`, `task_not_allowed`). `judge` is the default unless it is `ctx.generatedBy`, then an automatic alternative whose rate is no higher than the default judge's on every bucket (lowest total, then catalog order), so a judge never marks its own batch and the judge estimate stays an upper bound. One decision per batch and per turn: `generateBatch` resolves once and gives the same model to `creditsFor`, `generate()` and the judge; the chat routes resolve once and give it to `reserveChatTurn` and, through the gate, to `chat()`. |
| `models/overrides.ts` | **Server and test only** (in `SERVER_ONLY`; it imports `route.ts`), 2026-10-08, plan Task 6: a customer's picks at call time. `chatRouteFor(u, conversation)` and `generateRouteFor(u, brand, type)` hand the stored `Conversation.model` / `Brand.model` and `u.chinaHostingOk` (`chinaConfirmed`) to `route()`; the callers read those records under `sc(u)` themselves. `MODEL_UNAVAILABLE` (chat and generate, Arabic and English) is what the customer reads when a pick was not used; `MODEL_UNAVAILABLE_NOTE` is the same fact for an assistant to relay (chat tools, MCP). Written only by `src/app/api/models/route.ts` |
| `chat-budget.ts` | The hard per-message guards of a chat turn (2026-10-01, rules 311-313), checked in code before every model call: `CHAT_MAX_CALLS` (5), `CHAT_MAX_OUT` (8,000), `CHAT_MIN_OUT` (2,048: below it no call is made), `CHAT_FIRST_MIN_OUT` (4,000: what the first call should get at least), `nextMaxTokens(model, spentUsd, inputUsd)` (what is left of `CHAT_TURN_MAX_USD` at the model's output rate, capped at `CHAT_MAX_OUT`, 0 below `CHAT_MIN_OUT` or on a number it cannot read), `inputEstimateUsd(model, {cached, fresh}, first)` (an upper estimate of a call's input: `cached` is the prefix that carries `cache_control`, the tools and system blocks 1-2, priced as a cache write on the first call and as a cache read on later ones; `fresh`, the rest, at the plain input rate; `chat.ts` gives a later call the previous call's cache read and cache write tokens as `cached`, and its input, its output and the tool results added since as `fresh`) with `estTokens` at `CHARS_PER_TOKEN_EST` (3 characters a token, below the measured 3.3, so it over-counts), the history guard `trimHistory` (the newest `CHAT_HISTORY_MAX_MSGS` (16) messages, each cut to `CHAT_HISTORY_MSG_MAX_CHARS` (6,000) and together at most `CHAT_HISTORY_MAX_CHARS` (24,000), the owner's newest message always kept; a thread that starts with the assistant, as the first-run onboarding writes it, is kept as it is), and `fitFirstCall(model, messages, prefixChars, volatileChars)` (drops the oldest messages until the first call gets at least `CHAT_FIRST_MIN_OUT`, or only the newest is left; its `maxTokens` is 0 when even that leaves less than `CHAT_MIN_OUT`). Since 2026-10-12 (rule 1483) `workspaceLarge(trimmed, fit)`: the first call left out messages the history guard kept, or has less than `CHAT_FIRST_MIN_OUT`; the reply then carries `large: true` and the chat shows a quiet hint (rule 1484). |
| `topups.ts` | Credit top-ups (2026-10-01, rules 440-464, `flows.md` 38), imports only `pricing.ts`, `partner-rules.ts` (`ksaDay`; both import-free) and a type: `TopupLot` (`{id, credits, left, at, expiresAt, paidSar, paidOn, ref, by, paymentId?, refund?}`; `refund` since 2026-10-02, `TopupRefund`), `Receipt` (`{monthly, lots: [{id, n}]}`, where a charge's credits came from), `lotsOf` (earliest expiry first), `topupCredits`, `monthlyCredits` (the balance less the packs' credits), `settleTopups` (on every `getUser`: expired packs leave the balance, values repaired), `spendCredits` (plan credits first, then packs by expiry), `returnCredits` (a refund back where it came from; since 2026-10-02 the share of a pack that is gone or refunded goes to the plan credits, rule 461), `setMonthly` (renewal, plan change), `addMonthly` (bonuses, "+ credits"), `newLot`, `parseTopup` (the founder's request; the payment date is a Riyadh day not after today, rule 459), `topupRevenueSar`, `NO_CREDITS_HINT`, `topupView` (the billing page), `topupBalance` (`GET /api/profile`: credits, left, expiry) and `creditSplit` (the profile's plan and top-up credits, rule 456); since 2026-10-02 (founder decision, rules 460-464) the refund of unused credits: `unusedRefundSar` (credits left × price per credit paid, excluding VAT), `parseTopupRefund` (the founder's request), `refundUnused` (the credits left leave the pack and the balance, on a record held under the ledger lock) and `openLots` (packs not refunded: what the billing page and the profile list). |
| `chat-allowance.ts` | The chat allowance (2026-10-01, rules 308-310), server only (client components import its `ChatStatus` type alone): `ChatStatus` (`used`, clamped to the allowance, what the owner is shown; `allowance`; `left`; `counted`, the raw count, which can pass an allowance lowered mid-period; `period` "trial" or "month"; `overflow: {on, available, used, credits, hold}`: the messages continued on credits this month, the credits they were billed, and `chatOverflowHold()`), `chatStatus(u)` (up to three reads in parallel), `reserveChatTurn(u)` (INCRBY the account's counter before the turn: inside the allowance the message is counted; past it the count is taken back, and the turn goes ahead only when `overflowAvailable(u)` (a paid plan), `User.chatOverflow` is on and the balance is at least `chatOverflowHold()`; nothing is taken from the balance before the turn, and `chat_over` is counted; else `chat_allowance` or `chat_credits`), `settleChatTurn` / `runChatTurn` (after the turn, on every path: a turn that threw, cost nothing or never reached the model, paused, mock and a workspace too large for one message included, is given back and not billed; an over-allowance turn is billed by `settleChatOverflow` in `credits.ts` and the credits added to `chat_cred`; `runChatTurn` strips `usd` from the reply), and `refusalText` (the MCP answer). Counted per ACCOUNT (`u.email` is the owner's for every seat): `chatMsgKey` per UTC month, or `chatTrialKey` once for a trial. Called by `/api/chat` and MCP `ask_noor` before `chat()` saves the owner's message. |
| `noor.ts` | Batch content generation (`generate`), task specs, second-pass reviewer `critique` (one prompt per owning agent, `reviewerPrompt`), and since 2026-09-30 the cross-agent review `crossReview`: after `critique`, dash stripping and `capDiscounts`, the reviewers `crossReviewers(type)` gives (`CROSS_REVIEW`: Razan for `gtm_plan`, `growth_experiments`, `retention_play`, `board_deck`, `decision_memo`, `strategy_review`; Majed for `pricing_review` and `gtm_plan`; never the author, never Reem) each get one Sonnet call (low effort, 4000 tokens, `crossReviewPrompt(reviewer)` cached: focus `CROSS_FOCUS`, a slice of the reviewer's playbook through `playbookSection`) and return verdicts (`parseCrossVerdicts`) that only add `Generated.crossReview`; any failure returns the items as they were (`flows.md` 33). Each call is one attempt of at most 45 s (`CROSS_REVIEW_TIMEOUT_MS`, `maxRetries: 0`), and `generate` starts none with less than 60 s of `GenOptions.deadline` left (`CROSS_REVIEW_MIN_MS`), so the optional notes never push a charged batch past the platform's limit. `GenOptions.leadBrief` (2026-09-30, a handoff item) puts Majed's brief in the generation message after the owner's notes as `leadBriefBlock` and hands it to the reviewer and the cross-reviewers as grounding; without one every message is unchanged. Since 2026-10-09 (spec A5) the specs of Dana's three reports and Majed's strategy review end with `reportData`: the WORKSPACE, STORE, SITE, SOCIAL and AD DATA blocks, ad totals only from AD DATA, a change only where `prev` exists (else [رقم] and a second read), and the NOT READ list named exactly as listed (`flows.md` 50 step 20). |
| `generate-batch.ts` | The one pipeline for every paid text batch (`/api/generate`, chat `generate_content` and `draft_items`, MCP `generate_content`, `runWeekly`, `routineFor`): `generateBatch(user, scope, opts)` checks the model switch, brand, trial and `missingData`, charges the owning agent under its monthly cap, reads the grounding inside the refunding try, calls `generate`, builds each task with `taskFromItem` (status by `initialStatus` on the item's type, channel, title and body, so #227's channel rule for `{{1}}` slots holds on every path at once; it also stamps `pb`, the prompt versions, `prompt-stamp.ts`, keeps the cross-agent verdicts as `crossReview` and holds a flagged item pending under auto-approve), saves the batch with `Promise.all` together with its one `generated` activity row (and one `cross_reviewed` row per reviewing agent, `crossReviewRows`) and calls `judgeLater` with the grounding and the batch's `via`; refusals are typed and charge nothing, and a refusal at the charge on an owner path (app, chat, MCP) is one `cap_reached` or `no_credits` row (`flows.md` 8.13, 32). `opts.by` is who asked (the chat turn's, `tokenBy` for MCP, else `byOf(user)`; the system job on the scheduled paths). `opts.leadBrief` and `opts.parent` (2026-09-30) serve a handoff item's run: the brief goes to `generate` and the judge's grounding only when set, and `taskFromItem` keeps `parent` on every task. `opts.deadline` is the caller's request or invocation deadline, handed to `generate` for the cross-agent review; `BATCH_RUN_MIN_MS` (180 s) is what a batch needs of it before its charge, which the handoff run, the auto-run and the routines check themselves. Since 2026-10-01: a paid account due its monthly credits is renewed (`renewIfDue`) right before the charge (rule 332); `opts.once` charges through `chargeOnce` and refunds through `refundOnce` (rule 333); the refund guard covers the reads, the generation, the task saves and `opts.commit` (the Sunday plan's `lastWeekly` marker), a refund removes every task of the batch (`dropTasks` in `brand.ts`), and the activity row is written after it (rule 334). `opts.agentScope` (2026-10-10, rule 796) carries the agent-scope modes the owner's routes read once (`agentScopePreflight`); the batch does not read the field yet. `opts.notesGate` (rule 796, set only by `POST /api/generate` and MCP `generate_content`) is the input gate on the owner's notes: `runBatch` runs `checkGenerationNotes` after `paused`, `no_brand`, `trial_over` and `missing_data` and before `renewIfDue` and the charge, on the batch's own brand read. In `enforce` it is awaited before the charge and a block is the typed refusal `agent_scope` (with `message`) or `agent_scope_slow` (with `retry` "hour" or "day"), through `refuse()`; in `shadow` the check runs beside the batch and `generateBatch` awaits it in a `finally` on every path out. The refusal type `ScopeRefusal` is returned only by the overload that takes a gate, so no other caller handles it. |
| `judge.ts` | Post-response quality scoring of generated tasks (`judgeLater` via `after()`, `:192`), scored against the owner's notes and workspace data, with automatic-fail caps (`AUTOFAIL`); `judgeTasks` writes each verdict with `updateTask` (`:145`), so an approval, an edit or a render that landed while the judge worked keeps its fields, and in the same write sends an item only auto-approval passed back to `pending` when its verdict carries an automatic fail (main's security gates, `judgeveto:` notification), then records one `judged` activity row (mean score, count) and one `autofail` row per trigger that fired (`judgedRows`). |
| `evals.ts` | Behavioural metrics per agent, chat thumbs storage. |
| `decisions.ts` | Decision records per scope, whoever decided (the owner, an admin or a member; `decisionOf`, `recordDecisions`, `listDecisions`), written by the approve and publish routes, each with `by`, who decided (`byOf`). `recordDecisions` also writes each decision's `decision` activity row (not for `publish`: the publish paths record every publish as `published`). |
| `activity.ts` | The per-agent activity log (2026-09-29): `Activity` (`at`, `kind` from `ACTIVITY_KINDS`, `agent`, `by`, `via` from `ACTIVITY_VIAS`, `type`, up to 10 `tasks`, `count`, `credits`, `outcome`, `score`, `autofail`, a 200-character `note`, `media`, `refunded`), `recordActivity(scope, rows)` (bounded rows, `pushCapped` to `activity:{scope}` in one `Promise.all`; never throws), `listActivity(scope, {agent, kind, before, limit})` (one lrange, unreadable rows skipped, sorted by `at`, filtered in memory, an `at` cursor that never splits rows of one time), `recordRun` (a routine's or the Sunday plan's outcome, `idle`/`no_data` once per period through `actskip:`), `cross_reviewed` rows since 2026-09-30 (the reviewing agent, `outcome` `flags:N`), `isActivityCursor`, `publicActivity` (a row with `by` through `publicBy`, for GET /api/activity and the Activity page). |
| `actor.ts` | Who acted: `By` (`kind` owner, member, founder, token or system, plus `email`, `role`, `imp`, `job`), `byOf(u)` worked out from the session's `u.actor` alone (typed with `auth.ts`'s `Actor`): the owner, a member with its own login email and role (a role a member cannot hold is dropped), the founder impersonating as `{kind: "founder", email: <the account>, imp: actor.impersonating}` (the founder's email or "key", never `actor.email`, which is the owner's there), a token; no cookie is read, so the impersonation cookies alone make nobody support. A user with no actor never comes from a request (a cron, routine or script user loaded with `getUser`) and is `{kind: "system", job: "cron"}`, never the owner; the scheduled paths pass their own `systemBy(job)`. Also `systemBy(job)`, `tokenBy(u)` (the MCP route's connected app, the same as `byOf` gives for `userFromBearer`'s token actor), `isBy`, and `publicBy(b)`: the `by` the account's own logins receive, without `imp` (GET /api/activity, the Activity and Governance pages, the governance save and restore responses); only the founder console reads `imp`. Never taken from a request body. |
| `prompt-stamp.ts` | Which prompts made an item: `promptStamp(type, brand?)` = 12-hex hashes of the owning agent's playbook, review.md, the brand's industry playbook (`id:hash`) and the prompts written in code (`codeHash`: the agent's `fixedPrompt` with the playbook left out, the task spec, `reviewerRubric`, `JUDGE_RUBRIC`); `promptHashes(types)` for a golden run. |
| `feedback.ts` | `feedbackFor(scope, agent)`: the last 30 days of the owner's decisions, typed reasons, the judge's low scores and (chat only) thumbs-down reasons on one agent's own work, as escaped data for that agent's next prompt (uncached block, `feedbackBlock`); empty when `gov.learning` is off. |
| `golden.ts` | Weekly fixed "golden set" regression run across agents. Each run records the prompt versions it started with (`playbooks`, from `promptHashes`) and each result its `pb`; `summarize` marks `promptChanged` per agent by comparing each measured case's stamp with the same case's in the previous run, and tags that agent's regression lines "(prompt changed)". It calls `generate` with `crossReview: false`: the cross-agent review writes notes only and would cost a call per reviewer per case. Version 6 (2026-10-09): Dana's weekly report and board deck cases read one fixed fixture of the fictional roastery's workspace, site, social, ad and not-read blocks (`flows.md` 50 step 20). |
| `onboard.ts` | Builds brand file, workspace files and starter plan from a crawled site in three parallel model calls (`PARTS`: core, identity, market), one retry per part. Since 2026-10-01 `buildFromSite(..., deadline)` ends every call by the onboarding request's deadline and retries a part only with `ONBOARD_RETRY_MIN_MS` (60 s) left (rule 336). |
| `onboard-confirm.ts` | The owner's confirmation of the site onboarding builds from (`onboardok:{scope}`); a link only pre-fills the start page. Since 2026-09-30 also `heldOnboard(u, scope)` (an added brand's setup charge held on the account, `User.onboardPaid`) and `sweepOnboardHolds` (the daily cron's resolution of holds no run used for a day: refunded without a brand, dropped with one; each refund is one `onboard_refunded` activity row in that brand, by the cron). The ledger side (`chargeOnboard`, `refundOnboard`, `settleOnboard`, `resolveOnboardHolds`) is in `credits.ts`. |
| `crawl.ts` | Fetches a user-supplied URL server-side and reduces HTML to text, colors, fonts, platform. |
| `safe-fetch.ts` | `assertPublicUrl` and `safeFetch(input, init, opts)` (`:65`): http(s) only, no credentials in the URL, no loopback, private, link-local or metadata address, redirects followed by hand (5 at most), each hop re-validated, credential headers dropped on a cross-origin hop, no redirect on a write. Since 2026-10-12 every request goes through `pinnedAgent()` (an undici `Agent` whose connect-time lookup is `pinnedLookup`): the socket connects only to an address that lookup checked, so a name that resolves to a public address for the check and to a private one a moment later cannot reach the private one (rule 1342, DNS rebinding). A call with no `signal` of its own gets a 30-second deadline (`providerTimeout`, `src/lib/fetch-timeout.ts`, rule 1343). With `opts.httpsOnly` (`:61`) every hop, the first and each redirect, must be https (`unsafe_url:not_https`, `:70`); only `copyToStore` passes it, so crawl, catalogs and connectors keep following public http hops. |
| `turnstile.ts` | Cloudflare Turnstile (2026-10-01): `turnstileSiteKey()` (the public key, only when both keys are set), `verifyTurnstile(token, ip)` (form POST to the fixed `siteverify` URL, 5 s timeout; `ok` / `failed` / `unavailable`, never throws; `ok` when Turnstile is off; `unavailable` in a production deploy with exactly one key set, rule 978), `turnstileState()` (`on` / `off` / `half`) and `captchaRefusal(req, token)` for the signup and forgot routes. A production deploy logs `turnstile off` or the missing key's name once per process (rules 909, 978). The widget is `src/components/app/Turnstile.tsx` (explicit render, reset after a refused submit). |
| `auth-client.ts` | Import-free helpers for the auth forms (2026-10-01): `postJson` (never throws), `failureCode`, `AUTH_ERR` / `authMessage` (AR and EN), `passwordChecks`, `rulesStatus`, `unmetRules`. |
| `admin-audit-shape.ts` | Client-safe half of the audit log (rule 1179, no imports): `AUDIT_SECTIONS`, `auditText`, and `auditDiff(before, after)` (the keys whose value changed, one level deep) that the Log page shows; `admin-audit.ts` re-exports the section names. |
| `admin-users.ts` | Pure helpers for the Users table (rule 1178): `parseUserQuery` (filters, sort, page from the query string, known values only), `userQueryParams`, `matchUser`, `sortUsers`, `pageOf`, `userFacets` and `tileCounts`; `GET /api/admin/plan?view=table` applies them on the server. |
| `admin-workspace.ts` | The founder console's read-only workspace view (2026-10-17, rules 2077-2083, flows.md section 84): `workspaceView(chosen)` finds the account with `getUser` and, for a member's address, `getMember` then the owner named on the stored member record; then one `Promise.all` reads `listMembers`, `peekInvites`, `listBrands`, `lastSignIn` and `lastActive`, and one `pmap` the members' last sign-ins. Returns the owner, the plan with seats (`seatLimit`) and brands (`brandCapFor`), members, invites (no ids or tokens), brands and `openedFrom`. Writes nothing. |
| `suspension.ts` | Client-safe (imports only `./dates`, 2026-10-17, rules 2070-2076): the stored `suspended` shape (`true` = no end date, `{ until: ISO }` = until then), `isSuspended(u, now)` (the only reader, fails closed on a broken value), `suspensionEnd`, `suspensionExpired`, `parseSuspendFor(for, date)` (indefinite, 7, 30 or a Riyadh day up to 365 days away; else `bad_until`), `formatSuspensionEnd`, and the customer message `SUSPENDED_UNTIL` / `suspendedMessage`. |
| `suspension-server.ts` | `endExpiredSuspension(email)` (rule 2073): under the account lock, re-checks `suspensionExpired`, sets `suspended = false` and writes one `suspension.expired` audit row as actor `system`. Called from `finishLogin` and the console's account detail; best effort. |
| `components/admin/` (Phase 4) | Founder console panels (moved from `components/app`, rule 1181): `AdminForm` (`useAdminForm`, the reason form every mutation opens, rule 1176), `DataTable` and `Pager` (rule 1178), `users-copy.ts`, `Users.tsx` (server table with filters in the address), `UserSheet.tsx` (account detail in a `Sheet` with tabs, rule 1180), `Log.tsx` (rule 1179). `components/app/ImpersonationBanner.tsx` is the app's sticky banner while the founder views an account (rule 1177). |
| `admin-fetch.ts` | Import-free helper for the founder console (2026-10-01, rule 436): `adminFetch` (every admin call of the console sections in `src/components/admin` (one per section since rule 874), `GoldenPanel`, `WishlistPanel`, `LeadsPanel`, and since the partner programme `PartnersPanel` and `PaymentsPanel`, rule 418) announces a 401 on `window` (`ADMIN_ENDED_EVENT`), and the console then shows `ADMIN_ENDED` (AR and EN: the key session ended after 15 minutes idle or 12 hours) with a link from `adminEndedHref` to the key form (`?login=1`) or the sign-in page. |
| `admin-2fa.ts` | The founder console's second factor (2026-10-12, rules 1370-1384): the enrolment `admin:2fa` (authenticator secret sealed with AES-256-GCM under a key derived from `CONNECT_SECRET` with its own label; the recovery code's SHA-256), the pending setup, step-ups (`admin2fa:step:{sha256(id)}` behind the `nashr_admin_2fa` cookie), burned steps (`admin2fa:used:{step}`), the code budget, and `secondFactorRefusal(req, who)`, the gate every founder write calls after `adminActor`. |
| `staff.ts` | Staff records (2026-10-13, rules 1630, 1637, 1641-1644, 1654): `Staff` at `staff:{email}` (name, role, bcrypt cost-12 `passHash`, the authenticator secret sealed with AES-256-GCM under SHA-256(`nashr-staff-totp:` + `CONNECT_SECRET`) with its `kid`, hashed recovery codes, `sessionsAfter`, `disabled`), `staff:index`, `staff:config` (`customerPathOff`), `withStaffLock`, the password policy (`passwordRefusal`), `staffPasswordMatches` (always one compare, a dummy hash for an unknown address), recovery codes, the sign-in lock (`stafffail:`), single-use setup and reset codes (`staffsetup:`, 30 minutes). |
| `staff-session.ts` | The staff session (rules 1633-1635, 1648): `__Host-thakeen_staff` (dev name only on loopback http) to `staffsession:{sha256(id)}` with the limits of `idle-session.ts`; the 5-minute pre-auth step `__Host-thakeen_staff_pre` / `staffpre:`; `readStaffSession`, `openStaffSession`, `destroyStaffSession`, `endStaffSessions` (`sessionsAfter`), `staffSessionLive` (ends a customer view opened from it). |
| `idle-session.ts` | One copy of the idle and absolute limits for short console sessions (the key session and the staff session): live while both hold, renewed at most once per window with `SET XX EX`, so a renewal racing a logout never revives one. |
| `staff-signin.ts`, `staff-code.ts` | The sign-in steps (rules 1631, 1632, 1642): limits per IP (`stafflogin:`, `staffstep:`, `staffsetup:`), the person's own code (`checkStaffCode`: 5 tries per IP and per address, steps burned per person, a secret sealed with the previous key resealed), enrolment (`staffenrol:`, 15 minutes), and opening the session with its step-up. |
| `staff-admin.ts` | Helpers of the staff management routes (rules 1636, 1638-1640, 1650): `freshCodeRefusal`, `isStaffOwner`, `staffView` (no hash or secret), `lastOwner`, `secretJson` (`no-store`). |
| `staff-alert.ts` | Founder alert of a break-glass staff change (2026-10-13 security review, rule 1643): `keyStaffAlert` (bilingual subject and text: action, login and UTC time only, never a code or reason) and `alertFoundersKeyStaff`, which mails every `ADMIN_EMAILS` address through `sendEmail` when `mailReady()`, run in `after()` by `POST /api/admin/staff` for a key-session `reset` or `enable`. |
| `console-origin.ts` | `consoleWriteRefusal` (rule 1647): every console write refuses a foreign `Origin`, `Sec-Fetch-Site` other than `same-origin`/`none`, and a non-JSON body; run by `secondFactorRefusal`, the key form and every staff route. |
| `totp.ts` | Pure RFC 6238 / RFC 4226 codes (HMAC-SHA1, 6 digits, 30 s, one step either side), base32, the otpauth URI, the recovery code and its hash (2026-10-12, rules 1370-1372). No store, no dependency. |
| `qr.ts` | `qrPath(text)`: the QR code of a text (error correction M, a quiet zone of four modules, `QR_QUIET`) as one SVG path of rectangles, encoded in the calling process by `uqr` (2026-10-13, rules 1620-1621). No request, no store, no markup. Used by `components/admin/QrCode.tsx` for the second factor's setup. |
| `catalog.ts` | Discovers product URLs via sitemaps/robots and scrapes product pages into `products.md`. |
| `files.ts` | Workspace Markdown files ("memory"), `memoryBlock` for prompts (opens with the owner's onboarding goal). |
| `conversations.ts` | Multiple chat threads per scope, substring search (since 2026-10-12 the list is one MGET and the search reads the threads 8 at a time, rule 1431). `liveConversations(scope, ids)` (2026-09-30): which conversations still exist, in one MGET, for Majed's auto-run (a proposal whose thread was deleted never starts on its own). |
| `governance.ts` | Per-scope rules: auto-approve by type, weekly cap, learning, reviewer, dash stripping, and a monthly credit cap per agent (`agentCaps`). `saveGov` validates every field of the request body (agent ids, task types, booleans, finite numbers, clamps). History (2026-09-29): `saveGovAs(scope, patch, by)` reads, merges and writes under `govlock:{scope}` and pushes a `GovRevision` (who, dotted `changed` paths, whole `before` and `after`) to `govhist:{scope}` only when something changed; `govDiff`, `listGovHistory`, `historyView` (the changed settings of each revision, `by` through `publicBy`); `rollbackGov` puts a revision's `before` back through the same checks as a full patch (`restorePatch`: every task type and every agent cap explicit) and records it as a new revision with `rollbackOf`. Each revision is also one `gov_changed` or `gov_rollback` activity row naming the changed settings. Majed's auto-run switch (2026-09-30): `majedAutoHandoff` (off by default), `HANDOFF_AGENTS` and `hasHandoffCap`, and `govRefusal`, checked by `saveGovAs` under the lock (on only with a cap on an agent Majed hands work to; off to on only with the owner's `confirmAutoHandoff`) and by `saveGov` (the cap rule; `GovRefusedError`). |
| `brand-rules.ts` | Import-free brand gate: banned words as rules (`brandRules`, `checkText`, `gateTask`, `initialStatus`) and, since 2026-09-29, unfilled placeholders (`placeholderHits`; `gateTask(..., { publish: true })` refuses every placeholder at publish), used by the approve and publish routes, auto-approval, the chat publish tool, the MCP `check_brand` and `save_draft` tools and the inbox (the golden evals use the banned-word check only). |
| `publish-once.ts` | One publish per item and platform (2026-09-29): `publishOnce` (lock, repeat refusal, attempt marker for a result nobody saw, `again`), `mayHavePosted`. Used by `POST /api/publish/{id}` and the chat tool `publish_item`; each writes its `published` activity row only after a publish that went out (`ok: true`); the route also writes it, and a pending item's `publish` decision, for `earlier_live`, whose unknown attempt went out and wrote none (`publishOnce` returns the task and `approving` with it; only an `again` gets that answer, and the chat tool never sends one). |
| `brand-proposals.ts` | Brand changes an agent may only propose (2026-09-29; since 2026-10-05 every editable field, `banned` additions included, rule 568): `PROPOSAL_FIELDS`, `DIALECTS`, `FIELD_MAX`, `withBrandLock` (also used by `PUT /api/brand`, rule 570); `proposeBrandChange`, `decideProposal` (once, stale check; an applied one names its field and proposing agent, for the `brand_changed` activity row `POST /api/brand/proposals/{id}` writes), `proposalKeys` (every key `wipeAccount` deletes). |
| `instructions-safety.ts` | **Client safe** (imports only `normalize` from `brand-rules.ts`). The pure safety layer of the agent instructions (2026-10-10, rules 930-935): the caps (`INSTRUCTION_CHARS` 600, `PLATFORM_CHARS` 2000, `WORKSPACE_ITEMS` 120, `PLATFORM_ITEMS` 100, `MAX_REFS` 3, `TEST_BRIEF_CHARS` 500), the plan table `INSTRUCTION_PLAN` (trial 3 / starter 10 / pro 25 / business 60 / agency 100 active instructions per brand; per-agent targets from pro; reference files from starter), `normalizeInstruction` and `screenInstruction` (`injection`, `authority`, `secret`, Arabic and English). |
| `instructions-store.ts` | The stored layers of the agent instructions (2026-10-10, rules 936-941, 954): `getLayer`, `saveDraft` (the `base` edit-counter check, `editBase`, 409 `stale`), `discardDraft`, `activate`, `restore`, `appendActive` (a chat apply), `listHistory`, `listFeed`, `removeFeedRowsFor`, `isPaused`/`setPaused`, `instructionKeys` (the keys the account wipe deletes). Every write is read, checked and written under `skillslock:{scope}` (`db.lockOwned`). The platform layer is the scope literal `PLATFORM`. |
| `agent-instructions.ts` | The one place stored instructions become prompt text (2026-10-10, rules 942-945): `loadLayers` (two `mget`s) and the pure `instructionBlocks` (platform block, fenced workspace block, stamp hashes), `NO_LAYERS`, `skippedCount`. No route, gate or tool list imports it (rule 951). Callers: `noor.ts`, `chat.ts`, `judge.ts`, `generate-batch.ts`, `golden.ts`, `media/prompts.ts`, `media/start.ts` and the two test routes. |
| `instructions-diff.ts`, `instructions-http.ts`, `instructions-ui.ts` | `diffSets` and `summarizeRevision` (what changed between two sets, shared by Compare and the revision list; `instructions-store.ts` re-exports `diffSets`); `statusOf`/`refused`/`PLAN_ERRORS` (a refused store call to its HTTP status) and `publicLayer`/`publicRevision` (every `by` through `publicBy`); and the page's own helpers in `instructions-ui.ts` (`errorText`, `editBaseOf`, `newNonce`, `formAfterWrite`, `restoreBlocked`, `agentsControl`, `showRefs`, `parseTestResult`, `PLATFORM_TARGETS`). |
| `instructions-test.ts` | `testQuote`/`testPrice`/`testOptionsFor`: what "Test this instruction" costs (two items of the type at the model `generateRouteFor` picks) and which model, shared by the charging route and the price on the page. |
| `instruction-proposals.ts` | Standing rules an agent may only propose from chat (2026-10-10, rules 953-954; the `brand-proposals.ts` pattern): `proposeInstruction`, `decideInstructionProposal` (claim, outcome record, `appendActive`), `instructionProposalStates`, `instructionProposalKeys` (the wipe). |
| `spend-intent.ts` | Pure, import-free (2026-10-13, rule 1510): `spendIntent(text, "content"|"image")`, the deterministic check that the owner's own latest message asks for paid work in words. Arabic (folded: alef forms, ى, ة, diacritics) and English request verbs, deliverable nouns and image nouns, a negation or question word within three words cancelling a verb, clauses split on punctuation. Reads only a string; anything else is no intent. |
| `spend-proposals.ts` | The spend gate's cards (2026-10-13, rules 1511-1512; the `instruction-proposals.ts` pattern): `proposeSpend`, `getSpendProposal`, `spendProposalStates`, `spendProposalKeys`, `spendCard` (drops `by`), `decideSpend` (claim, once, then `generateBatch` or `renderImage` with the stored fields). `SPEND_CARDS_PER_REPLY = 2`. |
| `regulated.ts` | Import-free (2026-10-13, rules 1520-1522): `regulatedSector(industry)` (pharmacy, health, real estate, legal, finance; Arabic folded prefixes and whole words, English whole words), `isRegulated(brand)`, `DISCLAIMER` and `withDisclaimer(body, sector)` (one plain line in the body's language, idempotent). `initialStatus` never auto-approves a regulated brand; `taskFromItem` appends the line to customer-facing items. |

| `cloud-drives.ts` | The customer's cloud drives (2026-10-13, rules 1775-1789, branch only): Google Drive, Dropbox and OneDrive, each a Composio connection (toolkits `googledrive`, `dropbox`, `one_drive`). `searchDrive` (one call, constant cap and type filter), `importFromDrive` (id and extension checked first; bytes from the temporary `s3url` through `store.ts` `download`, or inline base64 measured before decoding; media into the library through `keepInLibrary`, text into `drive-<name>.md` or `.csv`), `exportToDrive` (only a file indexed in the caller's scope, staged with a signed link, never overwriting), `drivesOf`, `driveReady`. Imported only by `api/drives`, `api/media/upload` and `LibraryPanel` (types). No iCloud: Apple has no public API for it; the device upload covers it through the Files picker. |

Each part also reads the 28 days before its window in the same run (GA4 named `dateRanges`, rows split by the `dateRange` column found in `dimensionHeaders`, the landing pages ordered window first; one more Search Console query by date) and keeps `prev` only where Google returned a figure (rule 733); a figure not read is null, never 0 (rule 737). Exports the `Ga`, `Gsc`, `GaTotals` and `GscTotals` types. |
| `social-stats.ts` | Instagram account and post results, LinkedIn page totals and page views (rule 748), the Facebook Page's results (rule 739, read in `social-read/facebook.ts`) the YouTube channel's (rule 740, read in `social-read/youtube.ts`) and the TikTok account's (rule 742, read in `social-read/tiktok.ts`) through Composio (2026-10-08, rules 663-673; snapshot v2 with one part per platform and monthly history, rules 737, 746): `READERS` (platform to provider, `holds`, `read`; a reader answers a `ReadPart`, a part with an optional known state that `syncSocial` moves into `errors`), `holdsSocial` (some reader of that provider holds the record), `syncSocial` reads every part the workspace holds at once under one 30-second deadline (`SOCIAL_DEADLINE_MS`, `beforeDeadline` before every Composio read, rule 741) and keeps what succeeded, each reader also reading the previous period (Instagram a second account-insights read, LinkedIn 56 days in one call split by `splitDays`, its page views over the same days in one more; `rangeBefore`, rule 733), `upsertHistory`, `getSocialSnapshot` (v2, or a snapshot from before v2 read as not read yet), `syncSocialIfDue` (daily cron, last in the day), `readSocialSoon` (a first read after a connect or a new page, run after the response) and `socialDataBlock` for Dana's reports. A refused token is converted inside `syncSocialIfDue` with the refused part's provider; `PartlyRead` (a `ReconnectRequired`) marks a read that saved the snapshot while one part's token was refused and some part was actually read (`nothingRead`, the Not-read list's own test: a follower count or a total) (rule 735). Read only; captions are cleaned and capped. The Reports page shows it in `SocialStats.tsx`, with X's last read as its own row, the not-read items (rule 738) and the refresh buttons of `RefreshButton.tsx` (2026-10-09, spec section 8). |
| `social-read/common.ts` / `social-read/facebook.ts` / `social-read/youtube.ts` / `social-read/tiktok.ts` | The social readers' shared helpers, the Facebook Page reader, the YouTube channel reader and the TikTok account reader (2026-10-08). `common.ts` imports nothing at run time: `SOCIAL_PUB_KEY`, `EMPTY_TOTALS`, `beforeDeadline` (rule 741), `rangeBefore` and `splitDays` (rule 733), the strict `count` (a clean number or null), `settle`, `ISO_DAY`, `handle` (an Instagram username or an X handle: letters, digits, `_` and `.`, at most 80, since `clean` drops `_`) and the adapter's error shapes; a strict count makes every Graph value a clean number or null; `fb_no_insights` when the app lacks `read_insights`) and `fbUrl` (https `facebook.com` links only, no query string). `youtube.ts` (rule 740, spec B2): `holdsYt` (a Composio YouTube record with a UC `meta.channelId`), `readYoutube` (the login's own channel only, `channel==MINE` and `mine`: subscribers, 56 days of daily analytics in one call split into the window and `prev`, at most 30 uploads published in the window and not private with their public counts; totals null and no `prev`, since TikTok's counts are lifetime; `tt_no_scope` when a scope is refused, never a reconnect) and `ttUrl` (https `tiktok.com`/`www.tiktok.com` links only, query and hash cut). All three import `composio.ts` directly, so all three are on the rule-652 list. |
