Since 2026-09-30 `getSchedule(scope)` defaults `weeklyPlan` to on only for the default brand (`!scope.includes("#")`), and `saveSchedule` stores only fields set explicitly and returns the merged view (rule 247). A save that sets `weeklyPlan` stamps `weeklyPlanSetAt`; on an added brand an unstamped stored `weeklyPlan` (the old merged default) is ignored. |
| `routines.ts` | Owner-enabled routines for agents with real input data (`ROUTINES`: Dana's weekly report, Noor's cart recovery, Razan's cash forecast, Majed's monthly review), each with a gate checked in code; `runRoutine` (period, model state, lock, trial, gate, the invocation's time left: `retry:late` with less than 180 s (`BATCH_RUN_MIN_MS`) of the cron's `deadline`, weekly cap, charge with the agent's cap, refunding generation, save, judge, notify) and `dueRoutines` for the cron. `runRoutine` records the outcome of every run that took the lock (`recordRun`, `systemBy("routine")`; a throw is `error`); `routineSkipKeys` lists the `actskip:` markers for `wipeAccount`. `holdBatch(scope, holdKey, count, cap)` (exported, 2026-09-30) is the weekly-cap check and hold every autonomous batch shares, the routines' and Majed's auto-run's (`autoHoldKey`); it and Dana's gate count `isAutonomous` items (`brand.ts`: `source: "scheduled"` or an auto-run handoff item; never an MCP draft). `markOwnerSync(scope, now)` and `OWNER_SYNC_KEY` (2026-10-08, rule 736): a person's Refresh of the site or social figures, which Dana's gate counts as activity. |
| `changelog.ts` | Import-free content for `/{lang}/updates`: `CHANGELOG` (dated, tagged, bilingual entries of merged work), `SEED_WISHES` (wishlist items with stable ids), statuses and the page copy. Safe to import from client components. |
| `wishlist.ts` | Wishlist storage: `listWishes` (counts in one `scardMany`), `setVote`, `votableId` / `existingId`, suggestions queue, founder promote / status / hide / delete, `cleanText`, and `forgetWishlistUser` for account deletion. Server only. |
| `notifications.ts` | In-app notifications per account, daily follow-ups with dedupe fingerprints claimed atomically (`pushNotif` → `pushMany`: SET NX on `notifseen:`, then one write of the bell; since 2026-09-29, a read then a write let two overlapping runs both notify). Since 2026-09-30 every read-then-write of the bell (`pushNotifs`' list write, `markAllRead`) holds the account's bell lock (`withBellLock`, `notiflock:{email}`, 10 s; written anyway past about 3 s), and a bell write that fails releases the fingerprints it claimed before the error goes on. Reem's overdue alerts (`overdueTodoAlerts`, since 2026-09-29): one `todo` alert per overdue item on the local day it fell behind and 1, 3 and 7 days later (`overdueAlertFingerprint`: scope, item, due time, step), at most `SEC_ALERTS_PER_DAY` (5) a brand a local day with the claims beyond it handed back; after the seventh day only the brief. Since 2026-10-03 (Reem phase 3 A1, rules 479-480) the alerts follow the chase rule (`overdueAlerts` with the brand's `CalData`): the input is the overdue, chase and waiting buckets; the fingerprint's anchor is the due time for the owner's own item and the chase moment's anchor for what someone else owes; the owner's and an agent's items count calendar days and alert any day (ruling R16), a contact's count working days and are never sent on a quiet day; a step that only holds today keeps its marker alive (SET XX, 7 days, ruling R15); on a Ramadan morning a debt held for a window later today is sent with that time; the first alert after a contact's absence ended in the last 7 days says they are back, once per absence (`backFingerprint`, SET NX, 7 days). |
| `secretary.ts` | Reem's store (server only): `SEC_KEYS`, `SEC_ID` (`s` + 16 hex), `zoneFor` (the brand's zone, one read), `parseFields` / `parseOwnerChange` (typed, bounded, cleaned input in the brand's zone; too long is refused), `createItem` (a they-owe item without a date gets `expectBy`, two working days on), `changeItem` (under `sec:lock:{scope}:{id}`, checked against `rev`, one audit line per change), `mutateItem` (the `OWNER_ONLY` actions complete, confirm_done, reject_done, delete and restore, a change of an item's inbox link and a private note need the owner on the web; a proposal ends a snooze; a restore that reopens an item counts against the 500 open items; `chased` records a follow-up, restarts the chase clock and never closes, only on someone else's item), `closeLinked` (the one automatic close: the linked inbox task is approved; chat and MCP link only at creation and only to a pending task), `agendaFor` (one sorted-set read, one MGET, the pending count, the brand's zone, one MGET of linked tasks, which also yields `facts`: what code read about each link, shown next to an agent's claim), `factOf`, `listDone`, `listDeleted`, `purgeExpired`, `secretaryDaily`, `secretaryKeys` (for `wipeAccount`), `overdueAlertFingerprint`, `todoPage`, snooze presets (local time), and the four tools (`SEC_TOOL_DEFS`, `runSecretaryTool`) shared by chat and MCP; `toolFields` decides in code which arguments each tool may set, because MCP arguments are not checked against the schema. Free: no credits, no model call. Since 2026-09-30 (phase 2): `indexWrites` also keeps the reminder index (`dueWrites`: `sec:due:{scope}` and `sec:duescopes`), `fireReminder` and `timedReminders`, `reopen` (owner-only, counted against the open cap), `remindAt` (future, brand's zone) and a they-owe contact's `phone` (owner in the app only, never in a tool answer), the `nextweek` snooze preset (`nextWeekStart`), `agenda_next` among the tools (five), and `forgetDueScope` for `wipeAccount`. After the phase-2 review (same day): `fireReminder` delivers before it writes (a `deliver` callback from the sweep, under a 30 s item lock, and drops a stale `sec:due` entry), `reopen` also drops the inbox link (the `reopened` note keeps its id), a change of who owes an item drops the contact's number unless the owner gives the new one, `cleanPhone` keeps the international form only (`isWaNumber`), a date-only reminder is 08:00 local (`REMIND_DAY_AT`), and tool answers say when a reminder comes (`remindLine`) and where an item shows (`whereShown`) at the brief's local time. Since 2026-10-03 (phase 3 A1, rules 478-487): `calFor(scope, now)` reads the brand file, `sec:cal:{scope}` and `sec:away:{scope}` in one round and gives `{ zone, cal, away }` (`calendarOf` = `workCalendar(usableCalendar(stored))`: a stored value the calendar cannot be built from gives the country's defaults and logs only the error's name, ruling R13; `awayMapOf`: each entry's label and last away day, `endedAt` once it is past, ruling R3), and `agendaFor` (which also returns `cal` and `away`), `createItem`, `changeItem`, `mutateItem`, `defaultExpectBy`, `parseOwnerChange`, `nextWeekStart` and `snoozePreset` compute on it; `todoPage` (also hands the page the brand file, the Working days view `working` and the away hash `awayRows` its agenda was read with, so nothing is read twice), `isLocalDay`, `OWNER_ALIASES` (also what the away list refuses as a contact), the away list's errors (`not_contact`, `away_range`, `away_limit`, `bad_request`, `already_decided`, and `busy` while another Save of an away card runs), `backFingerprint`; `secretaryDaily` runs `cleanAway` next to the purge (a failure is logged by its name and retried the next day); `secretaryKeys` adds `sec:cal`, `sec:away`, each away proposal's record and outcome by the ids in `secawayprops:{scope}`, and that index. Reem phase 3 A2 (rules 490-509): `parseRepeat`, `settleRepeat` (the rule is set from the item's date and time; chat and MCP limits), the `skip` and `stop_repeat` changes, `spawnNext` (the next copy on every close path: complete, confirm, skip and the linked close; one claim `sec:spawn`, two writes, the closed copy first), `deleteNextCopy` (reopen; since 2026-10-05 a call of `removeBySystem`, the system's soft delete under the item lock, also used by a booking's cancel, whose `missing` hook marks the claim of an item not yet written; `createItem(..., {id})` writes at an id a booking's claim chose, and `reindexOpen` writes an open item's indexes again), the `repeat` and `stop_repeat` tool fields with the agent gate in `runSecretaryTool`, `todoPage(scope, {series})` (the series view), and `indexWrites(..., {kept})` for the series sets. |
| `secretary-keys.ts`, `secretary-zone.ts`, `secretary-input.ts`, `secretary-mutate.ts`, `secretary-store.ts`, `secretary-change.ts`, `secretary-read.ts`, `secretary-owner.ts`, `secretary-tools.ts` | The split of `secretary.ts` (2026-10-17, wave 2a, rules 2010-2015, `flows.md` section 82), a pure move behind the barrel above, in import order: `-keys` (`SEC_ID`, `SEC_KEYS`, per-scope limits; imports nothing local), `-zone` (`zoneFor`, `calFor`), `-input` (cleaning and parsing, `fail`), `-mutate` (`OWNER_ONLY`, `mutateItem`), `-store` (`getItem`, `createItem`, `withLock`, `listOpenItems`), `-change` (`changeItem`, `fireReminder`, `timedReminders`), `-read` (`agendaFor`, `todoPage`, `secretaryDaily`, purge), `-owner` (snooze presets, `parseOwnerChange`), `-tools` (`SEC_TOOL_DEFS`, `runSecretaryTool`). One-way graph, each under 450 lines |
| `secretary-agenda.ts` | Browser-safe (imports `./dates`, `./zones` and `./agents` only): the `SecItem` type and limits, local time in the brand's zone as a fixed offset (`zoneDay`, `zoneTime`, `addWorkingDays` skipping the zone's weekend, or with the brand's `CalData` every quiet day, `parseWhen`, `formatWhen` through `dateFormat`, `zoneTimeLabel`, `nowLine` for every agent's prompt), and the pure `computeAgenda` / `classify` (buckets overdue, today, confirm, chase, upcoming 7 days, waiting, later, undated, cold; snoozed only counted), `chaseAt` (the chase clock: due time, else `expectBy`, else two working days after creation; two working days after a follow-up), `alertStep` / `overdueAlerts` (days 0, 1, 3, 7), `compareItems`, `partyName`, `nextStepText`, `arCount` (an Arabic count with the noun in the form the number takes), `approvalsLine`, `noteText` (the notes code writes, `not_yet`, `chased`, `reminded` and `reopened`, in the reader's language), `briefClock` (the morning brief's local time in a zone: `DAILY_CRON_UTC_HOUR` 05:00 UTC plus the offset, 08:00 or 09:00), `linkFactText` (the checked fact about a linked inbox task, never the agent's words), `briefAgenda`. A proposal is classed before a snooze, so it always shows under "needs your confirmation". Since 2026-10-03 (phase 3 A1, rules 471-480): `CalData` and `QuietWhy` (the brand's work calendar as plain data, built on the server by `secretary-calendar.ts`; `defaultCal(zone)`, the zone's weekend and nothing else, for every caller without one), `AwayMap`, `DEFAULT_RAMADAN_HOURS` (10:00-15:00), `isQuietDay`, `inRamadanWindow` and `outsideRamadanHours` (ruling R7), `firstWorkingMoment`, `partyKey` (one key per contact however the name is typed: cleaned as `cleanLine` does, invisible characters out, NFKC, lower case, tashkeel out, alef, taa marbuta and yaa folded; ruling R2), `chaseMoment(item, now, cal, away)` (the one chase rule, spec §2.1: from `chaseAt` with the calendar; an agent's item never moved; an away contact from `briefAt` after the absence, ruling R10; quiet days to `briefAt` on the next working day; Ramadan's off-hours to the next window start; and while today is not a working moment a chaseable debt still waits), `classify` and `computeAgenda` with `{cal, away}` (an item someone else owes is `waiting` while its moment is ahead, and carries `moment`), `workingDaysBetween`, `heldForRamadanToday` (ruling R18; asked about contacts' debts only, since agents keep no Ramadan hours, ruling R16), `overdueAlerts(entries, now, zone, cal)` (steps 0, 1, 3, 7: calendar days for the owner's and agents' items, working days from the anchor for a contact's, `send: false` on a quiet day), `nextStepParts` and `nextStepText` (the reason line while the rule holds an item, ruling R9), `briefAgenda` with `chaseAfter`, and `nowLine(clock, zone, weekend)` naming the week start from the brand's weekend. Reem phase 3 A2 (2026-10-03): `SecRepeat` (the rule: `every` day, workday, week, month, year, hmonth, hyear; `interval`, `anchor`, `weekStart`, `time`, `weekdays`, `monthDay`, `remindBefore`, `until`, `count`), the item's `repeat`, `series {id, n}`, `nextId` and `closedBy`, the `two_open` note, `SKIP_RESULT` (the constant "skipped", shown as a word through `resultText(result, lang)`), `gregorianNext` and `repeatText`. |
| `secretary-remind.ts` | Reem's timed reminders (2026-09-30), server only: `sweepReminders(now, {tick, deadline})` reads up to 200 scopes from `sec:duescopes` whose earliest reminder is due and groups them by account, eight accounts at a time; per account one read of the user and one of the brand list: a suspended account's scopes, and those of a brand that is not active (archived), wait 6 hours with their reminders still due. Per scope (each isolated, `Promise.allSettled`; the zone falls back to Riyadh) it reads up to 50 due ids from `sec:due:{scope}` and leases each with `SET NX sec:firing:{scope}:{id}` (`REMIND_LEASE_S`, 330 s, released when the attempt ends), then `fireReminder` in `secretary.ts` fires it under the item lock: the bell entry first (`pushNotifs`, fingerprint `todo-remind:{scope}:{id}:{remindAt ms}`), the email only when that push was new and `RESEND_API_KEY` is set and the preference is not `none` (multipart since 2026-10-08: the same text, and an HTML `notice` from `src/emails/` with the bell's title and body and a button to `/{lang}/app/todo`; rules 685-687), then the item (a `reminded` note, `remindAt` cleared, audit `remind` by `cron`). The id stays in `sec:due` until that write clears it, so a failed or killed run leaves it due and the next sweep retries it, with no second bell entry or email (late, never lost). No account starts and no lease is taken after the deadline (`SWEEP_BUDGET_TICK_MS` 40 s, `SWEEP_BUDGET_DAILY_MS` 60 s). The scope is then rescored (and re-read, so a reminder added meanwhile is never dropped; a failure is logged). Writes `sec:tick:last` when `tick` is set: every authenticated call of the tick route, QStash or the cron bearer (rule 1432; it was QStash only, and on thakeen.com the tick is a bearer call from hPanel, so the marker was never written); never for the daily fallback. |
| `secretary-walk.ts` | "Walk me through" (2026-09-30), server only: `walkNext(scope, now, {viewer, lang})` builds one card in code from the agenda (confirm, overdue, today, chase) and then the approvals inbox, oldest first: skipped cards leave before the cut to 50, past 50 candidates the oldest are chosen by the inbox list's order (`tasks:{scope}`, ids only), then one MGET; `left` counts every candidate. A confirm card carries the agent's `claim` (marked unchecked) and the app's `fact` about the link; an approval card the draft's first 500 characters (`excerpt`) and, when the brand guard holds it (`gateTask`, `explain`), why (`held`) and no Approve. `WALK_TABLE` gives each card's actions by kind and next step (held: open only), cut to what the viewer may do (`WALK_NEEDS`: `todo.close`, `todo.write`, `publish`, `read`); `walkSkip` hides a card for 3 hours in `sec:walk:{scope}`; `walkToolLine` is the chat and MCP `agenda_next` answer (the owner's actions, and the snooze and follow-up the agent may do with `todo_update` when the owner asks). |
| `secretary-evening.ts` | The evening wrap (2026-09-30), server only: `buildWrap` runs the agenda first (`agendaFor`, which closes approved linked items), then reads stored facts only (done today with results, today's approve, publish and reject decisions, what fell overdue since the previous evening's wrap time, `previousEveningAt`, the first three of the next working morning's overdue, today, confirm and chase; since 2026-10-03 both read the brand's work calendar and away map from `agendaFor`: a debt someone else owes "fell overdue" when its chase moment's anchor passed, ruling R12, and the next working morning is `firstWorkingMoment(09:00 tomorrow)`, named in the line as its day and time, `Wrap.tomorrowAt`, ruling R14); `eveningWrap(user)` sends it once per scope and local day (`sec:wrap:{scope}:{day}` claimed with SET NX, released when the notification cannot be written) as one `todo` notification, titled with the brand's name when the account has more than one active brand, and, with preference `all`, one email (multipart since 2026-10-08: the text, and an HTML `notice` with a button to `/{lang}/app/todo`); silent when all four parts are empty, off when follow-ups are off. `eveningAt` is 18:00 local, 21:30 in Ramadan (`isRamadan(now, zone)`, `activeSeasons` on the zone's own calendar day since 2026-10-03); `zonesDue(now)` names the zones whose time passed within the last 16 minutes. |
| `secretary-handoff.ts` | Agents' handoff notes (2026-09-30; not Majed's handoffs, which are `handoffs.ts`), server only: the chat tool `handoff_note {to, kind, body}` (refused over MCP), notes in the capped list `notes:{scope}` (30), `handoffNotesFor(scope, agent)` (the receiver's last 5 of 7 days; Reem also gets those to the owner) and `handoffBlock` (an `asData` block `<handoff_notes>` in the uncached prompt block). A `blocked` note opens a Reem item (source `agent`, `handoffFrom`; to the owner it is due all day today in the brand's zone), or, under one lock per scope, adds its body as a note to an open item the same agent opened for the same party that says the same thing (`sameBlock`: equal after `normForMatch`, or both at least 3 words, `similarity` >= 0.6 and at most 2 words different, `symmetricWordDiff`); at most 20 blocked notes a day per account. The answer is built from the stored item (`whereShown`). |
| `secretary-cal-link.ts`, `secretary-meetings.ts` | Reem B3 (2026-10-05, rules 917-919 and 921), server only: the store of the brand's linked calendar (`CalLink`, `CalTokens`, `CalEvents`, `getLink`, `putLink`, `sealTokens` / `openTokens`, `dropLink`, `scheduleSync`, `freshMeetings`, `meetingsNow`, `CAL_STALE_MS`) and the pure meeting helpers (`Meeting`, `meetingTitle`, `todayMeetings`, the quiet hold `quietUntil` with `QUIET_CAP_MS` read by the reminder sweep, and `freeSlots` behind Reem's read-only `free_slots` tool, which `secretary.ts` loads on demand because these modules import it). `secretary-gcal.ts` (provider side): the connect proof and step (`connectProof`, `connectCalendar`), the token refresh (`freshAccess`), the proof read again on every save (`reproof`), the account's own calendars (`listOwnCalendars`) the event fetch (`fetchEvents`), the lost-link step (`loseLink`: state `reconnect` with a reason, events deleted, scope out of the queue, `cal_lost` audit, and for a lost token one in-app notice) and the calendar lock (`withCalLock`: owned, 45 s, 60 tries 50 ms apart, ruling R8; the refresh is raced against 8 s), the sync (`sweepCalendars(now, deadline)`: up to 50 due scopes from `sec:cal:due`, 8 at a time, each `syncOne` under that lock; the secretary tick answers `calendars: {scopes, synced, lost, retried, deferred}`) and `revokeCalendarLinks(email)` (password reset and account wipe: drops every brand's link of an owner, calls no provider); `secretary-cal-routes.ts`: the rate limit, the refusal answer and the link's view the two calendar-link routes share. The agenda block follows in a later task |
| `secretary-calendar.ts` | The brand's work calendar (2026-10-03, Reem phase 3 A1, rules 472-474 and 484), server only: `SecCalendar` (the Working days settings in `sec:cal:{scope}`: weekend, Ramadan hours, `hijriFix` per Hijri year, holidays `add` / `remove`, `updatedAt`, `updatedBy`); `COUNTRY_CAL` (per country, its official private-sector Eid spans and other holidays with their Arabic and English names, the rule for days in lieu and the source each row was checked against on 2026-10-02; Oman's announced days per Gregorian year, `omanYear`, which replace the decree's non-Eid dates that year and are updated each January, `flows.md` 39) and `countryOf(zone)`; `workCalendar(cal, zone, now)` (the `CalData` the agenda reads: the quiet days from today-30 to today+400 with their reasons, weekend days left out, the Ramadan days, the Ramadan hours, `briefAt`; Hijri dates only through `hijriDate`, so it builds no date formatter of its own; memoised in an LRU of 200 keyed by zone, local day and a hash of the settings without `updatedAt` / `updatedBy`, and frozen); Saudi Arabia's and the UAE's Eid al-Fitr starts the day before the effective 1 Shawwal when Ramadan, counted from its Umm al-Qura start, ran 30 days or more, else on it, and no corrected Ramadan runs past 30 days (ruling R26); `upcomingQuiet` (the next 365 days and the next Ramadan); `eidDays` (this and next Hijri year's Eid: Umm al-Qura's month start, the one in force, the `hijriFix` value each "a day earlier / later" button saves, the holiday's name and its days off); `calendarView` (what `GET` / `PUT /api/secretary/calendar` and the page show, `effective` with the zone and its default weekend, ruling R23); `parseCalendar` (field by field, a field it does not know refused; stale values dropped, not refused, ruling R8; a removal already stored kept though no default produces it, ruling R28; `hijriFix` takes `shawwal1` and `dhulhijja1` only, ruling R30); stored Ramadan hours are checked again when the calendar is built and read as the default when they are not valid windows; `namesSharjah` (the page's hint only). Reem phase 3 A2: `nextOccurrence(rule, prevDue, now, zone, cal, n)` gives the next copy's date (Gregorian rules through `gregorianNext`, Hijri ones on the calendar with `hijriFix`), or null when `count`, `until` or the bounded search ends the series. |
| `secretary-away.ts` | Contacts who are away (2026-10-03, Reem phase 3 A1, rules 481-486), server only: `sec:away:{scope}`, one field per contact (`partyKey(label)`) holding `{label, until, note?, by, via, at}`. `parseAway` (a contact only: `not_contact` for an agent id or an owner alias by its key, however typed; `until` from today to today + 366 in the brand's zone, `away_range`; a note of 200 after `cleanLine`; a key the store cannot hold, `__proto__`, `constructor` or `prototype`, is `bad_request`, ruling R22), `setAway` and `endAway` (under `withLock(scope, "away")`, one `away` or `away_end` audit line each, never the note; ending is idempotent and keeps the entry with `until` yesterday for the back alert, `remove: true` deletes it, ruling R21; at most 200 fields, ended ones included, `away_limit`), `listAway` (every stored entry, `ended` once past, ruling R20; `awayListOf` the same for a hash already read), `AWAY_PROPOSAL_ID` (an away card's id, the one regex the proposals route checks), `cleanAway` (the daily clean-up from `secretaryDaily`: entries ended over `AWAY_KEEP_DAYS`, 30, days ago, ruling R19, and values that are not entries, every HDEL in one round under the lock, one `away_clean` line). Reem's `contact_away` cards (rules 485-486): `proposeAway` (checked like `setAway` or `endAway`, the away list and open items read in one round, the id added to `secawayprops:{scope}` first, then the record `secawayprop:{scope}:{id}` for 7 days; nothing on the list), `awayProposalStates` (one MGET for a thread's cards), `decideAwayProposal` (a person's Save or Dismiss, once: the outcome `secawayprop-done:{scope}:{id}` is claimed with SET NX; Save runs `setAway` / `endAway` again with every check and ends its audit line "proposed in Reem's chat ({id})"; a refused Save frees the claim). Free: no credits, no model call. |
| `secretary-series.ts` | The closed copies of recurring series (2026-10-03, Reem phase 3 A2, rules 502-505), server only: `warnSeries` (`sec:sdone` to `sec:spurge`, one notification per brand and local day naming at most 5 series, a 30-day marker `sec:swarn:{scope}:{seriesId}` per series; its email, unless the preference is `none`, is multipart since 2026-10-08: the text, and an HTML `notice` with a button to `/{lang}/app/todo?series={seriesId}`), `purgeSeries` (deletes due copies of `sec:spurge` under each item lock after reading them again), `requeueSeries` (puts back closed copies of unkept series in neither window), `setSeriesKeep` (`sec:serieskeep`, at most 500, `keep_limit`), `seriesRows` (one series, newest first) and `seriesDaily`, which `secretaryDaily` runs for active brands only |
| `feed-token.ts` | The private calendar link's token and records (2026-10-05, Reem phase 3 B1, rules 530-532, 582-583), server only; imports `./db`, `./parallel`, `./keys` and `node:crypto` only, so `team.ts` and `account.ts` can revoke without a cycle: `FEED_TOKEN` (the 43-character base64url shape), `FEED_KEYS` (`rec`, `of`), `hashFeedToken` (SHA-256 hex), `createFeed(who, scope, lang)` (since multi-workspace M1 `who` is a `LinkOwner` `{pid, aid, login, home}`: under `lock:feed:{pid}:{aid}` and, for the home account, `lock:feed:{login}` inside it; a plain login keeps the pre-M1 form; returns the plain token once, or `"busy"`), `linkIds` and `linkLive` (shared with `booking-token.ts`), `resolveFeed(token)` (the shape check comes before any read; then the pointers in one round: a record with `pid` is live while `sec:feedof:{pid}:{aid}` names it and, for a home record, `sec:feedof:{login}` too; a record made before M1 while the legacy pointer names it), `getFeedStatus`, `revokeFeed` (waits for the lock 20 times, then revokes anyway) and `revokeFeeds`. Only the hash is stored. Called by `api/secretary/feed`, `team.ts` (`dropMember`, `wipeMemberships`), `account.ts` (`wipeAccount`), `api/account`, `api/auth/reset` and `api/admin/users/[email]` |
| `secretary-feed.ts` | The calendar file of one brand's open dated to-dos (2026-10-05, Reem phase 3 B1, rules 534-539, 577-578), server only: `feedItems(scope, now)` (`listOpenItems` filtered to due from 14 days before to 365 days after, nearest first, at most 500), `buildIcs(items, {lang, zone, origin, now})` (plain events, UTC timed items with a 30-minute end, all-day items as a date in the brand's zone, `UID` `{id}@thakeen`, `SEQUENCE` = `rev`, no `RRULE` or `VALARM`; the who-acts words are `appDict[lang].todo.feed`), `icsEscape` and `icsFold` (RFC 5545 escaping after control and direction-override characters are removed; 75-octet folding that never splits a UTF-8 character; CRLF) `linkOwner(u)` (the `LinkOwner` of the session: person, active account, acting address, home; multi-workspace M1), `loginHolds(rec)` (for a record with `pid`: the scope's account is `aid`, then person, account, membership and brands in one round, rules 2119 and 2131; a record without `pid`: R1's check, below) and `feedHolds(rec)`, its name for the feed, the session-free half of `resolveSession`, run on every fetch (the login exists and is active, the owner is not suspended, a member is on the roster and accepted after the owner's record, the scope is one of the owner's active brands, the record is not older than the login's life or than its last password event: the owner's `sessionsAfter`, and for a member also the member's `sessionsAfter` and the owner's `teamSessionsAfter`) and `isSnoozed` (from `secretary-agenda.ts`) leaves snoozed items out. Called by `api/secretary/feed/[token]` |
| `booking-token.ts` | The booking link's token and records (2026-10-05, Reem phase 3 B2a, rules 589 and 598), server only; imports `./db`, `./parallel`, `./keys`, `./feed-token` (`linkIds`, `linkLive`, `LinkOwner`) and `node:crypto` only, so `team.ts` and `account.ts` can revoke without a cycle: `BOOK_TOKEN`, `BOOK_PROVIDERS`, `BOOK_KEYS`, `hashBookToken`, `bookingKey(hash)` (the derived webhook key), `createBook`, `resolveBook`, `getBookStatus`, `revokeBook`, `revokeBooks` (per `(pid, aid, provider)` since multi-workspace M1, as `feed-token.ts`; a plain login keeps the pre-M1 form). Used by `api/secretary/booking/[token]` (the webhook), `api/secretary/booking` (the owner's management) and, to revoke, by `team.ts`, `account.ts`, `api/auth/reset`, `api/account` and `api/admin/users/[email]`; `roles.ts` names it in the `booking.manage` comment only |
| `secretary-booking.ts` | Bookings in from Cal.com and Calendly (2026-10-05, Reem phase 3 B2a, rules 586-593 and 597): the two webhook signature checks and the two pure parsers to a neutral `Parsed` shape, and `applyBooking(rec, parsed, hash)`, which acts in the link record's scope only: create (claim `sec:book:ext` with SET NX, the link's cap `book:new:{hash}`, then `createItem` as the rule with source `booking`), move (the item named by the previous id, then by this one; `changeItem` as the rule), cancel (`removeBySystem`, restorable 30 days; a claim whose item was never written is marked `cancelled` so its creation never happens). The claim names a pre-allocated item id and `createItem(..., {id})` writes the item there, so a retry finishes a creation instead of repeating it. It stores the guest's name, the event-type name and the times only. Returns `created`, `moved`, `removed`, `noop` or `busy` (a lock or the store unavailable: 503 to the provider). Also `calendlySubscribe(pat, url, signingKey)`, the owner's one-time Calendly setup (two calls through `safeFetch`, `httpsOnly`, to `https://api.calendly.com`; the token is never stored; a typed `calendly_plan` or `calendly_rejected`, which the route answers as 422). Used by `api/secretary/booking/[token]` and `api/secretary/booking`. |
| `fill.ts` | Import-free (2026-09-30): `fill(template, vars)` fills `{key}` slots in one pass with a function replacer, so a title or name someone typed (with `$'`, `$&` or `{title}` in it) is inserted as typed. Used by the to-do page (the WhatsApp message, the reminder line, the agent badge) and the walk card. |
| `qstash.ts` | `verifyQstash(signature, rawBody, {path})` (2026-09-30): the `Upstash-Signature` JWT checked with `node:crypto` only: HS256 with `QSTASH_CURRENT_SIGNING_KEY` or `QSTASH_NEXT_SIGNING_KEY` (constant-time), `iss` `Upstash`, `sub` ending with the path, `nbf`/`exp` with 30 s leeway, `body` the base64url SHA-256 of the raw body. Anything else is false. |
| `zones.ts` | Import-free, shared with the browser: the six Gulf zones a brand may keep its list in (`ZONES`: offset, weekend, city name), `isZone` (the allow-list), `zoneOf(brand)` (the stored zone, else the city's, else the dialect's, else Riyadh), `weekStart(zone)` (the first working day of the zone's own weekend), `zoneUtcLabel`; since 2026-10-03 `weekStartDay(weekend)` (the day after the last day of a brand's own weekend: Friday and Saturday give Sunday, Saturday and Sunday Monday, Friday alone Saturday, none Sunday) and `WEEKDAY_NAMES`. Since 2026-10-12 (rule 1472) also `currencyOf(brand)`: the currency of the brand's zone (SAR, KWD, QAR, BHD, AED, OMR), SAR by default; `taskSpec(type, brand)` in `noor.ts` fills the specs' `{CURRENCY}` with it (rule 1473). |
| `lifecycle.ts` | Lifecycle emails (day 1, 2, 3, 10), once per user, only while `mailReady()` (since 2026-10-01; a key without `MAIL_FROM` sends nothing and takes no marker). Multipart since 2026-10-08: the plain-text body as before, and `lifecycleHtml` puts the same subject and paragraphs into the HTML `notice` (`src/emails/`), turning the paragraph that is only the app's address into the button (the subscription page for the day 2 and day 3 trial notes); every other address stays text (rules 685, 687). |
| `notify.ts` | `sendEmail(to, subject, text, html?)` via Resend (since 2026-09-30 it gives up after `EMAIL_TIMEOUT_MS`, 10 s, and answers false; since 2026-10-08 an optional HTML part from `src/emails/`, sent only when given and always with the text, so a templated message is multipart and an untemplated one is text only with no `html` key at all, rule 689; Resend click tracking must stay off for the sending domain, because the reset and join links carry their token in the fragment, rule 546); `sendOnce(email, key, subject, text, html?)` for named one-time emails (it forwards the HTML part) (since 2026-10-01, replacing `once()`): a 120 s claim (`notifying:`, SET NX with an owner token), the send, then the 400-day `notified:` marker only when Resend accepted it; the claim is released either way, so a refused send is retried on the next run. The marker is a dated record (`{ sent }`) since 2026-10-01; a bare marker from the old claim-before-send guard proves no delivery (MAIL_FROM was never set while it ran), so when `mailReady()` and the message is due it is cleared once per (account, message) under the claim, behind the 400-day `notifyrepair:` flag, and the message goes out (founder decision 2026-10-01); a `{ sent }` marker is never cleared. Only old markers written before `REPAIR_BEFORE` (2026-10-01 17:00 UTC, when MAIL_FROM was still unset) are cleared, whatever the deploy order: the old guard wrote every marker with a 400-day expiry, so `writtenBeforeRepairCutoff` reads the write time from the remaining TTL (`db.ttl`; no expiry counts as older), and a marker the old guard wrote after MAIL_FROM went live, for a send that succeeded, is never cleared. |
| `mail-provider.ts` | Where an email goes (2026-10-16, rules 1962-1963, branch only). `MailProvider` with two adapters: `resendProvider(key)` (reads the refusal from the `{ data, error }` Resend resolves with) and `consoleProvider` (off production with no `RESEND_API_KEY`: keeps the last 20 messages in memory, `consoleOutbox()`, writes one debug line with the recipient's domain and sizes only, answers not delivered). `mailProvider()` picks one at call time; a production deploy without a key has none. `sendEmail` in `notify.ts` now also takes `{ to, subject, text, template, context, locale }` and renders the HTML part with `renderEmail` over `EMAIL_TEMPLATES` (`src/emails/index.ts`); the positional form stays for `auth/forgot` (owned elsewhere), the support forward and the staff alert |
| `log.ts` | The logger (2026-10-16, rules 1970-1974, branch only): `log.debug/info/warn/error(message, fields?)` over `console`, `log.with(base)`, `withRequest(req)` (adds `rid`). `scrub` redacts secret-named fields at any depth, credential-looking values (Bearer, `sk-`/`re_`/`whsec_` keys, JWTs, long hex or base64 runs), turns an `Error` into its `errorTag` (never the message) and caps strings at 300 characters; debug lines are dropped on a production deploy (`isProductionDeploy`). Every `console.*` in `src/lib` goes through it except the import-free `origin.ts` |
| `request-id.ts` | Import-free (the proxy loads it, rule 1975): `REQUEST_ID_HEADER` (`x-request-id`), `REQUEST_ID_SHAPE` and `requestId(headers)`. `src/proxy.ts` keeps a well-formed id it was given or makes a UUID, passes it to the route as a request header and returns it on the response |
| `media-catalog.ts` | Import-free (pinned by `tests/client-bundle.test.ts`): the 11 media models with labels, hints and credit prices (`MEDIA_MODELS`), `DEFAULT_IMAGE_MODEL`, `MEDIA_MAX_AGE_MS`, `TRIAL_VIDEO_MODELS`, `aspectFor`, `hasArtwork`, `MEDIA_HOSTS`, `ourMediaName`, `isOurMediaUrl` (the disk store, or a legacy Blob copy during the expand release, rule 1759), `isOurOutgoingMediaUrl` (the same, or a disk URL with exactly `exp` and `sig`), `mediaImageFor`, and `plural` (fills the inbox's credit strings: "1 credit", "12 credits"). Read by the inbox and the server alike. |
| `media/types.ts` | Contracts between the engine, the adapters, the store and the routes (`MediaAdapter`, `MediaJob`, `MediaDeps`, `StartResult`), plus the two error classes whose messages are fixed codes (`ProviderError`, `StoreError`). |
| `media/keys.ts` | Every media Redis key (`mk`), `JOB_ID` and `PROVIDER_TASK_ID` formats, `deadlineFor`, `budget`, `abortAfter`. |
| `media/config.ts` | Which providers are configured (`providerConfigured`, `blobConfigured`), `modelsSwitchedOn` (`MEDIA_MODELS_ON`), `offModels` (`media:off`), `modelAvailable`, `availableModels`, the cost tables `COST_USD` and `EXPECTED_KIE_CREDITS`, and `callbackUrl`. |
| `media/fal.ts` | `falAdapter`: FLUX schnell, synchronous, `img-fast` only. |
| `media/kie.ts` | `kieAdapter` (one request body per model in `KIE_MODELS`, `:50`), `verifyKieSignature`, `kieTaskIdFrom`. |
| `media/higgsfield.ts` | `higgsfieldAdapter` (one endpoint and body per model in `HF_MODELS`), `hfRequestIdFrom`. |
| `media/store.ts` | The private media store (rules 1750-1763): `copyToStore` (download through `safeFetch` with `httpsOnly`, cap, sniff, JPEG) and `writeToStore` (size cap, quota and free-space guard, index `mfile:{scope}:{name}` plus `mfiles:{scope}` first, then `storage().put`), `listLibrary` / `deleteLibraryFile`, `storeHasRoom` / `storeReady`, `forgetScopeMedia` (account deletion) and `probeStore` (usage and limits plus the adapter's probe). Imports no `node:fs`: the bytes go through the storage adapter (rule 2058). |
| `media/storage.ts` | The storage adapter seam (rules 2058-2063, flows section 85): the `StorageAdapter` type (`configured`, `put`, `get`, `stream`, `head`, `delete`, `url`, `freeBytes`, `probe`) and `storage()`, the one place the backend is chosen. An adapter sees a name that passed `MEDIA_NAME` and bytes, never a scope. |
| `media/disk.ts` | `diskStorage`, the only `StorageAdapter`: `MEDIA_DIR/{2 hex}/{name}` with owner-only modes, `put` through `.tmp/{name}.part` opened `wx`, fsynced and hard-linked (never overwrites), `mediaPath`, `statfs` free space and the probe with its build sentinel (rule 1762). The future disk + Drive archive backend (draft PR #320) would be a second adapter. |
| `media/share.ts` | `shareUrl` / `shareValid`: the 24-hour signed `/m/{name}?exp=&sig=` link minted only when a file is handed to Meta, kie, Higgsfield, Composio or WordPress (rule 1755); key `HMAC(CONNECT_SECRET, "media-share:v1")`, compared with `sameSecret`. |
| `media/prompts.ts` | `imagePrompt` / `videoPrompt` (one Sonnet call, 20 s, no retries) and the fixed templates `fixedImagePrompt` / `fixedVideoPrompt` used while Claude is paused or on any failure; the prompt call is metered as `image_prompt` for the agent that owns the item (`agentForTask`). |
| `media/jobs.ts` | The job engine: `startMedia`, `advance`, `sweepMedia`, `maybeSweep`, `jobForHook`, `getJob`, `publicJob`, `setOff`; charge, refund exactly once (`refundOnce`, `:510`), breaker, spend counters, owner notices. Every task write goes through `updateTask` (the start's pointer `:190`, its cleanup `:247,257`, finalize `:470`, fail `:555`); finalize writes the task before the job and records the job in `task.mediaApplied` (`withResult`, `:413`). Imports no adapter (providers arrive as `MediaDeps`). Activity rows: `image_started` beside the charge, `cap_reached` / `no_credits` at the pre-check and ledger refusals (`StartInput.by` and `from`), and `render_done` / `render_failed` (with `refunded`) at the terminal writes, under the job's stored `scope` and `spend.agent`, only while `sameAccount` holds (`terminalRow`). |
| `media/registry.ts` | `mediaDeps()`: the real adapters (each `submit` signs a disk-store reference image at the moment it is sent) and `copyToStore` / `storeReady` handed to the engine. |
| `media/start.ts` | `renderImage` / `renderVideo` (the validation shared by both routes and the chat tool), the uncharged placeholder card (`placeholder`, `:47`: written through `updateTask` only while the item has no artwork; an item that has a generated image keeps it and the answer is 409 `model_unavailable`, `:68`), `mediaResponse` (the HTTP contract the inbox reads; `media_failed` without a refunded balance goes out as `media_pending`, `:126`), `startThrew`, `errCode`. The render's `by` is `opts.by` (chat passes its turn's) or `byOf(u)`, and `from` app or chat, for the activity rows. |
| `metered.ts` | Since 2026-10-10 (rules 980-986, flows section 61): `metered(stream, {email, kind, agent?, requested?})` awaits every model stream, meters the reply with `meterBilled` (each `usage.iterations` entry at its own model), and on a failure meters what the stream had received (`partialUsage`: the snapshot's input and cache, output raised to `streamedTokens`) before rethrowing. The only caller of `finalMessage()`; no `messages.create` in `src`. |
| `usage.ts` | Model token and cost metering per account per month, per kind and (since 2026-09-29) per agent. `usageMonthKeys` lists every counter of one month and is what `wipeAccount` deletes. Since 2026-10-01 also the chat message counters, under the same month prefix so that list covers them: `chatMsgKey` (`:chat_msgs`, messages inside the allowance) and `chatOverKey` (`:chat_over`, messages continued on credits), and `chatTrialKey` (`chatm:{email}:trial`, a trial's messages, once for the whole trial); `getUsage` returns this month's `chatMessages` and `chatOverflow`, while `byKind.chat` stays the chat's model calls and their cost. Since 2026-10-10 (rule 970) `costUsd` and `hasRate` read the rate from the model catalog (`models/catalog.ts`) only: the old `RATES` table is gone, `hasRate` is false for any other id, and `costUsd` prices an id outside the catalog (a retired Opus 5 or Sonnet 5 in a response included) at the highest catalog rate of each of the four buckets, so a call is never metered below what the dearest catalog model would cost. The catalog rates are Anthropic's list prices, checked on 2026-10-10 against the Console token export of 2026-09-11 to 2026-10-10 (within $0.05 of the credits spent). Since 2026-10-09 (Task 8) `meter(email, model, kind, usage?, agent?, provider?)` also counts every call, in the GLOBAL `_all` month only (no per-account copy, so `wipeAccount` has nothing to miss and `usageMonthKeys` does not list them), under `model:{modelKey}` (the catalog id, or `other` for any id outside the catalog, so a hostile or dated id never becomes part of a key) and `provider:{anthropic|straitly}` (the `provider` argument, else `effectiveProvider()` from `model.ts`: `straitly` only with `LLM_PROVIDER=straitly` and `STRAITLY_API_KEY`, the decision `anthropic()` makes; any other value is ignored), each with `:calls` and `:usd_micro` (the same micro-dollars as every other counter; `costUsd` and the rates are unchanged). Since 2026-10-10 `meterBilled(email, model, kind, usage, agent?, requested?)` meters a reply's `usage.iterations` per model (`billedParts`): the account, kind, agent and provider counters count one call, each model counter its own attempt. `noteRejectedPick(purpose, reason)` counts a customer pick the router refused under `pickreject:{chat|generate}:{reason}` (both checked against fixed lists; best effort, one fixed log line on a store error). `getUsage("_all")` returns `models`, `providers`, `fallbacks` and `rejectedPicks` (only entries with a count, read in one `mget`); `getUsage(email)` returns them empty with no extra read. Counted from the release that added them: earlier months read as zero, nothing is backfilled. |
| `usage-view.ts` | Import-free (2026-10-01, rule 315): `runway({used, left, periodStart, resetAt, trialEnds?, now?})` projects the average daily pace since `periodStart` (at least one day) to the period's end (`unused`, `out`, `lasts`, or `runs_out` with a `YYYY-MM-DD` date; null when the dates cannot be read), `runwayLine` words it in Arabic or English (for messages, what happens after: a pause until the 1st, continuing on credits, or a trial's subscription), and `monthStartUtc` / `paceStart` (the later of the month's start and the account's creation). Read by the profile panel and the billing page; no locale date formatter. |
| `track.ts` | Product analytics counters and active-user sets in Redis. `userEvents` reads a user's eleven counters in one MGET (rule 1433). |
| `stats.ts` | Workspace KPIs from stored tasks (credits count `mediaCredits`, `videos` counts items with a clip); `statsFor` gives Dana's tasks and Majed's strategy review the WORKSPACE DATA plus STORE DATA (`salesDataBlock` in `store-sales.ts`) when a store was synced (and SITE, SOCIAL and AD DATA when read), Razan's cash forecast the STORE DATA alone, and cart recovery the CART DATA. `statsText` says sales, traffic or ad spend are missing only when STORE, SITE or AD DATA is absent (rule 730). Dana's tasks and Majed's review end with NOT READ (`notReadBlock` in `not-read.ts`, read in the same `Promise.all`, rule 738). |
| `admin-stats.ts` | Founder overview (model cost plus render cost from `renderSpend`) and users CSV. Since 2026-09-30 MRR is the sum of `priceOf(u)` over accounts that are not suspended (the list price, or an Agency deal's), `paying` skips suspended accounts, and the CSV columns are `monthly_price_sar` (`priceOf`), `monthly_credits` (`allowanceOf`), `chat_allowance` (`chatAllowanceOf`, since 2026-10-01: the founder override, else the plan's messages), `seats` (`seatLimit`) and `deal` (`credits@priceSar`), every cell through `csvSafe`. Since 2026-10-09 `Overview.modelUsage` carries this month's `getUsage("_all")` per-model and per-provider calls and USD, the fallback count and the refused picks (founder only: the same `GET /api/admin/overview`, `isAdmin`), shown in the Billing tab. Since 2026-10-12 (rules 1474, 1475) `overview()` also returns `stalled` from the pure `stalledSignups`: accounts at least 24 h old, not suspended, with no brand file, newest 50 and the count, each with `email_unverified` or `no_brand`. |
| `referral.ts` | Deterministic referral codes; +50 to the new account at signup, +50 to the referrer at the friend's first payment (`rewardReferrer`, called by `recordPayment` in `partners.ts`; once per referred account, at most 10 per referrer per Riyadh month), `referralKeys` (the referrer-side keys `wipeAccount` deletes). |
| `safe-next.ts` | Pure, client-safe (2026-10-05, rules 543-545, 694): `safeNext` keeps a `?next=` only when it is a same-site path (no control character, no backslash, one leading `/`, same origin once parsed) and, since 2026-10-08, answers the normalised path, query and fragment (dot segments resolved), refusing a value that normalises to `//host`; `%2F` is not decoded. `safeHref` is the same rule for links in rendered Markdown (`md.ts`), checked on the href after decoding its HTML entities and escaped again. Used by `AuthForm.tsx` and `md.ts`. |
| `verify-email.ts` | Email verification (2026-10-08, rules 757-769, `flows.md` 53): `VERIFY` (900 s code life, 5 attempts, 60 s gap, 5 an hour, 10 a day, 20 an hour per IP, 3 moves a day); `issueVerifyCode` (6 digits from `randomInt`, stored as a SHA-256 hash salted with the address in `verify:code:{email}` `{hash, sentAt}`, replacing any older code and its counter), `checkVerifyCode` (the attempt taken with `INCRBY verify:tries:{email}` before the `sameSecret` comparison), `markVerified` (sets `emailVerifiedAt` under `withUserLock`, deletes code and counter), `sendVerifyCode` (mails through `sendEmail` in the account's language; drops a code that did not go out unless `devCodeExposed()`), `verifySendRefusal` (503 `mail_unavailable`, then the `verify:gap:`, `verify:hour:`, `verify:day:` and `verify:ip:` buckets, `retryLater`), `countVerifySend` (signup's first code, once its mail went out, counts in the address buckets), `devCodeExposed` (`APP_ENV=test` and not `isProductionDeploy`, rule 766) and `moveUnverifiedAccount` (change a mistyped address of a gated, unverified signup under both login locks and the user lock, then `wipeAccount(old)`). Server-only; the browser half is the pure `verify-client.ts` (`typedDigits`, `retryWait`, `waitLabel`). |
| `task-version.ts` | Pure, browser-safe (2026-10-05, rules 560-564): `taskVersion(task)` is the SHA-256 hex of `[title, editedBody ?? body, generated imageUrl]`, written out in code so one synchronous function runs in the browser, in `updateTask`'s change and in `publishOnce`'s check. Approving and publishing carry it, and the server compares it with the stored item. |
| `mcp.ts` | Personal MCP tokens and `userFromBearer` (owner accounts only, actor kind `token`; a token naming a member login is refused). |
| `external-drafts.ts` | MCP `save_draft` (2026-09-29): `parseDraft` types and bounds a draft the owner's own assistant wrote (type from `TASK_TYPES`, body up to 20,000 characters, title 200, notes 500, channel a short `[a-z0-9_-]` name; control and direction-override characters stripped; too long is refused, not cut), and `saveExternalDraft` saves it as one `pending` task with `source:"external"` in `sc(u)` after `draftAllowed` and the brand gate. Free: no credits, no model call, no judge. Action `drafts` in `MCP_TOOL_ACTION`. Saved together with one `external_draft` activity row (no agent, `by` the token, via `mcp`, the title as the note). |
| `oauth.ts` | Minimal OAuth 2.1 server: metadata, dynamic client registration, codes, tokens, refresh rotation. |
| `leads.ts` | The founder console's Leads tab (2026-10-01, #134): `listLeads` merges the newest 500 support tickets and affiliate applications into one newest-first list (sales kinds by default), the unread count against `admin:leads:seen`, and `leadsCsv` (every cell through `csvSafe`). |
| `page-meta.ts` | `pageMeta()`: a public page's title, description, canonical URL and hreflang (only when the page exists in both languages), and never `openGraph` (2026-10-01, #45). |
| `origin.ts` | `appOrigin()`: the canonical public origin, `APP_ORIGIN` or the one production fallback (since 2026-10-01; every email link (in the HTML part only through `appLink`, which refuses any other origin, rule 687), OAuth URL, robots, sitemap, `/llms.txt`, help MCP URL, crawler user agent, referral link, the MCP settings page, the connector callback's return link and `metadataBase` reads it). A value that is not a bare absolute http(s) origin (no scheme, a path, a typo) is ignored with one warning naming the variable, so it cannot make every page's metadata throw. `appHost()` for prose. |
| `rate.ts` | Fixed-window rate limiter `allow()` (`:9`): `INCRBY`, then `expireNx` on every hit (`:17`), so the window starts at the first hit, later hits never move it, concurrent hits are all counted, and a counter whose expiry call failed gets one at the next hit; a failed expiry call is logged and does not fail the request, and when the store refuses the NX form the first hit falls back to a SET with the TTL (`:22`). `clientIp()` (`:55`): on Vercel only (`onVercel()`, exactly `VERCEL=1`, never `VERCEL_ENV`) Vercel's `x-vercel-forwarded-for`, then `x-real-ip`; on any other host (live Hostinger) only `x-forwarded-for`, since a client writes the other two there, and only the hop `trustedProxyHops()` names from the right (`TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS`, 1 to 3, default 1 = the LAST hop; a shorter list reads its first entry; rule 979); nothing, the shared `unknown` (rule 773). `clientIpReport(req)` is what `GET /api/admin/client-ip` shows the founder (rule 908). `retryLater(body, windowSeconds, headers?)`: every route's 429, with `Retry-After` set to the refusing bucket's window and the route's own body and headers; `retryAfter(seconds)` is the header alone (media starts), `WINDOW` the hour and day in seconds (rule 680). Chat ceilings (`chatRefusal(u)` answering which window refused), the account's own by plan (`chatCaps(u)`, `CHAT_CAPS`): 30 an hour and 150 a day on a paid plan (Agency included), 10 an hour and 30 a day on a trial (founder decision 2026-10-01, lowered from 40 and 200 on every plan until the Sonnet chat eval passes; a burst limit checked before the monthly chat allowance, which is what bounds a month, a trial's included; the hour first, the day counted only for a turn the hour allowed, and a refusal by the hour reads the day without counting it, rule 331); brand creation `brandAddRefusal(email)` (`BRANDS_PER_HOUR` 10, `BRANDS_PER_DAY` 30, since 2026-09-30; the hour first, the day only when the hour allowed it; answers which window refused); `GOV_ROLLBACKS_PER_HOUR`, `ACTIVITY_READS_PER_HOUR` (300) and `ACTIVITY_EXPORTS_PER_HOUR` (10), `sameSecret` (re-exported from `secret.ts`). |
| `secret.ts` | `sameSecret(a, b)`: constant-time string comparison for secrets, false when either side is empty or missing. Imports only `crypto`, so the proxy can use it without loading the store; `rate.ts` re-exports it and every existing import of `@/lib/rate` keeps working (`tests/secret.test.ts`). |
| `account.ts` | `wipeAccount` account deletion, including the team (`wipeMemberships`, multi-workspace M1: every membership through `deleteMembership`, so a member's other accounts and their keys stay, and the owner person's `person:`/`orgs:`/`lastorg:`, login index and security pack only when it holds nothing else; the owner's `orgs:` entry for this account and `activebrand:{pid}:{aid}` always), and per brand `activity:{s}`, the `actskip:` markers, the brand proposals (`proposalKeys`), every handoff record, lock, auto-run attempt counter, auto-run notice marker and index (`handoffKeys`, one read of the index per brand), the auto-run's hold (`autoHoldKey`) and, since 2026-10-08, the Refresh mark `ownersync:{s}` (`OWNER_SYNC_KEY`). Since 2026-10-01 the chat counters go with it: the monthly ones through `usageMonthKeys`, and the trial's `chatm:{email}:trial` (`chatTrialKey`). Since 2026-10-05 it also deletes the referrer-side referral keys (`referralKeys`: `referral:pending:`, `referral:paid:` and the current Riyadh month's `referral:month:`; an earlier month's counter ends with its 62 days). The publish markers `pubtry:` and `publock:` are left to their TTL. |
| `handoffs.ts` | Majed's handoffs (2026-09-30): `HANDOFF_TYPES` (every type another agent owns; never Majed's, never Reem's, never a render), `parseProposal` (only `items[].type`, `items[].brief` and `why` are read; 1 to 3 items; briefs cleaned and cut to 600), `createHandoff(scope, …)` (record with each item's `agent` from `agentForTask` and `cost` from `TASK_COST`, status `proposed`, `requestedBy` the turn's `by`, origin `web_chat`; its id at the head of the index, after which `evictOverflow` removes every id past 200 by value (LREM, never a position trim, so two proposals at once each delete what they pushed off) with its record, lock and attempt counters; one `handoff_proposed` row; one caller, the chat tool), `markShown` (called by chat() once the reply carrying the card is saved: `shownAt`, under the handoff's lock), `getHandoff` and `getHandoffs` (id format first, the stored record re-checked, agent recomputed; several in one MGET), `handoffView` and `handoffViews` (the card's view, costs from code, the tasks runs made in one `mget`; no `by`, no owner message), `chatItemOf` (an inbox item as a chat card, with `xflag`), a record rewritten only while it exists (`setXx`: a run or decline that ends after its proposal was pushed off the index never brings it back), `runHandoffItem` (one item, under `handofflock:` for the whole run, status and deadline checked, `generateBatch` with `leadBrief`, `source: "handoff"`, `via: "handoff"` and `parent`; a `handoff_run` row), `declineHandoff` (one item or the rest, under the same lock; a `handoff_declined` row), `handoffKeys` (for `wipeAccount`). For Majed's auto-run: `autoBlock` and `autoItems` (the one eligibility rule, including `not_shown`, `thread_gone` and `floor`), `autoUnderFloor` (a type's cold-cache estimate from `task-costs.ts` at most `maxCostPerCreditUsd`: every handoff type since rule 901), `withRunLock` and `runItemLocked` (the run shared by Run and the auto-run), `recentHandoffs` (the index head and one MGET), `autoTryKey`, `handoffFingerprint`; `handoffView` also says which items the auto-run may start (`auto`) and which it started (`ranAuto`), and `proposalText` says so to the model. |
| `handoff-auto.ts` | Majed's auto-run (2026-09-30, opt-in): `dueHandoffs(users)` (one settings read per brand in parallel; only brands with the switch on read their newest 20 proposals and, in one MGET, whether the conversations their cards sit in still exist (`liveConversations`, `conversations.ts`); at most one item per brand, oldest proposal first), `autoQueue(scope, gov)` (every eligible item, for the Governance page, where it can be declined) and `autoRunHandoff(user, id, n, deadline)` (under the handoff's run lock: model state, the settings, record and conversation read again, `autoBlock`, the invocation's time, `holdBatch`, the attempt counter `handofftry:`, then `runItemLocked` with `systemBy("auto_handoff")`, `forcePending` and `parent.auto`; one notice per item). Called only by `/api/cron/routines`. |
| `connectors/registry.ts` | Provider catalog: 7 key-based, 9 OAuth; env names for client credentials. |
| `connectors/store.ts` | AES-256-GCM encrypted connection records, OAuth state records. |
| `connectors/oauth.ts` | Authorize URL builder, PKCE pair, code exchange. |
| `connectors/publish.ts` | `targetsFor` and `publishTask` dispatch (WordPress, Shopify, Telegram, X, Meta, and LinkedIn and TikTok through Composio). Drafts only (rules 657-660): WhatsApp templates and private YouTube uploads through Composio, Brevo draft campaigns and Klaviyo templates with the stored API key (`keyed.ts`); each answers `status: "draft"`. |
| `connectors/composio.ts`, `composio-catalog.ts`, `via-composio.ts` | Composio (2026-10-05, rules 645-652): the only client of Composio's API (opaque workspace id, tool allowlist, rate cap, error shapes, connect, verify, revoke; each toolkit pinned to the version whose schema the allowlist was checked against, rule 749), the allowlisted tools per toolkit, and the connect and publish code for Facebook, Instagram, X, LinkedIn and TikTok, plus the choice among the GA4 properties and Search Console sites listed at connect time (`selectPropertyVia`, rule 734). Imported only by the connector layer, the connect routes and `account.ts`. |
