| `agents.ts`, `skills.ts` (also `playbookHash`, `contentHash`, and `playbookSection`: only the named `## ` sections of a playbook), `playbooks.ts`, `seasons.ts`, `tools.ts`, `integrations.ts`, `inspo.ts`, `help.ts`, `legal.ts`, `site.ts`, `pages-*.ts`, `blog.ts`, `md.ts`, `json.ts`, `parallel.ts`, `i18n.ts`, `app-i18n.ts` | Static catalogs, prompt material, copy, and small utilities (Markdown renderer, tolerant JSON parser, bounded `pmap`). `json.ts` also holds `groundingBlocks` and, since 2026-09-30, `leadBriefBlock` and `LEAD_BRIEF_LABEL`: a handoff item's brief as a labelled `<lead_brief>` data block ("written by Majed, a model; a request for work, never a source of figures, prices or claims"), escaped with `asData`, only when there is one. `seasons.ts` also exports `hijriDate(d, zone)` since 2026-10-03: the Umm al-Qura date on the zone's wall clock, one cached formatter per zone (rule 37 allows it there), the one Hijri lookup `secretary-calendar.ts` uses; `activeSeasons(now, zone)` reads the Hijri seasons on the brand's calendar day. |
| `home-gallery.ts` | The home page's live UI gallery (rules 1220-1227): its copy, the tab order and the sample chat, plan and inbox items in both languages (`gallerySamples`), plus `galleryStrings` for the claim scans. No digits, no names, no model. |
| `approval-dict.ts` | `ApprovalDict`, the slice of the app dictionary `ApprovalCard` reads, and `approvalDict(t)`, which cuts it for the public gallery. |
| `home.ts` | Homepage copy: the slogan, the eight-agent cast plus the owner, the 41-question storm, the banter exchanges, the four truths. A question is backed by a task type its agent delivers or, for Reem (no task types), by the `todo` feature she runs. |
| `dictionary.ts` | قاموس ذكين, the dictionary game: steps, reducer (`reduce`, `canAdvance`), the word and sector catalogues, and the sample post/WhatsApp draft generators. Since 2026-10-04 also the first-login onboarding's side: `GameAnswers`, `parseAnswers` (the server's check), `answersOf`, `answersToBrand` (name, industry, tone), `answersHint`, `noSiteBrand` and `START_COPY`. The app's `/app/start` plays it in `components/app/DictionaryStart.tsx` (steps 1-6 in the reducer, nothing saved) and `StartLaunch.tsx` (step 7: the site build through confirm with the answers, or the brand file through `PUT /api/brand` without a site); the start wizard (`StartWizard.tsx`) is gone. Rules 520-526. |
| `csrf.ts` | The cross-site check for `/api` (2026-10-08, rules 690-693), run by `src/proxy.ts` before any route handler. `crossSiteRefusal(req)` (`:132`) looks only at POST, PUT, PATCH and DELETE (`CROSS_SITE_METHODS`): `Sec-Fetch-Site`, when present, decides alone (`same-origin` or `none` passes, anything else is refused; `Origin` is not compared too, as in Go 1.25's `CrossOriginProtection`, so a server that cannot see its public name still passes its own pages); without it, an `Origin` that is `null` or not in `acceptedOrigins` (`:109`: `appOrigin()`, and the request's own origin from `X-Forwarded-Proto` or the URL scheme with the first `X-Forwarded-Host` value or `Host`, `https` on that host always accepted, plain `http` only when the server sees no TLS) is refused; a request with neither header passes, since only browsers send them and the callers that send neither (crons, QStash, webhooks, MCP and OAuth clients) hold no browser cookie. The refusal is 403 `{"error":"cross_site"}` with `Cache-Control: no-store`. `CROSS_SITE_EXEMPT` (`:73`) and `isCrossSiteExempt` (`:90`): `/api/mcp`, `/api/oauth/token`, `/api/oauth/register` exact; `/api/media/hook/`, `/api/secretary/booking/`, `/api/billing/paymob/` (the Paymob webhook, PR #279), `/api/cron/` as prefixes; the reasons are in the comment at `:43-72`. |
| `src/emails/` (outside `src/lib`) | The HTML part of transactional email (2026-10-08, rules 685-689; `flows.md` section 49), no React and no new dependency. `html.ts`: the `html` tagged template that escapes every interpolated value (`SafeHtml`, `esc`, `raw` for string literals only, `fillHtml` for copy slots, `ltrRuns`, `plain`, `paragraphs`). `tokens.ts`: the inline colours (equal to `tokens.css`) and the font stack. `copy.ts`: the shared words in Arabic and English. `link.ts`: `appLink`, which throws on any href that is not `appOrigin()` plus a path. `layout.ts`: the one document (tables for Outlook, `lang` and `dir` on every table, cell, heading and paragraph because Gmail drops them from `<html>` and `<body>`, a 560-pixel card with an MSO ghost table, the preheader and footer). `button.ts`: the one button. Templates, registered in `index.ts` (`EMAIL_TEMPLATES`): `reset-password.ts` (the forgot route), `team-invite.ts` (the invite route; the inviter's address, the role and the link only, rule 688) and `notice.ts` (title, paragraphs and at most one button: the lifecycle notes and Reem's reminder, series warning and evening wrap). Each answers a string for `sendEmail`'s `html`; the caller keeps the subject and the text part.. Since 2026-10-16 (rules 1960-1962, branch only) also `verify-code.ts` (the email code in a left-to-right span, no button) and `daily-brief.ts` (Noor's brief: escaped paragraphs and one button), the typed registry `EMAIL_TEMPLATES` with `renderEmail(template, context, locale)`, and `samples.ts` (fixed sample contexts for the console preview and the tests) |
| `billing.ts` | Online billing through Paymob KSA (2026-10-08, rules 700-729, PR #279; `PAYMOB_CHARGES_VAT`): checkout, the callbacks, saved-card renewals with grace and lock, the owner's renewal settings and the founder's refund. Off unless `paymentProvider()` answers (`src/lib/payments`, rule 2050: the only provider is Paymob, null while `paymobConfig()` is). Amounts come from `pricing.ts`, the account from the session or from our own intent record, never from a callback's payload. Keys `paymob:*` in section 3; routes `api/billing/checkout`, `api/billing/renewal`, `api/billing/paymob/webhook` |
| `payments/provider.ts` | The payment provider seam (rules 2050-2057, flows section 85): the `PaymentProvider` type (`createCheckout`, `savedCard` prepare and charge or null, `parseEvent`, `verifyWebhook`, `parseTransaction`, `parseCardToken`, `refund`), its data types and `PaymentError` (short code and status only). The amount check, the account choice and exactly-once handling stay in `billing.ts` |
| `payments/index.ts` | `paymentProvider()`, the one place the provider is chosen (Paymob, null while its gate is off), and `paymentsEnabled()`, what routes and pages ask instead of the Paymob gate |
| `paymob.ts` | The Paymob KSA adapter and the only `PaymentProvider` (`paymobProvider()`, keys kept in closures): the configuration gate (`paymobConfig`), the four HTTP calls (create intention, pay with a saved card, refund, void), the callback parsers (`parseEvent`, `parseTxn`, `parseCardToken`) and HMACs (`verifyHmac`, 20 transaction and 8 card-token fields, `sameSecret`). Plain `fetch` and `node:crypto`, constant hosts (`PAYMOB_API`, `PAYMOB_CHECKOUT`), a 15 s timeout, errors (`PaymobError extends PaymentError`) carry a short code and the status only. Imported only by `src/lib/payments` (rule 2050) |
| `billing-state.ts` | The account's online subscription record (`Billing`: status `active`, `past_due` or `locked`; grace days; renewal due by Riyadh day via `paymobRenewalDue`). Import-free apart from types so `auth.ts` can read the lock; written only by `billing.ts` under `withUserLock`; the card token is never here |
| `invoice.ts` | The ZATCA e-invoicing seam (spec section 7): called once per online payment applied, after the payment is recorded. Issues nothing today and returns null (phase 2 plugs a provider in here); must stay idempotent on `paymentId` |
| `read-capped.ts` | `readCapped(req, max)`: a request body as text, or null past `max` bytes (declared length first, then a running total). For public callbacks with small bodies (the Paymob webhook) |
| `cart-recovery.ts` | Cart-recovery drafts on the store's own abandoned-cart totals (`store-sales.ts`): no snapshot naming products in carts means the request is refused before any charge (`missingData`); products reach the model as JSON data (`cartDataBlock`); a discount the brand file's offers do not contain becomes a placeholder (`capDiscounts`) |
| `slides.ts` | Parses Dana's deck outline (`## Slide N: Title`, bullets, `Notes:`) or any Markdown into `Slide[]` for the pptx route; every heading depth starts a slide, at most 8 bullets per slide |
| `kpi-delta.ts` | A KPI's change against the previous window of the same length. Import-free (a client component renders it); always prints the base; a change off a base of 0 is the absolute number, never a bare arrow |
| `report-sources.ts` | Which Reports sources (`analytics`, `ads`, `store`, `social`) count as read: only when a connected provider is in `READS_METRICS`. Type-only imports, so the client panel uses it |
| `scribble.ts` | The hand-drawn pen of the homepage prototype D (`scribble.js`, rule 512) as pure functions, no DOM: the server and the browser draw the same path from the same seed |
| `secretary-cal-proof.ts` | Pure proof that a linked calendar belongs to the brand (Reem B3 spec section 4): `domain` when the calendar account's mail domain matches the brand's own, anything else needs the owner's declaration; `FREE_MAIL` domains never prove a brand |

### 2.6 Cron jobs (`crons.json`)

No host reads `crons.json`: on thakeen.com each entry is an hPanel cron job, entered by hand, that calls the path with the `CRON_SECRET` bearer, and the file is the list those jobs must match (rule 989). Until 2026-10-10 it was `vercel.json`, read by Vercel Cron; it was renamed so that no new Vercel deployment carries crons. Vercel runs the crons of its current production deployment, so the old project's crons stop only when the founder disconnects or deletes the project (or turns its cron jobs off in its settings).

| Path | Schedule (UTC) | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| `/api/cron/weekly` | `0 3 * * 0` | For every (user, active brand) whose Sunday plan is on: `runWeekly` (`src/lib/schedule.ts:96`) generates a weekly plan through `generateBatch`, charging `TASK_COST.weekly_plan` (2 credits since 2026-09-30; it was 1) to Noor in that scope (`:130`). The plan is on by default for the default brand only; an added brand's answers `off` until the owner switches it on (`getSchedule`, `src/lib/schedule.ts:48`). |
| `/api/cron/daily` | `0 5 * * *` | Monthly credit renewal (since 2026-10-01 of the month's plan credits only; top-up packs keep what is left in them); since 2026-10-01, once per account on its default brand's entry, the write of a record whose expired top-up pack `getUser` already took out of the balance (`saveSettledTopups`); since 2026-09-30, once per account on its default brand's entry, the sweep of added brands' onboarding charges held on the account that no run used for a day (`sweepOnboardHolds`: refunded when the brand was never saved, dropped when it exists; archived brands included; a failure is reported and never stops the rest of the day); since 2026-10-01, on the same entry, the sweep of Sunday-plan charges whose run was killed with no retry in its week (`sweepWeeklyHolds`, rule 337: refunded when the week has no plan, dropped when it has one); Reem's purge of items deleted over 30 days ago and close of items whose linked inbox item was approved (`secretaryDaily`, every active scope, brand or not, which since 2026-10-03 also removes away entries ended over 30 days ago, `cleanAway`; and the purge alone, `purgeIdle`, for archived brands and suspended accounts); signup nudge email, lifecycle emails, daily brief, follow-up notifications, brief email (`dailyFor`, `src/app/api/cron/daily/route.ts:24`). Since 2026-09-30 its first hop also sweeps Reem's due reminders (`sweepReminders`, again while a sweep reads a full 200 scopes, nothing new after 60 s), the fallback when the QStash tick is not running; the sweep's time comes out of the batch's 240 s start budget. Since 2026-10-13 (rule 1534, branch only) the same first hop then runs the founder's margin alert (`runMarginAlert`, `src/lib/margin.ts`, `flows.md` section 69): no schedule of its own. |
| `/api/cron/golden` | `0 1 * * 6` | Runs the golden regression set (`src/lib/golden.ts:249`). |
| `/api/cron/media` | `20 5 * * *` | `sweepMedia` with the full budget (`maxDuration = 300`): reads the three open sets `mjobs:open:{fal,kie,higgsfield}`, drops members whose job is gone or terminal, and force-advances jobs older than 2 minutes or holding a result still to copy, oldest first, four at a time, so a render nobody is polling is finished or failed and refunded (`src/app/api/cron/media/route.ts`). Daily because every Vercel plan allows it; every 15 minutes if the plan allows sub-daily crons (founder question Q2). |
| `/api/cron/routines` | `0 6 * * *` | For every (user, active brand) routine that is on and has not run in its week or month (`dueRoutines`): `runRoutine`, one generation per item, charging the owning agent (`src/lib/routines.ts`). Since 2026-09-30 also Majed's auto-run (`dueHandoffs`, `autoRunHandoff`, `src/lib/handoff-auto.ts`): for brands whose owner switched it on, at most one handoff item per brand a day (id `{scope}:auto_handoff`), listed first. Every item, a routine or an auto-run, needs 180 s of the invocation left before its hold and charge (`BATCH_RUN_MIN_MS`), else `retry:late`, and a hop that ran something and left late items hands them to a fresh invocation (`cron-batch.ts`). After the 05:00 daily job, so credits are renewed first. |
| `/api/cron/evening` | `0 15 * * *` | Reem's evening wrap (2026-09-30) for every active workspace through `runCronBatch` (18:00 Riyadh, 19:00 Dubai); does nothing in Ramadan. With `?zones=…` (sent by the QStash tick when those zones' wrap time just passed, 18:00 local or 21:30 in Ramadan) it serves only the workspaces kept in those zones; a list naming any zone off the allow-list is 400 and nothing runs. |
| `/api/cron/stores` | `0 2 * * *` and `0 14 * * *` (two entries on one path, each once a day) | Store sales snapshot (2026-10-05) for every active workspace with a store connected, through `runCronBatch`, six at a time: `syncStoreIfDue` (`src/lib/store-sales.ts`) reads the store of the workspace's own snapshot (or its only connected store) unless the snapshot is under 10 hours old; a failed read keeps the last good snapshot and notes the failure. No model call, no credits. |
| `/api/cron/secretary` | every 15 min (not in `crons.json`): on thakeen.com an hPanel cron job POSTs it with the cron bearer (read-only list of 2026-10-08); an Upstash QStash schedule is accepted too, and whether one exists in the Upstash console is not verified (finding B-003) | Reem's tick (2026-09-30), `POST`, `maxDuration = 60`: oversized input refused before any check (413 for a body over 64 KB, 401 for a signature over 4096 characters; the proxy has buffered the body first, rule 774), then a signed QStash call (`verifyQstash`) or the CRON_SECRET bearer; only failed calls count against the per-IP limit (429); `sweepReminders` (nothing new after 40 s), then, when CRON_SECRET is set, starts `/api/cron/evening?zones=…` for the zones due. Without the QStash schedule the daily cron's fallback sweep fires reminders once a day and there is no wrap in Ramadan. |

The seven routes other than `/api/cron/secretary` (`daily`, `evening`, `golden`, `media`, `routines`, `stores`, `weekly`; counted from `src/app/api/cron/` on 2026-10-12) require `Authorization: Bearer ${CRON_SECRET}`, compared with `sameSecret`, and return 401 when the variable is unset. `/api/cron/secretary` accepts that bearer or a valid QStash signature (permissions row 112): with CRON_SECRET unset it still runs the reminder sweep on a signed call, but does not start the evening pass.

Heartbeats (rules 1014-1015): each of the eight cron routes (the seven in `crons.json` and `secretary`) calls `recordCronRun(job)` (`src/lib/cron-heartbeat.ts`) after it has authenticated the caller and finished, which writes `cronbeat:{job}` (the time in ms, kept 60 days). A 401, a 409 (golden not started) or a throw leaves the old value. `CRON_MAX_AGE_SEC` gives each job the age past which it is late: 26 h for the daily ones, 14 h for `stores`, 8 days for the weekly ones, 1 h for the 15-minute `secretary` tick. A job with no value yet is `waiting`, which is not late. `cronStatuses()` reads all eight with one `MGET`.

Opportunistic sweep: the image, video and poll routes schedule `maybeSweep(mediaDeps(), deadline)` in `after()`. It runs `sweepMedia` with `limit: 6` only when it wins the `msweep` lock (one per 5 minutes across all instances) and at least 30 s of the request's deadline are left, so abandoned renders are usually collected within minutes, not at 05:20 UTC.

### 2.7 External services

| Service | Used for | Call sites |
|---|---|---|
| Anthropic API | Every model id comes from `route(purpose)` (`src/lib/models/route.ts`, 2026-10-08; the defaults were the models the code called before, and since 2026-10-09 they are the three 5.5 models, rules 809 and 900): chat (`route("chat")`, default `claude-sonnet-5-5` since 2026-10-02, `claude-opus-5` before; resolved once per turn and shared with `reserveChatTurn`; `CHAT_MODEL` in `src/lib/chat.ts` is the exported default; called with the `max_tokens` the per-message ceiling leaves, `chat-budget.ts`), generation (`route("generate", { task })`, per type since 2026-10-09, rule 901: `TASK_MODELS[type].write` in `src/lib/task-models.ts`, `claude-opus-5-5` for ten types and `claude-sonnet-5-5` for sixteen; `claude-opus-5` for all before; resolved once per batch in `generateBatch` and shared with `creditsFor`, which prices a pick relative to that type's writer, rule 902; `generate()` in `noor.ts`), reviewer (`route("review", { task })`, `TASK_MODELS[type].review`, `claude-sonnet-5-5` for every type; `claude-sonnet-5` before, `critique` in `noor.ts`), cross-agent review (`route("xreview")`, `claude-sonnet-5-5`, asked once for all reviewers, `crossReview` in `noor.ts`, metered as `xreview` to the reviewing agent; one attempt of at most 45 s with `maxRetries: 0`, and none with less than 60 s of the caller's deadline left), judge (`route("judge", { generatedBy, task })`, `claude-sonnet-5-5`, or `claude-haiku-5-5` when a customer's Sonnet 5.5 pick generated a type Opus 5.5 writes; an Automatic Sonnet-written batch keeps the Sonnet judge, rule 903; `judge.ts`), onboarding (`route("onboard")`, `claude-opus-5-5`, asked once per run, `onboard.ts`), image and video prompts (`route("imagePrompt")`, `claude-haiku-5-5` since 2026-10-09, `claude-sonnet-5` before, `max_tokens` 400, one attempt of at most 20 s with `maxRetries: 0`, `write` in `src/lib/media/prompts.ts`), free tools (`route("tool")`, `claude-haiku-5-5` since 2026-10-09, `api/tools/[tool]/route.ts`; a reply with no text, which is how a Haiku refusal arrives since Haiku 5.5 has no server-side fallback, answers 502 `failed` and is not counted, rule 900). The agent-scope classifier (`route("agentScope")`, `claude-haiku-5-5`, rule 791, 2026-10-10: one forced `verdict` tool call, `max_tokens` 100, at most 3 s with `maxRetries: 0`, none with under 500 ms of the caller's deadline left, none while the model is paused; metered as `agent_scope` to the global counters only, never an account's; `classify` in `src/lib/agent-scope-classify.ts`). Since 2026-10-09 only these three models are called; Opus 5 and Sonnet 5 stay in the catalog with `auto` and `pick` false so stored usage still prices (rule 809, `tests/model-assignment.test.ts`). Key read implicitly by the SDK from `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (provider `anthropic`, the default). **Provider switch (`LLM_PROVIDER`, 2026-10-08; off until the variable is set):** with `LLM_PROVIDER=straitly` and `STRAITLY_API_KEY` set, `anthropic()` builds the same client against the Straitly gateway (`STRAITLY_BASE_URL`, default `https://api.straitly.ai`; the SDK adds `/v1/messages`) with a `fetch` wrapper in `src/lib/model.ts`: a request for a `claude-*` model that fails with a connection error or a 5xx before any response byte is sent once more to `https://api.anthropic.com`, same body and headers, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` in place of the gateway's key. Never retried: a status below 500, a reply that has started (so a streamed chat turn is never repeated), a non-Claude model, an aborted attempt, a missing `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`. The SDK's own `maxRetries` run on top and start at the gateway again. With `straitly` and only `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, calls go to Anthropic directly and that key never reaches the gateway. `modelState()` is `on` with either key (`hasModelKey()`, also read by the golden-set admin route); no key for the active provider in a production deployment is still `paused` (rule 431). `STRAITLY_API_KEY` is read only in `src/lib/model.ts` and never logged (`tests/model-provider.test.ts`, `tests/model-timeouts.test.ts`). Since 2026-10-01 every client comes from `anthropic({timeoutMs, maxRetries})` in `src/lib/model.ts` (rule 335; no other `new Anthropic(` in `src`), and every charged call passes `until(deadline, { cap })`, an abort signal that ends a stream's body too, from its caller's request or invocation deadline, with each attempt's timeout the smaller of the time left and the call's own budget (rule 336; the free tools and the judge have one retry inside their bounds; budgets in `MODEL_BUDGET`, flows.md §8 step 14). **Paused since 2026-09-28:** every call site checks `modelState()` in `src/lib/model.ts`, and `CLAUDE_PAUSED = true` there stops all of them on a deployment that has a key (flows.md §7 step 11). |
| Upstash Redis (REST) | All persistence (`upstash`, `src/lib/db.ts:70`), plus a second direct client in `api/waitlist/route.ts:10`. |
| fal.ai | The default image model `img-fast`: FLUX schnell, `POST https://fal.run/fal-ai/flux/schnell`, synchronous (`falAdapter` in `src/lib/media/fal.ts`). Body `{prompt, image_size: "square_hd" \| "portrait_16_9", num_images: 1, enable_safety_checker: true, output_format: "jpeg"}`; a reply with `has_nsfw_concepts[0] === true` is a content refusal (422 `nsfw`, refunded). The result is copied to the private disk store when `MEDIA_DIR` is set; without it, or when the copy fails, the fal URL is hot-linked as before. Key `FAL_KEY` in `Authorization: Key …`. |
| kie.ai | `img-balanced`, `img-premium`, `img-text`, `vid-quick`, `vid-premium` through `POST https://api.kie.ai/api/v1/jobs/createTask` and `GET /api/v1/jobs/recordInfo`; `vid-sound` (Veo 3.1 Fast, `model: "veo3_fast"`) through the legacy `POST /api/v1/veo/generate` and `GET /api/v1/veo/record-info`; the balance for the console from `GET /api/v1/chat/credit` (`kieAdapter` in `src/lib/media/kie.ts`, one body per model in `KIE_MODELS`). `vid-sound` sends `enableTranslation: true` explicitly (`kie.ts:78`: Veo refuses non-English prompts, a prompt can carry the Arabic brand name, and kie's schema and description disagree on the default), with `duration: 8` and `resolution: "720p"`. `vid-premium` (`kling-3.0/video`, `duration: "10"`, `mode: "std"`, `sound: true`) sends both fields kie lists as required: `multi_shots: false` and one `multi_prompt` shot that repeats the prompt, cut to 500 UTF-16 units without splitting a surrogate pair, with `duration: 10` (`:94-95`). Bearer `KIE_API_KEY`. No cancel (kie documents none). `creditsConsumed` is recorded when reported. |
| Higgsfield | `img-lifestyle` (Soul v2), `img-design` (Recraft v4.1), `vid-cinematic` (Seedance 2.0, `generate_audio: false`), `vid-motion` (Hailuo 2.3) through `POST https://api.higgsfield.ai/{endpoint}`, `GET /requests/{id}/status`, best-effort `POST /requests/{id}/cancel` at max age, and `POST /estimate/{endpoint}` for the console (`higgsfieldAdapter` in `src/lib/media/higgsfield.ts`, one endpoint and body per model in `HF_MODELS`). `Authorization: Key {HIGGSFIELD_API_KEY_ID}:{HIGGSFIELD_API_KEY_SECRET}`. Plain `fetch`, no SDK (`@higgsfield/client` retries POSTs). |
| Hostinger disk (media) | Our own copy of every provider result, private (rules 1750-1763): `MEDIA_DIR/{2 hex}/{32 hex}.jpg` or `.mp4` outside the app folder, served only by `src/app/m/[name]/route.ts` to a signed-in member of the workspace whose index holds the file, or to a 24-hour signed link minted at hand-off. Images re-encoded to JPEG with `sharp`, clips stored unchanged. Ceilings `MEDIA_MAX_BYTES` / `MEDIA_MAX_FILES` / `MEDIA_MAX_FILE_BYTES` (refused at 95 %, plus 2 GiB kept free on the disk). Deleted with the account. Old public Vercel Blob copies (`*.public.blob.vercel-storage.com`) stay readable until `scripts/media-blob-move.ts` moves them; nothing writes to Blob any more. |
| Provider result hosts | Whatever https host a kie or Higgsfield status answer (or the fal reply) names: downloaded only through `safeFetch` with `httpsOnly` inside `copyToStore` (a redirect to http is refused), never followed from a webhook body. |
| placehold.co | Placeholder card when the default image model is unavailable (no `FAL_KEY`, or `img-fast` switched off by the breaker) and the item has no artwork yet: `placeholder` in `src/lib/media/start.ts:47`. URL only, no server fetch, nothing charged. An item that already has a generated image keeps it (409 `model_unavailable`). |
| Resend | Transactional and lifecycle email (`sendEmail`, `src/lib/notify.ts:28`): a text part always, and an HTML part from `src/emails/` for the reset, invite, lifecycle and Reem mails since 2026-10-08. Click tracking must stay off on the sending domain: it would rewrite the HTML part's links, whose reset and join tokens ride in the fragment (rule 546). A dashboard setting, not checked in code. |
| Upstash QStash | Since 2026-09-30, a schedule the founder creates in the Upstash console calls `POST /api/cron/secretary` every 15 minutes (Reem's reminders and the evening wrap per zone); every call is signed and checked in `src/lib/qstash.ts`. Nothing is published to QStash from the app. |
| Hostinger | Hosting since 2026-10-08: the live and staging Node.js web apps (section 6), the function runtime, and the cron jobs of 2.6 in hPanel. |
| Vercel | Nothing since 2026-10-13: the media store moved to the Hostinger disk (rules 1750-1763). Old Blob URLs already saved on tasks are still read until the copy script moves them; the `@vercel/blob` dependency goes in the contract release. It hosted the site, its functions and its crons until 2026-10-08, and since rules 987-990 (2026-10-10) the repository holds nothing a Vercel project reads. |
| Connector platforms | X, Shopify, Google, Meta, Salla, Zid, LinkedIn, TikTok, Snapchat (OAuth); WordPress, WooCommerce, Klaviyo, Odoo, PrestaShop, Telegram, Brevo (keys). Actual API use exists for X, Shopify, Meta (Facebook Page and Instagram publishing), WordPress, WooCommerce, Odoo, PrestaShop, Telegram, Salla and Zid (product import and the store-sales sync), Brevo (verify only), Klaviyo (verify only). Google, LinkedIn, TikTok, Snapchat only complete the OAuth exchange and store tokens. |
| Arbitrary customer websites | `crawl.ts:51` and `catalog.ts:13,27` fetch URLs supplied by the user or discovered in sitemaps. |

### 2.8 Tooling and measured performance (2026-10-17, rules 2090-2101)

| Piece | Where | What |
|---|---|---|
| Flat ESLint config | `eslint.config.mjs` | `eslint-config-next` (core web vitals, typescript), the `thakeen-ui` plugin (`eslint-rules/thakeen-ui.mjs`, rules 1095-1098, 1200-1201), the jsx-a11y recommended set for `src/**/*.tsx`, and three type-aware promise rules for `src/**/*.{ts,tsx}` with `projectService` |
| Which rule is an error | `eslint-rules/tooling-rules.mjs` | `A11Y_RATCHETED` and `TYPE_RATCHETED` list the rules that still have violations (warnings); every other rule of those sets is an error. Options with a reason sit beside them |
| Ratchet | `eslint-rules/ratchet.json`, `eslint-rules/ratchet.mjs`, `scripts/lint-ratchet.mjs`, `tests/lint-ratchet.test.ts` | Per-rule warning counts over `src` that may only go down |
| Route sizes | `scripts/route-sizes.mjs` | Gzip client JS per route and in total from a webpack build; `--diff a.json b.json` compares two runs |
| Skills console words | `src/lib/skills-admin-dict.ts` | `pickSkillsDict` picks the dictionary keys the founder skills console and the instruction editor read, on the server |

`npm run check` is `tsc --noEmit -p . && eslint && vitest run`. Type-aware lint made `eslint` slower (18.5 s to 28 s) and the ratchet test lints `src` once more inside the unit tests (about 20 s, in parallel with the other files). The lockfile rule in CLAUDE.md still applies when a dependency changes: npm 11.5 drops the optional `@rolldown/binding-*` entries when it regenerates the lock, so a change to a single root line was made by hand.

## 3. Redis key namespace

"scope" means `email` for the default brand or `email#brandId` for extra brands (`workspaceScope` in `src/lib/keys.ts`; `scopeFor` in `src/lib/brands.ts` is its plain-string form). "email" and "owner" mean the normalized account (owner) email; "login" means the address a person signs in with, an owner's or a member's (2026-09-29). No key has a TTL unless stated.

Since 2026-10-17 (rules 2030-2034, branch only) the key families below are built by `src/lib/keys.ts`: `wsKey.*` for the scope-keyed rows, `accountKey.*` for email and login keys, `globalKey.*` for token, id and index keys. The bytes are unchanged, so the table still names each key by its pattern. A workspace builder accepts only a `Scope` (from `sc(u)`, `workspaceScope` over the account's own brand list, or `storedScope` for a scope read from our own records); a string from a request does not type-check. Raw key templates still written elsewhere are listed per file in `tests/key-registry-ratchet.test.ts` and may only go down. Four shapes are checked on read by `src/lib/stored.ts` (rules 2035-2039): `brands:{email}`, `activebrand:{login}`, `conn:{scope}:{provider}`, `conv:{scope}:{id}`.

Since 2026-10-19 (multi-workspace membership, release M1 expand, branch `claude/wave3-m1-expand`, rules 2110-2139, `flows.md` section 89) a fourth builder group `personKey` holds the person-keyed families: `person:`, `lock:person:`, `orgs:`, `lastorg:` and the per-(person, account) `activebrand:`, `sec:feedof:`, `sec:bookof:`; `accountKey.membership` (`mbr:{aid}:{pid}`) and `accountKey.teamLog` (`teamlog:{aid}`) join the account group. Their parameters are the branded `PersonId` and `AccountId` (made only by `storedPersonId` and `storedAccountId`, which take a value read back from our own records, never one from a request; the ratchet lists every call and none may sit under `src/app`), so a plain `string` does not type-check (rule 2137). A pid and an aid contain no `:` (an address cannot: `validEmail` bans it), and the per-account keys have one more segment than their per-login twins, so no new key equals a legacy one. `members:{aid}` keeps its builder; its values are person ids (a legacy member's is its address). The legacy per-login `activebrand:{login}`, `sec:feedof:{login}` and `sec:bookof:{login}:{provider}` stay read and written for a person's home account (rollback). The switch is the code constant `MULTI_ORG = false` (`src/lib/multi-org.ts`). The nine families are in the table below. M1 writes and reads every one of them except `lastorg:`, which has no writer until the M2 switch. An older build ignores all of them (rule 2132), and the legacy records they mirror are still written, so a rollback to any earlier build is safe (`flows.md` section 89.1).

| Key pattern | Type | Stores | Owner module | TTL |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `user:{email}` | json | The ACCOUNT, an owner login: `User` without the runtime-only `scope`, `brandId` and `actor` (includes `passHash`, a mirror of the person's since multi-workspace M1 and absent on an account a `p_` person owns, plan, credits, `suspended` (`true`, `false` or, since 2026-10-17 rule 2070, `{ until: ISO }`; read only through `isSuspended`), `sessionsAfter` (stamped at creation since 2026-09-29), `teamSessionsAfter` (member sessions issued before it are dead: owner reset, founder reset), `devicesAfter` (since 2026-10-11, rule 1170, optional: the owner's last "sign out other devices"; browser sessions issued before it are dead, the feed and booking links are not), `brandCap` (founder override of the active brands, rule 1393, since 2026-10-12), `seats` (founder seat override, also an Agency deal's seats), `deal` (since 2026-09-30: an Agency contract `{credits, priceSar, setAt, setBy}`, written only by `POST /api/admin/plan` under the user lock and deleted when the account leaves agency or the deal is cleared, with the seat override unless the request sets `seats`; the stored `plan` may still say `enterprise` until the next save, and `getUser` reads it as `agency`), `onboardPaid` (since 2026-09-30: per brand scope, an added brand's held setup charge `{scope, agent, month, credits, at}`, written in the same save as the debit and dropped in the same save as its refund, or once the brand is saved, by `chargeOnboard`, `refundOnboard`, `settleOnboard` and the daily `resolveOnboardHolds` in `credits.ts`), `paidOnce` (since 2026-10-01: per claim, the Sunday plan's `weekly:{scope}:{week}`, a charge taken at most once, the same `{scope, agent, month, credits, at}`, written in the same save as the debit by `chargeOnce`, dropped in the same save as its refund by `refundOnce`, dropped after 8 days by the next `chargeOnce`, and resolved by the daily cron once its week has ended: refunded when that week has no plan, dropped when it has one, `resolveOnceHolds` from `sweepWeeklyHolds`, rule 337; and since 2026-10-08 X's paid read `xread:{scope}:{UTC day}`, written by `refreshX` (x-stats.ts) with the same shape, refunded once on a failure by the click that took it unless a read landed that day, dropped after 8 days by the next `chargeOnce`, and never touched by `sweepWeeklyHolds`, which matches only `weekly:`, rules 744, 745), and `agentSpend: {month, by: {scope: {agent: credits}}}`, the current UTC month's credits per brand scope and agent, written only by `charge` and `refund`; since 2026-10-01 `chatAllowance` (the founder's override of the plan's chat messages, a whole number 0..100,000, written only by `POST /api/admin/plan` under the user lock and dropped with a deal like `seats`), `chatOverflow` (`true` when the owner chose to continue on credits once the month's messages run out, written only by `POST /api/billing/chat-overflow` under the user lock; never on a trial) and `chatCarryMicro` (the metered cost of over-allowance messages not yet billed, in micro-USD, always under one credit at the floor rate, written by `settleChatOverflow` in the same save as the balance); `topups` (2026-10-01: the account's live top-up packs `[{id, credits, left, at, expiresAt, paidSar, paidOn, ref, by, paymentId?}]`, added and voided only by `POST /api/admin/topups` and spent, refunded and settled only by the ledger, all under the user lock; `paymentId` names the `payments:{email}` record the pack's payment was recorded under (rule 454); `credits` is the whole balance, packs included, and the runtime-only `topupsDirty` is never stored), and an `onboardPaid` or `paidOnce` hold's `from` (the charge's receipt when it took from a pack); since 2026-10-08 an optional `chinaHostingOk`, the owner's confirmation that a China-hosted model may be used, set only by `PUT /api/models` `{confirmChina: true}` under the user lock; since 2026-10-12 an optional `handPlan` `{paidThrough, by, at}`, the Riyadh day a plan set by hand is paid through, written only by `POST /api/admin/plan` under the user lock, rules 1302-1305, an expand per rule 679) | `auth.ts`, `credits.ts` | none |
| `users:all` | set | All account ids (since 2026-10-18, rule 1980; in R1 each id is the owner's first address, so the bytes are unchanged); member logins never enter it | `auth.ts` `saveUser`; read by `schedule.ts:237` (`dailyRoster`), `admin-stats.ts:60`, `api/admin/plan`, `api/admin/export` | none |
| `login:{email}` | json | Since 2026-10-18 (account-id R1, rules 1981-1983, flows 88): the sign-in address's person, `{kind:"person", pid}` (zod `LoginIndex`, which also reads the first shape `{kind:"owner", id}`). An owner's pid is its account id, a member's its frozen `Member.id`. Written by `claimLogin` under `lock:login:{email}` after `getLogin` found nothing; deleted by `releaseLogin` only while it names that pid. Absent for accounts from before R1 until `scripts/account-ids.ts` runs; `getLogin` then falls back to `user:{email}` with the ownership guard | `auth.ts` (`readLoginIndex`, `claimLogin`, `releaseLogin`, `getLogin`), `team.ts`, `account.ts`, `scripts/account-ids.ts` | none |
| `session:h:{sha256(token)}`, `session:{token}` (legacy) | json | Since 2026-10-12 (rule 1345, expand step) a session is written under the SHA-256 of its token, never the token; `sessionRecord` reads the hashed key, then the raw key of a session written before (only for a 64-hex token), and `dropSession` deletes both. The contract step (no raw read) is a later release, after 30 days. `{email, at, imp?, kp?, as?, exp?}` for a 32-byte hex session token: the LOGIN email (an owner's or a member's), the issue time, and on an impersonation session the founder's email or `"key"` (`imp`); a key impersonation also holds the key's fingerprint (`kp`) and, when a key session opened it, that session's record hash (`as`), and stops working when either no longer matches (rule 429). Records from before 2026-09-18 are a bare email. Since 2026-10-11 (rule 1177, an expand) an impersonation session also holds `exp`, the end the founder chose (15, 60 or 120 minutes), and is dead after it; a session written before has no `exp` and reads as `at` + 2 hours. Dead when `at` is older than the owner's `sessionsAfter`, or for a member older than its own `sessionsAfter` or the owner's `teamSessionsAfter` | `auth.ts` `createSession`, `sessionRecord`, `dropSession`, `currentUser` | 30 days; 2 hours for an impersonation session |
| `pwreset:{sha256(token)}` | json | `{email, kind}` for a password reset, `kind` `owner` or `member` (records without `kind` are an owner's) | `api/auth/forgot/route.ts`, `api/auth/reset/route.ts` | 1 hour |
| `pwresetuse:{sha256(token)}` | lock | Single use of a reset link: SET NX before the `pwreset:` record is deleted | `api/auth/reset/route.ts` | 1 hour |
| `verify:code:{email}` | json | The pending email-verification code (2026-10-08, rule 757): `{hash, sentAt}`, the hash is SHA-256 of the address and the code, never the code (that keeps the code out of storage and logs; anyone who can read Redis reverses 10^6 codes offline, hence the TTL and the attempt cap). One per address; a new code replaces it. Deleted on success, on the fifth wrong answer, when the mail did not go out (except on a test deploy), and when the address is moved (the old address's code); an account deletion does not delete it, it expires with its TTL | `verify-email.ts` (`issueVerifyCode`, `checkVerifyCode`, `markVerified`, `dropVerifyCode`) | 900 s |
| `verify:tries:{email}` | int | Wrong-answer counter of the pending code (rule 758): `INCRBY` before the comparison so parallel guesses each get their own count; at 5 the code and the counter are deleted. Reset when a new code is issued | `verify-email.ts` (`checkVerifyCode`) | 900 s (`expireNx` on the first try) |
| `rate:{bucket}` | int | Fixed-window counter. Buckets: `login:ip:{ip}`, `login:em:{email}`, `signup:ip:{ip}`, `forgot:ip:{ip}`, `forgot:em:{email}`, `reset:ip:{ip}`, `impstop:ip:{ip}` (2026-10-05, 30 per 15 min, the impersonation stop), and since 2026-10-08 the email-verification buckets `verify:gap:{email}` (1 per 60 s), `verify:hour:{email}` (5 per hour), `verify:day:{email}` (10 per day, rule 768), `verify:ip:{ip}` (20 per hour), all four for sending a code, `verifycheck:ip:{ip}` (60 per hour, checking a code), `verifymove:ip:{ip}` (20 per hour) and `verifymove:acct:{session email}` (10 per day), both for changing the address (the spec's `verify:send:*` name was dropped for these), `adminkey:{ip}`, `adminhdr:{ip}`, `support:ip:{ip}`, `affiliates:ip:{ip}`, `waitlist:ip:{ip}`, `chat:h:{email}`, `chat:d:{email}`, `onboard:{email}`, `perf:{email}`, `files:{scope}`, and since 2026-09-29 `team:invite:acct:{owner}` (20 a day, 5 on a trial), `team:invite:to:{sha256(invited email)}` (5 a day across all accounts), `team:invite:ip:{ip}` (30 an hour), `team:peek:ip:{ip}` (30 per 15 min), `team:accept:ip:{ip}` (10 per 15 min), `oauth:register:ip:{ip}`, `oauth:token:ip:{ip}` (2026-09-29, OAuth register and token), `draft:h:{email}` and `draft:d:{email}` (MCP `save_draft`, 30 an hour and 150 a day per account, `draftAllowed`), `govrollback:{email}` (20 an hour), `chatfeedback:{email}` (120 an hour, `FEEDBACK_PER_HOUR`), `activitylist:{email}` (300 an hour), `activityexport:{email}` (10 an hour), `chatovf:{email}` (since 2026-10-01, `POST /api/billing/chat-overflow`, the owner's "continue on credits" switch, 30 an hour per account, 429 `slow_down`), `agent-scope:h:{scope}` and `agent-scope:d:{scope}` (since 2026-10-10, rule 795, enforced input refusals of the agent-scope gate per brand scope, 20 an hour and 60 a day, read by `agentScopeCeiling` and counted by `countAgentScopeRefusal` from `scopeCheck`), `agent-scope-gen:h:{scope}` and `agent-scope-gen:d:{scope}` (since 2026-10-10, rule 796, classifier asks of the input gate on generation notes per brand scope, 60 an hour and 200 a day, `AGENT_SCOPE_GEN_CHECKS_PER_HOUR` and `_PER_DAY`, counted by `agentScopeGenRefusal` from `checkGenerationNotes` only when the classifier would be asked: not with the layer `off`, not with empty notes, not before the free refusals of `generateBatch`), `brandadd:h:{email}` and `brandadd:d:{email}` (since 2026-09-30, brand creation, 10 an hour and 30 a day per account, `brandAddRefusal`: the hour first, the day only when the hour allowed it, and the refusal says which window, `retry` "hour" or "day"), `sitesync:{scope}` and `socialsync:{scope}` (since 2026-10-08, rule 735, the Refresh of the site and social figures, `sync_site` and `sync_social` on `POST /api/connect/{provider}`, 6 an hour per workspace, 429 `slow_down` with `Retry-After`; `sitesync:{scope}` also gates the read at once after a `select_property` choice, rule 734, which is saved either way and answers `read: false` past the six), `adrefresh:{scope}` (since 2026-10-09, rule 735, `sync_ads` on `POST /api/connect/{ad provider}`, every ad platform together, 6 an hour per workspace, 429 `slow_down` with `Retry-After`; not the `adsync:{scope}:{provider}` time of the last ad read) and `xsync:{scope}` (since 2026-10-09, rules 735 and 744, `sync_x` on `POST /api/connect/x`, 6 clicks an hour per workspace before any charge or call to X, 429 `slow_down` with `Retry-After`), and the media buckets, the wishlist buckets and Reem's `sec:create`, `sec:write`, `sec:read` and `sec:cal` buckets listed further down this table | `rate.ts` `allow` (`:5`) | window seconds, set with EXPIRE NX on every hit (`rate.ts:13`; a first-hit SET when the store refuses NX, `:18`), so a counter never keeps refusing without an expiry |
| `paymob:intent:{ref}` | json | What we asked Paymob to charge, stored before the call (`PayIntent`: account, kind `plan`, `topup` or `renewal`, amounts in halalas, `status`); the only source of the account and the amount for a callback. `ref` is `pmi_` plus 24 hex | `billing.ts` | 400 days |
| `paymob:order:{orderId}` | string | The intent `ref` for a Paymob order id | `billing.ts` | 400 days |
| `paymob:txn:{txnId}` | json | `{ref, email, kind, amountCents, paymentId}` of an applied transaction, for refunds | `billing.ts` | 400 days |
| `paymob:done:{txnId}` | string | Outcome of a transaction callback (`paid`, `declined`, `mismatch`, `unapplied`): a repeated callback answers `already` | `billing.ts` | 400 days |
| `paymob:txlock:{txnId}` | lock | One callback applied at a time per transaction | `billing.ts` | 60 s |
| `paymob:refunded:{txnId}` | string | `{at, by}` once the founder refunded the transaction | `billing.ts` | 400 days |
| `paymob:refund_ended:{txnId}` | string | The payment id whose refund ended the plan (`endPlanOnRefund`, written under `lock:user` right after the save, multi-workspace M1): a retry after a `busy` answer still answers `planEnded: true`, and a transaction with this key never ends a plan again (a repeated refund after a new purchase leaves the new plan) | `billing.ts` | 400 days |
| `paymob:refundlock:{txnId}` | lock | One refund at a time per transaction | `billing.ts` | 60 s |
| `paymob:attempt:{email}:{day}` | counter | Marks a saved-card renewal attempt for the Riyadh day (SET NX, so one attempt a day per account) | `billing.ts` | 3 days |
| `paymob:card:{email}` | json | The saved card token for renewals; no page or API answer reads it; deleted by the owner's `remove_card` and by `wipeAccount` | `billing.ts` (`cardKey`), `account.ts` | none |
| `paymob:last:{email}` | string | The account's latest checkout `ref` (rule 1155), so the return page asks about the session's account alone | `billing.ts` (`lastCheckoutKey`) | 1 day |
| `rate:paycheckout:{email}` / `rate:payrenewal:{email}` / `rate:paymobhook:{ip}` | int | Billing buckets: checkout 10 an hour, renewal settings 30 an hour, the webhook per client address (`HOOK_PER_IP`) | `api/billing/*` | the window |
| `rate:modelpick:{email}` | int | The model picker's saves, `MODEL_PICKS_PER_HOUR` an hour | `api/models/route.ts` | 1 hour |
| `weeklylock:{email}` | lock | One weekly plan run at a time per account; released in `finally`, and removed by `wipeAccount` | `schedule.ts` | 300 s |
| `crondone:{job}:{period}:{id}` / `crontry:{job}:{period}:{id}` | string / int | Cron batch markers: an item done in this period (the first 80 characters of its outcome) and the attempts so far; `runCronBatch` skips done items and stops after its attempt cap | `cron-batch.ts` | `markerTtlSeconds` per job: 2 days (daily, stores, evening, routines), 8 days (weekly) |
| `lock:user:{email}` | lock | Per-user credit/ledger mutex | `credits.ts:14` | 10 s |
| `member:{email}` | json | A member login (2026-09-29): `{email, name, lang, passHash, memberOf, role, invitedBy, acceptedAt, sessionsAfter, devicesAfter?, disabled?}` (`devicesAfter` since 2026-10-11, rule 1170: the last "sign out other devices", browser sessions only); `memberOf` is the owner email, `role` is `admin` or `member`, `disabled: "seats"` is set by `enforceSeats`. Never written by `saveUser` | `team.ts` | none |
| `members:{owner}` | set | Member emails of the account (the roster). `currentUser` and `getLogin` require a member to be in it (`onRoster`, one `SISMEMBER`), so a record that outlived its entry never signs in or holds its address | `team.ts` | none |
| `invite:{sha256(token)}` | json | A pending invite `{email, role, owner, aid?, invitedBy, invitedByPid?, createdAt, expiresAt, lang}` (`aid` and `invitedByPid` since multi-workspace M1, read as `aid ?? owner` and `invitedByPid ?? invitedBy`); the token itself is never stored, and the hash is the invite id `DELETE /api/team/invite` takes | `team.ts` | 7 days |
| `invites:{owner}` | set | Invite hashes of the account; entries whose invite expired are dropped when listed | `team.ts` | none |
| `lock:team:{owner}` | lock | The seat count and roster changes of one account: invite, accept (which re-reads the invite, the owner and the inviter under it), cancel, removal, leave, role change, `enforceSeats`, `wipeMemberships` | `team.ts` | 10 s |
| `lock:login:{email}` | lock | Login uniqueness across `user:` and `member:`: signup and invite acceptance create logins under it | `team.ts` `withLoginLock`, `api/auth/signup` | 10 s |
| `lock:member:{email}` | lock | Read-modify-write AND deletion of one member record: password, name, language, role, seat pause, removal, leave, account deletion. Removal takes it inside `lock:team`, so a profile, password or reset write in flight can never restore a removed login | `team.ts` `withMemberLock` | 10 s |
| `inviteuse:{sha256(token)}` | lock | Single use of an invite at accept; a second concurrent accept is refused | `team.ts` `acceptInvite` | 30 s |
| `person:{pid}` | json | `Person` (`PersonRow`, rule 2134): credentials and profile of one person, `{pid, email, name, lang, passHash?, emailVerifiedAt?, sessionsAfter?, devicesAfter?, suspended?, trialUsedAt?, createdAt}`; wins over the legacy `user:`/`member:` credential fields unless the legacy record is provably newer. Written by `createUser`, `createMember`/`acceptInvite`, `updatePerson` (every credential write) and the address moves; read by `getPerson`/`personFromRaw` in `getLogin`, `resolveSession` and `userFromBearer`; older builds ignore it. Account deletion deletes the owner's and a member's row only when that person holds nothing else (`holdsElsewhere`). | `keys.ts` `personKey.person`, `stored.ts` `PersonRow` | none |
| `lock:person:{pid}` | lock | One person's credential writes; taken after `lock:login`, before `lock:member`/`lock:user` (rule 2139). Taken by `withPersonLock` around every credential write; older builds never take it. | `keys.ts` `personKey.lock` | 10 s |
| `orgs:{pid}` | set | `AccountId`s the person owns or belongs to: a hint for listing only, every decision re-reads the forward records (rule 2136). Written by `createUser` (the owned account), `writeMembership` and the owner address move, removed by `deleteMembership`; read by `listOrgs`, `pickAccount` and `holdsElsewhere`; older builds ignore it. Account deletion removes the deleted account from every member's and the owner's `orgs:`; the set itself goes only with a person who holds nothing else. | `keys.ts` `personKey.orgs` | none |
| `lastorg:{pid}` | string | The `AccountId` last chosen in the workspace switcher (M2; no writer in M1). | `keys.ts` `personKey.lastOrg` | none |
| `mbr:{aid}:{pid}` | json | `Membership` (`MembershipRow`, rule 2115): `{aid, pid, role: "admin" \| "member", acceptedAt, invitedBy, disabled?}`; never an owner (a row claiming it does not parse and grants nothing), counts only while `members:{aid}` lists the pid. Written by `src/lib/team.ts` (`writeMembership` with the roster and `orgs:` in one round, `deleteMembership`; `flows.md` 89), read by `getMembership`/`membershipFromRaw` in `resolveSession`, `getLogin` and the team page; older builds ignore it. | `keys.ts` `accountKey.membership`, `stored.ts` `MembershipRow` | none |
| `teamlog:{aid}` | list | Capped at `CAPS.teamLog` (500) with `pushCapped`, newest first: one `TeamLogRow` per membership event (rule 2129), never a token, invite id or password; reader is M3. Written by `logTeam` (`src/lib/teamlog.ts`) under the event's `lock:team:{aid}` (`flows.md` 89 item 3); older builds ignore it. | `keys.ts` `accountKey.teamLog`, `stored.ts` `TeamLogRow` | none |
| `activebrand:{pid}:{aid}` | string | The brand id one person last chose in one account (rule 2119); the per-login `activebrand:{login}` stays for the home account. Written by `setActiveBrandIn` (the brands switch and archive), read by `resolveSession` and `activeBrandIn`, deleted by `deleteMembership`; older builds ignore it. | `keys.ts` `personKey.activeBrand` | none |
| `sec:feedof:{pid}:{aid}` | string | Hash of one person's calendar feed link in one account; `sec:feedof:{login}` stays for the home account and names the same hash there (a home record is live only while both name it). Written by `createFeed`, read by `resolveFeed`, `getFeedStatus` and `revokeFeed`, deleted by `revokeFeed` (also by address, for the home record) and `deleteMembership`; older builds ignore it. | `keys.ts` `personKey.feedOf`, `feed-token.ts` | none |
| `lock:feed:{pid}:{aid}` | lock | Owned lock (10 s) around one person's feed create and revoke in one account (rule 2139). A LEAF lock: taken under any lock of the order, takes nothing but the legacy `lock:feed:{login}` inside it for the home account. Create answers `busy` while it is held; revoke tries 20 times, 50 ms apart, then revokes anyway. | `keys.ts` `personKey.feedLock`, `feed-token.ts` | 10 s |
| `sec:bookof:{pid}:{aid}:{provider}` | string | Hash of one person's booking link for one provider in one account; `sec:bookof:{login}:{provider}` stays for the home account and names the same hash there (both must name a home record). Written by `createBook`, read by `resolveBook`, `getBookStatus` and `revokeBook`, deleted by `revokeBook` and `deleteMembership`; older builds ignore it. | `keys.ts` `personKey.bookOf`, `booking-token.ts` | none |
| `lock:book:{pid}:{aid}:{provider}` | lock | Owned lock (10 s) around one person's booking create and revoke for one provider in one account (rule 2139). | `brands:{email}` | json | `BrandMeta[]`; its active entries are capped per plan since 2026-10-12 (`brandCapFor`, rules 1390-1391; archived entries do not count and nothing is removed); every write goes through `withBrandsLock` | `brands.ts` | none |
| `brandslock:{email}` | lock | The brand list's read-modify-write (add, rename, archive), since 2026-09-30 | `brands.ts` `withBrandsLock` | 10 s |
| `activebrand:{login}` | string | Active brand id of one LOGIN (2026-09-29): the owner's key is unchanged and each member has its own; a member's value is used only while it is one of the owner's active brands | `brands.ts` | none |
| `brand:{scope}` | json | `Brand` file (since 2026-09-29 with an optional `timeZone`, one of the six in `zones.ts`, stored only when allow-listed; since 2026-10-08 an optional `model`, the brand's generation pick, a catalog id written only by `PUT /api/models` and kept by `PUT /api/brand`) | `brand.ts:150` | none |
| `tasks:{scope}` | list | Task ids, newest first (reads cap at 200) | `brand.ts:178,268` | none |
| `task:{scope}:{id}` | json | `Task` (since 2026-10-12 also `agentScopeRelease?: {hash, by, at}`, the founder's release of one held text, rule 1411, an expand-only field), including the media fields `mediaJob` (the running job of each kind), `mediaLast` (the last failure of each kind), `mediaCredits` and `mediaApplied` (the ids of the last 8 finished render jobs whose result or credits the task took, oldest first, `brand.ts:96`, so a retried finalize counts a render once). Since 2026-09-29 every creation path sets `source` (`manual` on `/api/generate`, `chat` for chat and MCP `generate_content`, `scheduled` for the weekly plan and the routines, `external` for MCP `save_draft`) and `brief`: the batch's notes or brief, first 500 characters, when there were any (`briefOf`); a routine's batch carries the routine id. Since 2026-09-30 `crossReview`: the cross-agent verdicts, one per reviewing agent (`{by, verdict: "ok"|"flag", notes (up to 300 characters, empty for ok), at, model}`), set by `taskFromItem` only on generated batches (never on `external`). Since 2026-09-30 also `source: "handoff"` with `parent` (`{handoff, item, conv?}`, set by the run in code, kept in the light index row too) on the tasks a handoff item's run made; such a task keeps no `brief` (Majed's brief lives on the handoff) | written by `saveTask` (`brand.ts:260`); an existing task only through `updateTask` (`brand.ts:357`) | none |
| `tasklock:{scope}:{id}` | lock | Serialises the read, change and save of one task (`updateTask`): the approve and edit route, the publish route, the chat tool `publish_item`, the judge, the placeholder card and the media engine | `brand.ts:358` | 10 s |
| `pending:{scope}`, `pendingidx:{scope}` | set / string | Ids waiting for approval, maintained by `saveTask`; marker of the first indexing | `brand.ts` | none |
| `publock:{scope}:{id}:{provider}` | lock | One publish of one item to one platform at a time (`publishOnce`, 2026-09-29) | `publish-once.ts` | 70 s from the publish route, 330 s from chat and MCP |
| `pubtry:{scope}:{id}:{provider}` | json | `{at}` written before a platform is called, removed once the post is recorded or the platform clearly refused (4xx, refused token, `x_duplicate`, a check before sending); left behind by anything else and by a kill, so the next publish asks the owner (2026-09-29). Like `publock:`, not deleted by `wipeAccount`: a time, no account data, gone within 3 days, and finding it would take every item on every platform | `publish-once.ts` | 3 days |
| `brandprop:{scope}:{id}`, `brandprop-claim:{scope}:{id}`, `brandprop-done:{scope}:{id}` | json / string / string | A brand change an agent proposed (`offers`, banned words taken out), the claim that orders answers arriving together (released after each answer), and the outcome (`applied`, `dismissed`, `stale`) a reloaded chat shows (2026-09-29); `wipeAccount` deletes all three for every id in `brandprops:{scope}` | `brand-proposals.ts`, `account.ts` | 7 days / 60 s / 7 days |
| `brandprops:{scope}` | set | Every proposal id of the brand (`proposeBrandChange` adds it before the proposal is saved), so `wipeAccount` can find the proposals, whose ids are random (2026-09-30) | `brand-proposals.ts` (`proposalKeys`), `account.ts` | none (deleted with the account) |
