Deleted by Renew, Stop, member removal, a password reset or change of that login (an owner's reset also those of the roster), `wipeMemberships` and `wipeAccount` | `booking-token.ts` | none (ends with the link) |
| `sec:bookof:{login}:{provider}` | string | The hash of that login's current link for one provider (`calcom` or `calendly`), one per login per provider (2026-10-05, rule 589); creating again replaces it and deletes the old `sec:book:{hash}`. Read by `resolveBook`, `getBookStatus` and `revokeBook` | `booking-token.ts` | none |
| `lock:book:{login}:{provider}` | lock | Owned lock (10 s) around one login's create and revoke for one provider (2026-10-05, rule 589): a held lock makes create answer `busy`; revoke tries 20 times, 50 ms apart, and then revokes anyway | `booking-token.ts` | 10 s |
| `sec:book:ext:{scope}:{provider}:{externalId}` | string | The claim on one booking (2026-10-05, rules 590-593): SET NX, 400 days, to the id the item will get, before the item is written (the `sec:spawn` pattern); the item is then written at that id under its lock, and only when no record exists. A replay, the same booking delivered at once, or a retry after a run that died finishes the same creation at the same id, so nothing is made twice and nothing is dropped. A reschedule claims the new id for the same item with SET NX (a claim naming another item is never overwritten); a move whose ids were unknown claims both. `cancelled` when a cancel came before the item was written or before the booking was ever seen (taken with SET NX and indexed; no item is made for that id after). A move whose previous id names a removed item, or is marked `cancelled`, creates a fresh item under its own claim. Released when a cap or a validation refuses the creation. `provider` is `calcom` or `calendly` from the link's record, `scope` the record's; only `externalId` comes from the payload (cleaned, at most 200 characters) | `secretary-booking.ts` | 400 days |
| `sec:bookext:{scope}` | set | Every `{provider}:{externalId}` that ever had a claim in this brand, added before the claim, so account deletion (`secretaryKeys`) finds each `sec:book:ext` key | `secretary-booking.ts`, `secretary.ts` | none (deleted with the account) |
| `sec:mprop:{scope}:{itemId}` | string | The tasks proposed for one booking item after a meeting (Reem B2b, 2026-10-05, rules 600-603): one JSON list of at most 10 `{ref, title, agent, dueAt?}`, TTL 14 days, replaced by each new proposal run. Only `approveMeeting` turns any of them into items, and it drops the set in the same step; `DELETE /api/secretary/meeting/{id}` drops it too. `scope` is the caller's; `itemId` is format-checked (`SEC_ID`) and must be a live booking item of that scope | `secretary-meeting.ts` | 14 days |
| `sec:mprops:{scope}` | set | Every item id that ever had a pending set in this brand, added before the set is written (and removed when it is dropped), so account deletion (`secretaryKeys`) finds each `sec:mprop` key | `secretary-meeting.ts`, `secretary.ts` | none (deleted with the account) |
| `sec:lock:{scope}:mprop:{itemId}` | lock | `withLock` around one approval (60 s) and around `savePending` and `dropPending` (10 s, the default): the set is read and dropped under it, so two clicks create each item once and a re-run or discard is never lost to an approve | `secretary-meeting.ts` | 60 s (approve), 10 s (pending writes) |
| `sec:mrun:{scope}:{itemId}` | string | How many transcript runs an item's proposals have had (Reem B2b Task 10, rules 604-606): an integer written after the pending set is saved, the `n` of the charge claim `meeting:{scope}:{itemId}:{n}` (`chargeOnce`), so a retry of a run that died reuses its hold and a re-run is charged. Absent means 0. No TTL: `chargeOnce` reuses a hold whatever its age, so a counter that lapsed would hand the next run for free. `secretaryKeys` lists it for every item still in `sec:all` | `secretary-meeting.ts`, `secretary.ts` | none (deleted with the account) |
| `sec:lock:{scope}:mrun:{itemId}` | lock | `proposeFromText` holds it for the whole run (60 s, `lockOwned`/`unlockOwned`, so a run whose lock lapsed cannot free the next run's): one run at a time per item, so a double click cannot charge or call the model twice | `secretary-meeting.ts` | 60 s |
| `rate:book:new:{hash}` | int | Items one booking link may create a day (60, through `allow()`), checked under the item's lock before `createItem`, so a leaked link cannot spend the owner's `sec:create` allowance (200 a day) | `secretary-booking.ts` | 1 day |
| `rate:feed:ip:{ip}`, `rate:feed:t:{hash}`, `rate:sec:feed:{login}` | int | The calendar feed's limits through `allow()` (2026-10-05, rules 578, 581): 120 an hour per client IP on the public route, counted before any store read; 60 an hour per link; 20 an hour per login for creating, renewing and stopping. The status read has its own bucket, `rate:sec:read:{login}` (`SEC_READS_PER_HOUR`), keyed on the acting login; the other Reem reads key on the account email (`rate:sec:read:{email}`) | `api/secretary/feed/[token]`, `api/secretary/feed` | 3600 s |
| `rate:book:ip:{ip}`, `rate:book:t:{hash}` | int | The booking webhook's limits through `allow()` (2026-10-05, rule 595): 1200 an hour per client IP, counted only on a miss (a well-shaped token with no live record), after the record read, and answered with the same empty 404 over the count or not, so it never refuses a live link (providers deliver every customer's webhooks from a few shared addresses; spec B2-R6 as amended); and 120 an hour per link, which answers 429 with `Retry-After: 3600`. The per-link creation cap is `rate:book:new:{hash}` above | `api/secretary/booking/[token]` | 3600 s |
| `rate:sec:book:{login}` | int | The owner's management of the booking links through `allow()` (2026-10-05, rule 596): 20 an hour per login across creating, renewing and stopping (POST and DELETE; `too_many`); the status read has its own bucket, `rate:sec:read:{login}` (`SEC_READS_PER_HOUR`) | `api/secretary/booking` | 3600 s |
| `rate:sec:blocked:{email}`, `rate:cronsec:{ip}` | int | Blocked handoff notes per account (20 a day); failed (unauthenticated) calls to the tick route per IP (60 a minute; a verified call is never counted) | `secretary-handoff.ts`, `api/cron/secretary` | window |
| `wish:votes:{itemId}` | set | Account emails that voted for a wishlist item. `itemId` is checked against the seeded ids in `changelog.ts` and the promoted ids in `wish:items` before any key is built; the email is the session's account, `u.email` from `currentUser()` (2026-09-29; any role, one vote per account) | `wishlist.ts` | none |
| `wish:mine:{email}` | set | Item ids this account voted for, so the page reads the viewer's votes in one call; deleted with the account, whose votes are taken back first | `wishlist.ts`, `account.ts` | none |
| `wish:items` | hash | id `w-{8 hex}` to `{id, title: {ar, en}, area?, at}`: suggestions the founder promoted, under a title the founder wrote | `wishlist.ts` | none |
| `wish:status` | hash | item id to `planned` / `in_progress` / `shipped`; missing means planned | `wishlist.ts` | none |
| `wish:hidden` | set | Seeded item ids the founder removed from the public list (seeded items live in code and cannot be deleted) | `wishlist.ts` | none |
| `wish:suggestions` | list | `{id, email, text, lang, at}` suggestions awaiting review. Never read by a public route; entries of a deleted account are removed | `wishlist.ts` | none |
| `rate:wishvote:{email}`, `rate:wishsuggest:{email}` | int | Wishlist vote (60 per hour) and suggestion (5 per day) allowances, through `allow()` | `api/wishlist/*` | 1 h / 24 h |
| `rate:tool:{ip}`, `toolusage:{slug}` | int | Free tool anonymous daily allowance (through `allow()`, platform IP), per-tool usage | `api/tools/[tool]/route.ts` | 24 h / none |
| `waitlist:entries`, `waitlist:emails` | list / set | Waitlist (direct Upstash client, bypasses `db.ts`) | `api/waitlist/route.ts:7-8` | none |
| `mjob:{scope}:{jobId}` | json | `MediaJob`: account, scope, language, `accountSince`, task, model, provider, credits, prompt, state, provider task id, result or `resultUrls` waiting to be copied, cost, `refunded`. `jobId` is 16 random bytes in hex | `media/jobs.ts` (`saveJob`, `getJob`) | 7 days, reset on every save |
| `mjobref:{provider}:{providerTaskId}` | json | `{scope, jobId}`: the reverse index a webhook uses. The provider task id must match `PROVIDER_TASK_ID` first | `media/jobs.ts` (`submitLocked`, `jobForHook`) | 7 days |
| `mjobs:open:{provider}` | set | `{scope}\|{jobId}` for every job not yet done or failed (split on the last `\|`, `parseOpenMember`) | `media/jobs.ts` | none; removed at the end of the job, and gone or terminal members are dropped by the sweeps and Higgsfield admission |
| `mjobs:recent` | list | Last 50 terminal jobs `{id, model, state, error, providerCredits, costUsd, at}`: no email, scope or prompt | `media/jobs.ts` (`recordOutcome`), read by `api/admin/media` | capped at 50 |
| `mflight:{scope}:{taskId}:{kind}` | lock | One render per task and kind at a time | `media/jobs.ts` (`startMedia`) | image 900 s, video 2100 s |
| `mjoblock:{jobId}` | lock | Serialises the status call, finalize and fail of one job | `media/jobs.ts` | 120 s |
| `mrefund:{jobId}` | lock, then string | Exactly-once refund marker, in two steps (`refundOnce`, `media/jobs.ts:510`, called by `failLocked` and `refundUnsaved`): a claim (`db.lock`) taken before the ledger call, then `"done"` once `refund` has returned or `"none"` when the account that paid is gone or was registered again. `"done"` reads as refunded, `"none"` as not owed; a standing claim makes the next pass throw `refund_pending` and leave the job open; a ledger error releases the claim. `finalizeLocked` treats any value as "a fail is under way" and never delivers the render (`:416`) | `media/jobs.ts` | claim 60 s (`REFUND_CLAIM_S`), then 7 days |
| `mpoll:{jobId}` | lock | At most one provider status call per job per 4 s (30 s after a failed copy) | `media/jobs.ts` (`advance`) | 4 s / 30 s |
| `msweep` | lock | At most one opportunistic sweep per 5 minutes | `media/jobs.ts` (`maybeSweep`) | 300 s |
| `mhfclean` | lock | At most one Higgsfield admission clean-up per minute | `media/jobs.ts` (`hfAdmission`) | 60 s |
| `mtrialvid:{email}` | lock | The one video a trial account keeps; deleted again when that video is refunded | `media/jobs.ts` | 14 days |
| `media:off` | json | `{[modelId]: {until, reason}}`: models switched off by the breaker (`setOff`); read once per page or start (`offModels`) | `media/jobs.ts`, `media/config.ts` | none (entries expire by `until`) |
| `media:offlock` | lock | Serialises writes to `media:off` | `media/jobs.ts` (`setOff`) | 5 s |
| `rate:media:{image\|video}:h:{email}`, `rate:media:{image\|video}:d:{email}` | int | Per-account render limits (images 30/h, 150/day; videos 6/h, 20/day) | `media/jobs.ts` (`RENDER_LIMITS`) | 3600 s / 86400 s |
| `rate:kie:create` | int | kie creates for all tenants together (18 per 10 s) | `media/jobs.ts` (`KIE_CREATES`) | 10 s |
| `rate:mfail:{modelId}` | int | Breaker window: defect-class failures of one model | `media/jobs.ts` (`trip`) | 3600 s |
| `rate:mpoll:u:{email}` | int | Polls per account (120 per minute) | `api/media/[jobId]/route.ts` | 60 s |
| `rate:mhook:{provider}:{ip}` | int | Webhook hints per IP (240 per minute) | `api/media/hook/[provider]/route.ts` | 60 s |
| `render:_all:{YYYY-MM}:m:{modelId}:{jobs\|failed\|credits\|usd_micro}`, `render:_all:{YYYY-MM}:kie_centicredits` | int | Render spend, global only, integers (`Math.round` before every `incrby`); provider and month totals are sums of the model counters (`renderSpend` in `admin-stats.ts`) | `media/jobs.ts` (`recordOutcome`) | none (like `usage:_all`) |

Media keys that carry an email (`mjob:`, `mjobref:` through its scope, `mflight:`, `mtrialvid:`, `rate:media:*`, `rate:mpoll:u:*`) all expire by TTL, within 14 days at most, and an `mjobs:open:*` member (which holds the scope) is removed when its job ends or, once the 7-day record is gone, by the next sweep; `wipeAccount` (`src/lib/account.ts`) does not delete them, and it does not delete old Blob files or images hot-linked at fal.ai either; since 2026-10-13 it does delete every disk-store file of every brand, with its index and its share of the store counters (`forgetScopeMedia`, rule 1761), and a disk error stops the wipe so a retry runs it again. The help centre and the privacy policy say so (`src/lib/help.ts`, `src/lib/legal.ts`).

Bounded lists (2026-09-18): `pushCapped` in `db.ts` trims on write. Caps in `CAPS`: chat threads 400 messages, `admin:log` 5000 (2000 until 2026-10-11, rule 1174), `support-tickets` / `affiliate-applications` / `integration-requests` 2000, `fblog:{scope}` 500, `prefs:{scope}` 100, `decisions:{scope}` 500, `wish:suggestions` 500, `govhist:{scope}` 100, `activity:{scope}` 2000, `sec:done:{scope}` 200, `sec:audit:{scope}` 500, `agent-scope:log:{day}` 5000 (2026-10-10), `handoffs:{scope}` 200 (2026-09-30; not trimmed by `pushCapped`: the proposal that pushes an id past 200 removes it by value and deletes its record with it). The task id list `tasks:{scope}` is never trimmed (owner data); `listTasks` reads the newest 200 full tasks **plus every id in `pending:{scope}`** (approvals inbox, MCP `list_inbox`, founder user card). That second half is what stops the sidebar badge from counting work the inbox cannot show: past 200 tasks an owner used to see "Approvals 50" above an inbox holding none of them. Consequences worth knowing: the pending chip is exhaustive while the approved and rejected chips are a recency window, a buried item leaves the list once it is decided (nothing is lost — `listLite` and the reports still see it), and the payload is bounded by 200 + the backlog rather than by the size of the workspace. `pendingCount` and `listTasks` share one `pendingIds` read, so they cannot drift; a pending id whose task record is gone is cleared by that read rather than waiting for the daily reconcile. The chat page's "waiting for you" card is a separate 90-day recency view of the same queue and is deliberately not exhaustive.

### 3.x Account security keys (2026-10-16, rules 1930-1956)

| Key | Type | Purpose | Written by | TTL |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `mfa:{login}` | json | Two-step enrolment `{enc, kid, rc, at}`: the TOTP secret sealed with `CONNECT_SECRET` (label `nashr-mfa-totp`), hashed recovery codes | `src/lib/mfa.ts` | none |
| `mfa:pending:{login}` | json | A setup waiting for its first code | `src/lib/mfa.ts` | 15 min |
| `mfachal:{sha256(id)}` | json | Sign-in challenge `{email, at, tries, via, next?}`, its id in the `thakeen_mfa` cookie | `src/lib/mfa.ts` | 5 min |
| `mfaotp:used:{sha256(login)}:{step}` | string | A TOTP step already used by this login (SET NX) | `src/lib/mfa.ts` | 120 s |
| `rate:mfacode:ip:{ip}`, `rate:mfacode:em:{sha256(login)}`, `rate:mfapw:{login}` | int | Code tries (5 per 15 min) and wrong passwords (10 per hour) | `src/lib/mfa.ts`, `api/account/2fa` | window |
| `devs:{login}` | hash | The login's sessions: field = the device id (a hash of the session key), value `{k, ua, ip, at}` where `k` is the session's own Redis key (`session:h:*`, already a hash of the cookie, so it opens nothing); 50 at most | `src/lib/devices.ts` (from `createSession`) | 30 days |
| `emailchange:{login}`, `emailchange:tries:{login}` | json, int | A pending address change `{to, hash, at}` (only a hash of the code) and its wrong tries | `src/lib/email-change.ts` | 15 min |
| `rate:emailpw:{login}`, `rate:emailchange:start:{login}` | int | Wrong passwords (10 per hour) and starts (5 per hour) | `api/account/email` | 1 hour |
| `gsi:state:{sha256(state)}` | json | A Google sign-in in flight `{nonce, verifier, mode, next, lang, ref, at, login?}`, taken once | `src/lib/google-signin.ts` | 10 min |
| `google:{sub}`, `googlesub:{login}` | string | The Google link, both ways (one Google account per login) | `src/lib/google-signin.ts` | none |
| `rate:gsi:ip:{ip}`, `rate:googlepw:{login}` | int | Google starts per address (30 per 15 min) and wrong unlink passwords (10 per hour) | `api/auth/google/start`, `api/account/google` | window |

`wipeAccount` deletes every one of these for the owner and each member login (rule 1954). Since multi-workspace M1 (2026-10-19, rule 2126 M1 half, `flows.md` section 89 item 7): the owner's pack goes only when the owner person holds nothing else (always, with `MULTI_ORG` off), and a member's goes with its last membership (`deleteMembership` through `wipeMemberships`); a member who still belongs to or owns another account keeps its pack, login index and devices.

## 4. Request lifecycles

### (a) Marketing page
1. Browser requests `/`. `proxy.ts:38` redirects to `/{lang}` (`:44`; and `/app` to `/{lang}/app`, rule 681). Since 2026-10-14 (rule 1874) a public address written without its language, a first segment in `PUBLIC_SEGMENTS` (`affiliates`, `agents`, `ai-marketing-agent`, `blog`, `compare`, `customers`, `enterprise`, `for`, `help`, `pricing`, `privacy`, `resources`, `terms`, `tools`, `updates`: the public folders under `src/app/[lang]`), answers the same 307 to `/{lang}{path}{query}` with `Vary: Cookie, Accept-Language`; `/api`, `/m`, `/p`, `/oauth`, `/llms.txt`, `/robots.txt`, `/sitemap.xml`, the manifest, `/icons`, `/_next` and `/.well-known` never move. `pickLang` (exported) reads the `thakeen_lang` cookie (or the old `nashr_lang`), then the first Accept-Language entry, and treats `en-SA`, `en-AE`, `en-KW`, `en-QA`, `en-BH` and `en-OM` as Arabic (rule 1873). An address outside the language tree (`/xyz`, `/ar-sa/pricing`, `/admin`: a first segment that is not `ar` or `en`, not in `ROOT_SEGMENTS` (`api`, `fonts`, `icons`, `m`, `oauth`, `p`), with no dot and no leading `_` or `.`) is rewritten, the address bar unchanged, to `/{lang}/__missing` with `Vary: Cookie, Accept-Language` (`strayPath`, rule 1891), so `[lang]/[...rest]` answers 404 with `src/app/[lang]/not-found.tsx` inside the visitor's language and navigation. Before this, `[lang]` took the segment as a language, its layout's `notFound()` had no root `not-found` above it, and the framework's untitled English page showed. `src/app/global-not-found.tsx` (`experimental.globalNotFound`, rule 1875, a whole Arabic right-to-left document with the English beside it) stays as the fallback for an address that matches no route at all. Other marketing paths pass through the proxy untouched (it matches every path since the staging lock; off staging it only acts on `/`, `/app`, app paths and, for `/api/*`, the cross-site check, `proxy.ts:33-37`) and go straight to the route.
2. `src/app/[lang]/layout.tsx` and the page render on the server from static dictionaries. No session lookup, no Redis access.
3. Public forms call `/api/support`, `/api/affiliates`, `/api/tools/{tool}` from the browser. Only the tools route calls a model (anonymous limit 5 per IP per day, `api/tools/[tool]/route.ts:25-26`).

### (b) Authenticated app page
1. Request to `/{lang}/app/...` goes through the proxy, which sets `x-pathname` (`proxy.ts:48`).
2. `app/layout.tsx` calls `currentUser()`, which resolves the session to the ACCOUNT, the owner's `User`, with `actor`, `brandId` and `scope` set, or `null` (section 4(f)). Since multi-workspace M1 (2026-10-19, rule 2135) that account is the ACTIVE account the session record names (`acct`), re-checked against the person and the membership on every request; the request itself never names it.
3. Anonymous and not a public path: redirect to `/{lang}/app/login` (`layout.tsx:28`). The redirect is skipped when `x-pathname` is empty.
4. Each page repeats its own guard (`currentUser()` then `redirect`) and reads data with `sc(u)` or, on several pages, `u.email` (see `permissions.md` section 2).
5. Client components then call JSON APIs with the same cookie.

### (c) Streaming chat turn with a tool call that generates content
1. `ChatPanel` posts `{text, conv, agent, stream:true}` to `POST /api/chat` with header `x-lang` (`src/components/app/ChatPanel.tsx:563`).
2. Route checks `currentUser()` (`api/chat/route.ts:28`), trims text to 4000 chars (`:31`, 400 `empty` when nothing is left), validates `agent` with `isAgentId`. Then, before anything is saved or a stream is opened: an ended trial (`trialOver`, since 2026-10-01, rule 330: 402 `trial_over` with `limit:"trial"` and a message linking the Subscription page, before any ceiling or allowance is touched), the per-account burst limit (`chatRefusal`: `chatCaps(u)`, 30 an hour and 150 a day on a paid plan, 10 an hour and 30 a day on a trial, `rate:chat:h:{email}` and `rate:chat:d:{email}`, the day counted only for a turn the hour allowed; 429 `slow_down` with `retry` and a message, rule 331), and since 2026-10-01 the plan's monthly chat allowance (`reserveChatTurn`, `:38`, `src/lib/chat-allowance.ts`): the message is counted on the account's counter with INCRBY; past the allowance it is taken back and the answer is 402 `{error: "chat_allowance", chat, credits}` (paused), unless the owner of a paid plan turned "continue on credits" on and the balance is at least `chatOverflowHold()` (5 credits, the most one message can cost), in which case the message goes ahead with nothing taken from the balance (the turn's own tools may spend it), or 402 `chat_credits` when the balance is below that. Only then it opens a `ReadableStream` SSE response with a 10 s `: ping` heartbeat (`:59`) and runs the turn through `runChatTurn` (`:61`), which settles it on every path.
3. `chat()` (`src/lib/chat.ts:363`): takes the turn's deadline first (`:365`, `deadlineFor(CHAT_MAX_DURATION_S)` = start + 285 s, since the route passes none), pushes the user message to `chat:{scope}[:{conv}]`, loads brand, this agent's preferences (`listPreferencesFor`), memory files, file names, connections, the thread (sent to the model through the history guard, `:401`, since 2026-10-01: `trimHistory` keeps the newest 16 messages that fit 24,000 characters, each cut to 6,000, a thread that starts with the first-run onboarding's assistant messages as it is, and `fitFirstCall` then drops the oldest until the first call has at least 4,000 output tokens) and the agent's recent feedback (`feedbackFor` with chat thumbs, in the uncached block) and, since 2026-09-30, the handoff notes other agents left for this one (`handoffNotesFor`, passed to `system` as the `handoffBlock` data block, uncached) in one `Promise.all` (`:391`), builds the system prompt (`system`, `:229`, called at `:395`; its uncached block starts with `nowLine`: the current date and time in the brand's zone), and, for each of at most `CHAT_MAX_CALLS` (5) calls, first works out its `max_tokens` (since 2026-10-01: the first call's from `fitFirstCall`, every later call's from `nextMaxTokens`, `:415`: what is left of the $0.14 per-message ceiling `CHAT_TURN_MAX_USD` after what the turn already cost and an upper estimate of this call's input, at most 8,000; below 2,048 no call is made and the turn ends with `CAPPED_REPLY`, or, when even the first call has no room, with `LARGE_REPLY`, which says the workspace is too large for one message and is not counted), then calls `client.messages.stream` with `CHAT_MODEL` (`claude-opus-5`) and the fifteen tools (`:425`; the nine content tools, Reem's five free to-do tools (four since 2026-09-29, `agenda_next` added on 2026-09-30) and, since 2026-09-30, the agents' handoff note `handoff_note` (a note that runs nothing, refused over MCP; not Majed's handoffs), all offered to every agent like the others; Majed's turns add `propose_handoff` and, since 2026-10-03, Reem's add `contact_away`, `toolsFor`; since main's security gates were merged in, the memory files sit in one fenced `workspace_files` block). Usage is metered per agent (`:432`), and each call's cost is added to the turn's total, which the route reads as `ChatTurn.usd` and never sends to the browser (`runChatTurn` strips it). A call the model ended at `max_tokens` (`:439`) is never acted on: its tool calls do not run, and its text is kept with `CUT_NOTE`.
4. The model returns a `tool_use` for `generate_content` or `draft_items`. `runTool` (`:581`, with the turn's deadline) first refuses, for `draft_items`, any type outside `DRAFT_TYPES` in code (`isDraftType`, `chat.ts:622`, since 2026-09-30, rule 250) before any read or charge, then hands the batch to `generateBatch` (with that deadline, which bounds the batch's cross-agent review) (`src/lib/generate-batch.ts`, `flows.md` 8.13, which also refuses a small ask of any other type): requires a brand, loads governance, rejects expired trials, `charge(user.email, cost, {scope, agent: agentForTask(type), cap: gov.agentCaps[agent]})` under `lock:user:{email}` (`credits.ts:72`), calls `generate()` (`noor.ts:174`: Opus generation, then Sonnet reviewer `critique`, optional dash stripping, then the cross-agent review when the type has reviewers and `gov.crossReview` is on). At the agent's monthly cap the tool returns a sentence and pushes `limit:agent_cap` (`batchRefused`, `chat.ts:545`), and the reply carries `limit: "agent_cap"`. On a thrown error the credits are refunded with the charge's receipt, which also lowers the agent's counter, and the error becomes an `is_error` tool result. A `generate_image` call renders against the same deadline and starts nothing with less than 60 s of it left (`CHAT_RENDER_MIN_MS`, `:836`); its charge goes through `startMedia` with the item's owning agent and cap, like the image route.
5. Each item becomes a task in `taskFromItem` with the status `initialStatus` gives on the item's type, channel, title and body (`approved` when `gov.autoApprove[type]`, no brand rule is broken, an unfilled placeholder included, and no reviewing agent flagged it, else `pending`), and the batch is saved together (`Promise.all`) as `task:{scope}:{id}`, ids pushed to `tasks:{scope}`. `judgeLater` schedules scoring after the response (`judge.ts:192`), which later writes `quality` on the task through `updateTask` (`judge.ts:145`, on the task as stored then; an automatic fail sends an item only auto-approval passed back to `pending`) and may push a notification.
6. Tool result goes back to the model (up to 5 calls, each within what is left of the ceiling). The final text is pushed to history with `items` cards (and, in Majed's turns, the handoff card, whose proposal is marked shown only after that push). Since 2026-10-01 the turn is then settled (`settleChatTurn` in `chat-allowance.ts`): a message inside the allowance stays counted; one that threw, made no model call or cost nothing (paused, mock) is given back; an over-allowance message is billed after the reply its metered cost at the floor rate, at most `chatOverflowHold()` (5) credits and at most what the balance still holds, the remainder under one credit carried (`settleChatOverflow`, `credits.ts`), and the credits billed are added to `usage:{email}:{YYYY-MM}:chat_cred`; a failed one is not billed. When the loop ends without a closing answer, a limit a tool hit still raises its dialog (`withLimit`). The reply is sent as `{type:"message", message, credits, limit, chat, charged}` (`api/chat/route.ts:64`): `chat` the account's `ChatStatus` after the turn, `charged` the credits this message cost (0 inside the allowance). `track(email,"chat")` and `touchConversation` run before the final event.
7. If the client disconnects, work continues and the reply is still saved (`api/chat/route.ts:57`), and the turn is settled the same way.

### (d) Cron run
1. An hPanel cron job (2.6) issues `GET /api/cron/{daily|weekly|golden|media|routines|evening|stores}` with `Authorization: Bearer $CRON_SECRET` (Reem's tick is a `POST /api/cron/secretary` every 15 minutes with the same bearer from hPanel, or a signed Upstash QStash call; section 2.6).
2. Route compares the bearer to the env value with `sameSecret` (`cron/daily/route.ts:139`, `cron/weekly/route.ts:15`, `cron/golden/route.ts:12`, `cron/media/route.ts:17`, `cron/routines/route.ts:34`, `cron/evening/route.ts:25`); 401 otherwise or when the variable is unset.
3. `allUsers()` (`schedule.ts:256`, the `users` of `dailyRoster`, `:237`) expands every email in `users:all` into one `User` per active brand with `scope` set, skipping `suspended` accounts. The daily cron also takes `dailyRoster`'s `idle` scopes (archived brands, suspended accounts) and runs only Reem's purge there (`purgeIdle`). The media cron does not fan out per user: it reads the open job sets.
4. Daily: `renewIfDue`, `sweepOnboardHolds` (once per account, on its default brand's entry, since 2026-09-30), `sweepWeeklyHolds` (the same entry, since 2026-10-01, rule 337), `secretaryDaily`, nudge email, `runLifecycle`, `runDaily`, `followUps`, optional brief email; for an idle scope, only `purgeIdle`. Weekly: `runWeekly` per scope, charging credits and refunding on failure. Golden: `runGolden("cron")` under `golden:lock`. Media: `sweepMedia` (section (e), step 6). Routines: `dueRoutines` turns the users into (scope, routine) items that are on and due, and `runRoutine` runs each, charging the owning agent and refunding on failure.
5. JSON summary is returned; weekly returns `runCronBatch`'s report, whose `results` are keyed by `u.scope ?? u.email` (`cron/weekly/route.ts:17,20`); media returns `{open, advanced, done, failed, dropped, skipped}`.

### (e) Media render (image or video)
1. `POST /api/tasks/{id}/image {model?}` or `POST /api/tasks/{id}/video {model}` (`maxDuration = 120`). One deadline for the whole request, `deadlineFor(120)` = start + 105 s (`src/lib/media/keys.ts`); every step gets `min(its own maximum, time left)`. `currentUser` (401; any role), then `getTask(sc(u), id)` and `getBrand(sc(u))` (404).
2. `renderImage` / `renderVideo` (`src/lib/media/start.ts`) refuse before any charge: trial ended (402 `trial_over`), a model id that is not a catalogue id of that kind (400 `bad_model`; the image route defaults to `img-fast`, the video route has no default), a task type outside `IMAGE_TYPES` / `VIDEO_TYPES` (400 `wrong_type`), a trial account asking for a video model outside `TRIAL_VIDEO_MODELS` (402 `plan_required`), a model that is not available (`modelAvailable`: provider keys, the Blob store for kie and Higgsfield, `MEDIA_MODELS_ON`, not in `media:off`; 409 `model_unavailable`, except `img-fast`, which falls back to the uncharged placeholder card), and Photo to motion without our own image (409 `needs_image`). The placeholder card is written through `updateTask` on the task as stored, and only while it has no artwork: an item that already has a generated image, or gets one while the placeholder waits for the task lock, keeps it and the answer is 409 `model_unavailable` with nothing charged (`src/lib/media/start.ts:63-68`); one that finishes after the card was written replaces it, since the card leaves `task.mediaJob` alone.
3. `startMedia` (`src/lib/media/jobs.ts`): the owning agent's monthly cap and the balance pre-check (402 `agent_cap` / `no_credits`), flight lock `mflight` (409 `in_progress` with the running job), account limits (429 `too_many`), the trial video marker (402 `trial_video_used`), the kie create throttle or Higgsfield admission (429 `busy` / `one_open`; every 429 here carries `retryAfter`, which `mediaResponse` sends as `Retry-After`: the account's hour or day, kie's 10 s window, else `BUSY_RETRY_S` 60, rule 680), then `charge` with the item's owning agent and its cap (`flows.md` section 8 steps 9 and 12). The job is saved `queued` together with its open-set member and `task.mediaJob[kind]` (through `updateTask`) in one round whose three writes all settle; a failure there refunds at once through `refundOnce` (502 `media_failed` with the balance), and if that refund fails too the job, its member and the pointer are written again, the flight lock stays held and the answer is 502 `media_pending`, so the next poll or sweep ends the job `submit_lost` with the refund. Then the prompt (Sonnet, at most 20 s, or the fixed template) and one submit, never retried, under the job lock.
4. fal answers in the request: the image is copied to the disk store (or hot-linked), the task gets it, 200. kie and Higgsfield answer a task id: the job becomes `running` with its `mjobref` pointer, 202, and the inbox polls. A submit refused, failed or out of time fails the job and refunds it once.
5. `GET /api/media/{jobId}` (`maxDuration = 120`; any role): `getJob(sc(u), jobId)` (404 for another account's id, no provider call), 120 polls per minute per account, then `advance`: at most one provider status call per job per 4 s (`mpoll`) under the job lock, a finished result copied through `safeFetch` (https on every hop) to the disk store under the job's own scope and written to the task (`updateTask`) before the job is saved `done`, a failure, a content refusal or a render past its max age (image 10 minutes, video 30) failed and refunded exactly once (`refundOnce` and its `mrefund` marker). A task write that fails at finalize leaves the job `running` with its result for the next pass, and `task.mediaApplied` keeps that retry from counting the render twice.
6. `POST /api/media/hook/{provider}` is a hint: per-IP limit, 64 KB body cap (refused after Next has buffered up to 6 MB of it for the proxy; rules 693 and 774), the kie signature when `KIE_WEBHOOK_HMAC_KEY` is set; only the provider task id is read, the job is found through `mjobref` under its stored scope, and `advance` runs in `after()` without `force`, sharing the poll throttle. The start and poll routes schedule `maybeSweep` in `after()`, and `/api/cron/media` runs `sweepMedia` daily for whatever is left. flows.md section 23 has the details.

### (f) Resolving who is acting (2026-09-29)
0. Since 2026-10-18 (account-id R1, flows 88, rule 1984): `createSession` also writes `pid` and `acct`, and `resolveSession` reads `login:{email}`, `user:{acct or email}`, `member:{email}` and `activebrand:{email}` in its second round. A session whose `pid` the index no longer names, or whose `acct` is not the login's workspace, resolves to `null`. Accounts are read by id (`getAccount`); the address only finds the login (`getLogin`).
1. `currentUser()` reads cookie `thakeen_session` and `session:h:{sha256(token)}`, then the legacy `session:{token}` (`sessionRecord`, rule 1345; `{email, at, imp?, kp?, as?}`; an `imp: "key"` session is checked against the current key and its key session first, one more read, and renews that key session at most once a minute), then, in one parallel round, `user:{login}`, `member:{login}` and `activebrand:{login}` (`resolveSession` in `src/lib/auth.ts`).
2. Owner login (the `user:` record exists; it wins should a member record exist too): `null` when suspended or when `at` predates `sessionsAfter`. Otherwise the record itself, `scope = scopeFor(owner, brandId)`, and actor `{kind:"owner", role:"owner"}`, or `{kind:"founder", role:"owner", impersonating: imp}` when the session carries `imp`.
3. Member login: `null` when the record is disabled, its role or `memberOf` is malformed, the session carries `imp`, or `at` predates the member's `sessionsAfter`. Then `user:{memberOf}` and `brands:{memberOf}` in one round: `null` when the owner is missing or suspended, when `at` predates the owner's `teamSessionsAfter`, or when the member's `acceptedAt` is earlier than the owner's `createdAt`. Otherwise the OWNER's record, with `brandId` = the member's own active brand when it is one of the owner's active brands (else `default`), `scope = scopeFor(owner, brandId)`, and actor `{kind:"member", email, name, lang, role}`.
3a. The verification gate (2026-10-08, rule 751). In the owner branch, an owner record without `emailVerifiedAt` resolves to `null` for `currentUser()` unless the session carries `imp` (a founder impersonation); `currentUserUnverified()` runs the same resolution with the gate off, and only the app layout, the verify page and the three verify routes call it. A member session is refused in both resolvers while its owner has no `emailVerifiedAt` (rule 767): the account spends nothing until the owner verifies, and a member cannot prove the owner's address.
4. Every caller keeps using `sc(u)`, `u.email` (the account) and `charge(u.email, ...)` unchanged; role checks read `u.actor` through `can()` and `forbid()` (`src/lib/roles.ts`), which refuse a `User` without an actor. `saveUser` strips `actor`, `scope` and `brandId`, so none is ever persisted.
5. MCP (multi-workspace M1, rules 2120 and 2131: the owner's person is read in the same round, `person:{acct}` and `activebrand:{pid}:{aid}` beside the account; one more round only for an account with `ownerPid`; a suspended person is refused; actor `{kind:"token", pid: ownerOf(u), ...}`; the token never reads a browser session's `acct`). `userFromBearer` resolves `mcptoken:{sha256(token)}` (falling back to a legacy raw key, renamed on first use; an OAuth access token also needs its grant to exist and list it) to an owner account only (a member login's email is refused, and so is an owner who has not verified the address, rule 753), with the owner's active brand and actor `{kind:"token", role:"owner"}`. The cron's `allUsers()` builds users without an actor.
6. Founder console: `adminActor(req)` answers `"key"` for the admin key (header `x-admin-key`) or a live key session (cookie `nashr_admin` = a random id; `adminKeySession()` looks up `adminsession:{sha256(id)}`, checks the stored fingerprint against the current key and both limits (15 minutes idle, 12 h at most), and renews it at most once a minute), the founder's email when `isFounderActor(currentUser())` (actor kind `owner`, actor email equal to the account email, in `ADMIN_EMAILS`), else `null`; `isAdmin` and `isAdminCookie` follow it.
7. Persons, memberships and ownership (2026-10-19, multi-workspace membership release M1 expand, branch `claude/wave3-m1-expand`, rules 2115, 2134, 2136; read by `resolveSession`, `getLogin`, `userFromBearer` and the team writers). `ownerOf(u)` (`src/lib/membership.ts`) is the only answer to "who owns this account": `u.ownerPid`, else the legacy owner `acct(u)`; the owner is never a membership row. `getPerson(pid)` (`src/lib/person.ts`) reads `person:{pid}`, `user:{pid}` and `member:{pid}` in ONE round: the `person:` row when it parses, else the legacy owner record when `ownerOf` names `pid` (credential fields only: `email, name, lang, passHash, emailVerifiedAt, sessionsAfter, devicesAfter, createdAt`; `suspended` stays an account field), else the legacy member record when `memberPid` is `pid` (`createdAt` = `acceptedAt`). A `person:` row loses to its legacy record when that record is provably newer, and `person stale` is logged without values: born after the person's `createdAt` (a leftover row of an account deleted under a rolled-back build, maybe another human), the legacy record is read alone and the leftover lends nothing but a set `suspended` (fail closed); a later `sessionsAfter` (the same human, changed by a rolled-back build: a new password, a new address with its verification cleared), every field the legacy record type carries is read from it even when absent there (the `PersonRow` defaults for `name`, `lang`, `createdAt` never replace a real value), except the revocation stamps `sessionsAfter` and `devicesAfter`, which keep the later of both records (a missing or older legacy stamp never un-revokes), and only the person-only fields (`suspended`, `trialUsedAt`, and a member's `emailVerifiedAt`) stay from the row. `getMembership(aid, pid)` reads `mbr:{aid}:{pid}`, `member:{pid}` and `SISMEMBER members:{aid} pid` in ONE round: the `mbr:` row with the roster entry, else the legacy member record when its `memberOf` is `aid` and the roster lists `pid`; a row without its roster entry, a roster entry without a row, a row naming another key and a row claiming `owner` all read as none. When both records exist for the same account and disagree, the stricter value of each field is read (`member` over `admin`, paused over enabled, the later `acceptedAt`) and `membership stale` is logged; this overrides a plain `mbr:` precedence on purpose (plan reconciliation 18): a resume or promotion made only on the legacy record by a rolled-back build is not honoured and the owner repeats it. `roleIn(u, pid, m)`: `owner` from `ownerOf` only, else the role of `m` when it is this person's membership in `acct(u)` and not paused, else null; `membershipHolds(u, m)` keeps today's member guards (role, this account, not paused, `acceptedAt` not before the account's `createdAt`). `listOrgs(pid)` reads `orgs:{pid}` (a hint) and then, in one `pmap` round, the account and the membership of every entry, skipping entries the forward records do not back; owned first, then the oldest membership. `homeAccountOf(person)` names the account the legacy per-login keys belong to: a legacy owner's own, a legacy member's `memberOf`, else the first of `listOrgs`. `personFromRaw` and `membershipFromRaw` are the same precedence as pure functions, for a resolver that reads the keys in its own round. `isPersonSuspended` (`src/lib/suspension.ts`) reads a person's `suspended` with `isSuspended`'s meaning. `withPersonLock` takes `lock:person:{pid}` through `withNamedLock` (`src/lib/locks.ts`), the lock `team.ts` now shares.
8. Sessions on persons and memberships (2026-10-19, multi-workspace M1, branch `claude/wave3-m1-expand`, rules 2111, 2119, 2120, 2131, 2132, 2135). `resolveSession` dispatches on the session record. A record with `pid` and `acct` (every session written from R1 on) goes to `resolveIds`: after the session read, ONE parallel round of `person:{pid}`, `user:{pid}` (skipped when `acct` is `pid`: the same command), `member:{pid}`, `login:{email}`, `user:{acct}`, `mbr:{acct}:{pid}` and `SISMEMBER members:{acct} pid` (neither when `acct` is `pid`: the person's own account, held only as its owner), `brands:{acct}` (a store error there alone does not end the request: the stored choice is used unchecked, as an owner's was before R1), `activebrand:{pid}:{acct}` and the legacy `activebrand:{email}`. It resolves only when the index still names `pid` (no index: `pid` is the address); `personFromRaw` gives a person holding this address, not suspended (`isPersonSuspended`), the session not older than its `sessionsAfter`/`devicesAfter`; the account exists at `acct`, is not suspended and is verified (rule 751 for an owner, with `currentUserUnverified` and an impersonation passing; rule 767 for a member, never passing); `roleIn` gives a role on these records, and for a membership `membershipHolds` (role, this account, not paused, `acceptedAt` not before the account's `createdAt`) and the session is not older than the account's `teamSessionsAfter`; an impersonation resolves only as the owner, until its `exp`. Then `u` is the account, `u.actor = {kind, pid, home, email, name, lang, role}` (`home`: the account is the person's legacy home, a legacy owner's own or a legacy member's `memberOf`, so the legacy per-login keys belong to it), `u.brandId` the person's active brand in this account (in the home account the legacy key when set, else `activebrand:{pid}:{acct}`; elsewhere only the new key: M1 writes both keys alike in the home account, so they differ only after a rolled-back build wrote the legacy one, and that later choice wins) when it is one of the account's active brands, else `default`. A record without them (written before R1) goes to `resolveLegacy`: today's rounds and checks unchanged (an owner one round after the session read, a member two), with `actor.pid`/`home` derived from the legacy records; it is never rewritten on read. Such a session skips the person-suspension check: nothing writes a person's `suspended` before release M3, and M3 (or M4, should it refuse sessions without `pid`) adds the check to `resolveLegacy` before any writer of it ships (`flows.md` 89.1; until then a pre-R1 session of a person-suspended member would survive to its 30-day end). In both paths the account comes only from the session record, re-checked on every request; no route reads an account or person id from the request (rule 2112), and `tests/multi-org-isolation.test.ts` (rule 2110) checks every key a workspace request touches against what the person holds at that moment. From M2 the workspace handle in the URL and the `x-thakeen-ws` header are a second guard on the same session account (decision 7 of the spec). `currentPerson()` (beside `currentUser()`) answers the session's person, checked the same way, and the active account when it still resolves; null for an impersonation. No route calls it in M1. `activeBrandIn(pid, aid, homeLogin)`/`setActiveBrandIn` (`src/lib/brands.ts`) replace `activeBrandId`/`setActiveBrand` everywhere but `resolveLegacy`; `brandChooser(u)` and `ownerChooser(u)` name whose key a write moves.

## 5. Trust boundaries

| Boundary | What crosses it | Controls present in code |
|---|---|---|
| Browser to app server | Cookies `thakeen_session`, `nashr_admin`, `thakeen_return`, `thakeen_impersonate`, and since 2026-10-01 `thakeen_partner` (set by `GET /p/{code}` for 30 days: `CODE.<click ms>`, read and spent at signup) (all `httpOnly`, `sameSite=lax`, `secure` in production, `path=/`), `thakeen_lang` and, since rule 1204, `thakeen_theme` (both client-set, not httpOnly; no server code reads `thakeen_theme`: the head script in `src/app/[lang]/layout.tsx` and `ThemeScope` read it in the browser); JSON bodies; query strings | Session lookup per request; no CSRF token, but since 2026-10-08 a cross-site check in the proxy (`src/lib/csrf.ts`, rules 690-693): a POST, PUT, PATCH or DELETE to `/api` whose `Sec-Fetch-Site` is not `same-origin` or `none` (when that header is present it decides alone), or, from a browser that sends no `Sec-Fetch-Site`, whose `Origin` is `null` or not this site's (`appOrigin()` or the request's own host), is refused with 403 `cross_site` before any route reads it, which also closes a `text/plain` form carrying JSON, login and logout forced from another site, and posts from a same-site sibling such as `staging.thakeen.com` that `sameSite=lax` lets through; a request with neither header passes (only browsers send them, and the callers that send neither hold no browser cookie). Exempt (the proxy runs on them, after the staging lock, but skips the check): `/api/mcp`, `/api/oauth/token`, `/api/oauth/register` (bearer or client credentials, CORS `*`), `/api/media/hook/`, `/api/secretary/booking/`, `/api/billing/paymob/` (provider callbacks proven by a signature or re-checked with the provider) and `/api/cron/` (the cron bearer and QStash); none of their state-changing handlers reaches the session or a cookie, which `tests/csrf.test.ts` pins through the import graph; input length clamps per route; rate limits on login, signup, forgot, reset, the admin key (form and header), the public write endpoints (support, affiliates, waitlist), chat, onboarding, file writes, CSV imports, anonymous free tools, the team invite, preview and accept routes, and media renders and polls (see `permissions.md`). `clientIp` (`rate.ts:55`) takes Vercel's headers (`x-vercel-forwarded-for`, `x-real-ip`) only on Vercel (`VERCEL=1`) and otherwise only the LAST `x-forwarded-for` hop, so a caller cannot pick its own bucket on Hostinger (rule 773). Every role check is in code (`forbid`/`can` after `currentUser()`); a hidden control is display only. |
| App server to Redis | Every record, including password hashes, session tokens (clear), personal MCP tokens (clear, both directions), OAuth client secrets (clear), encrypted connector credentials | TLS REST token from env. Tenant isolation is purely by key naming (scope prefix). No Redis ACL separation. |
| App server to model provider (Anthropic) | Brand file, workspace memory files, owner preferences, the owning agent's recent feedback (decision counts, typed reasons, judge notes, chat thumbs-down reasons), chat history, crawled site text, imported product text, task bodies | Recent feedback is escaped with `asData` inside `<recent_feedback>`, labelled as data, capped in count and length, and only in the uncached prompt block (`feedback.ts`). Reem's `agenda_get` returns the owner's items with each title and note JSON-quoted under a line saying they are data; private notes are never returned to a model; an agent's "done" is quoted as a claim, next to the fact code read from the linked inbox task. Since 2026-10-03 Reem's `contact_away` writes nothing: its card's absence is saved only when a person presses Save, through the away list's own checks, and the Working days change only through `PUT /api/secretary/calendar` (admin+); neither is reachable over MCP. The brand's time zone reaches the prompt only as the allow-listed zone `zoneOf` returns. Model output is parsed as JSON or text; chat tool inputs are schema-constrained with `strict: true` (`chat.ts:103`) but not re-validated server-side except where noted; MCP `tools/call` arguments are not checked against the schema at all, so Reem's tools decide in code which arguments each tool may set (`toolFields`). Majed's `propose_handoff` input is re-read in code (`parseProposal`: only the types, from a closed list, the briefs and `why`); each item's agent, cost, status and scope come from code, and his brief reaches the next agent, its reviewer, the cross-reviewers and the judge only as a labelled `<lead_brief>` data block, never as owner notes (2026-09-30). Content from crawled sites and imported catalogs enters prompts and the `products.md` memory file, so third-party text can influence agent behaviour including tool calls. |
| App server to third-party platform APIs | Connector credentials (decrypted in memory), content to publish, user-supplied base URLs | Credentials encrypted at rest with AES-256-GCM, key = SHA-256 of `CONNECT_SECRET`, falling back to `WAITLIST_ADMIN_KEY`, then to a fixed dev string, outside production only; production throws when `CONNECT_SECRET` is unset (`connectors/store.ts:19-29`). Base URLs for WordPress, WooCommerce, Odoo, PrestaShop and crawl/catalog targets are user-supplied and fetched server-side through `safeFetch` (http(s) only; loopback, private, link-local, metadata and multicast targets refused; every redirect hop checked again; DNS rebinding between the check and the connection remains). |
| App server to email (Resend) | Recipient address, email text and, since 2026-10-08, an HTML part built in `src/emails/` (every value escaped, every link only to `appOrigin()`) | API key from env. Click tracking off on the sending domain, so the token links in the HTML part are not rewritten through Resend's redirect (rule 546). |
| App server to fal.ai, kie.ai, Higgsfield | Image and video prompts (written by Sonnet from the post and the brand, or a fixed brand template while Claude is paused), a 24-hour signed link to our own copy of the post's image as a reference or first frame (`mediaImageFor`: only a real generated image on our disk store or a legacy Blob copy, `isOurMediaUrl`; signed in `registry.ts` at the send, rule 1755), and our public hook URL (`callbackUrl`) | Keys only in `Authorization` headers, never in a URL; fixed hosts; a provider task id is format-checked (`PROVIDER_TASK_ID`) before it enters a URL path; submits are never retried (no idempotency key); adapter errors are fixed codes (`ProviderError`) and response bodies are never logged or put in an error. Higgsfield's terms allow it to use inputs and outputs to improve its models and kie.ai's to use content to improve its service; the privacy policy says so. |
| Provider result hosts to app server | Result files named in a provider's own status answer (or fal's reply); URLs in webhook bodies are never read | Downloaded only through `safeFetch` in `copyToStore` with `httpsOnly`: https on the first hop and on every redirect (a redirect to http is refused before it is requested), capped (image 25 MB, video 100 MB, on the declared length and on a running total), magic bytes decide the type, images re-encoded to JPEG with `sharp` at its default `failOn`, every step bounded by the request's deadline. |
| App server to Hostinger disk | Generated images (JPEG) and clips (MP4); the console probe file and the build sentinel | `MEDIA_DIR` from env, absolute and outside the app folder. Names are 128 random bits with no email, scope or task id; the owner is in the index `mfile:{scope}:{name}`, written with the job's own scope. Folders 0700, files 0600. Deleted with the account (rule 1761). |
| App server to Composio, for a customer's drive (2026-10-13, branch only) | A search query, a file id and name to download, or one of the workspace's own files to upload (as a 24-hour signed link Composio fetches) | Started only by a person's click in the app (`api/drives`), never by chat, MCP or an agent. The account is the one stored for `sc(u)`. A downloaded file is untrusted: temporary `s3url` through `safeFetch` (https on every hop, capped), base64 measured before decoding, magic bytes checked, images re-encoded, text must be UTF-8. Writes are new files only (Dropbox `add` + `autorename`, OneDrive `rename`, Google always creates); no delete, move or share tool is in the catalog. |
| Browser, Meta, providers, Composio to `/m/{name}` | Our media files | A session in `sc(u)` whose index holds the name, or a valid unexpired signature for that one name; the same bare 404 otherwise. 1200 requests an hour per IP, at most 6 full-body streams over 10 MB at once, `Content-Security-Policy: sandbox`, `nosniff`, `noindex`. |
| Providers to `/api/media/hook/{provider}` (inbound, public) | JSON callback bodies from anyone who knows the URL | A hint only: 404 for an unknown or unconfigured provider; per-IP `allow("mhook:{provider}:{ip}", 240, 60)`; body at most 64 KB (413, after Next has buffered up to 6 MB of it for the proxy, rules 693 and 774); only the provider task id is read and format-checked (400); kie's `X-Webhook-Signature` (HMAC-SHA256 over `{taskId}.{timestamp}`, ±300 s) checked with `sameSecret` when `KIE_WEBHOOK_HMAC_KEY` is set (401), while Higgsfield does not sign; the job is found through the stored `mjobref` pointer and its stored scope; the status is re-fetched with our key under the job's 4 s throttle; 200 whether or not a job matches. |
| External MCP client to `/api/mcp` and OAuth server | Bearer tokens `thakeen_...` (an older `nashr_...` is still accepted, rule 1813); OAuth client registration, codes, PKCE verifiers, refresh tokens | `userFromBearer` (owner accounts only, actor kind `token`); every tool mapped to a role action (`MCP_TOOL_ACTION` in `src/lib/mcp.ts`); OAuth consent owner only; open dynamic client registration; PKCE S256 required at approve and token; redirect URI exact match; CORS `*` on MCP and OAuth endpoints. |
| hPanel cron to app server | `CRON_SECRET` bearer | `sameSecret` comparison in each GET cron route of `crons.json`, and in `/api/cron/secretary` as the alternative to a QStash signature. |
| Founder to console | Since 2026-10-13 (rules 1630-1654, branch `claude/staff-identity`): first a staff login (`__Host-thakeen_staff`, `SameSite=Strict`, password plus the person's own authenticator code, 15 minutes idle and 12 hours at most, every write behind `consoleWriteRefusal` and a step-up tied to the staff session); the key form below is break-glass and the founder customer session expand only. Before: `WAITLIST_ADMIN_KEY` (header `x-admin-key`, or once through the key form, which opens a server-side key session that ends after 15 minutes without an admin request, 12 hours at most; never the URL, never in a cookie) or a founder session | `adminActor` / `isAdmin` / `isAdminCookie` (`src/lib/auth.ts`): the session path requires the founder's own owner login (`isFounderActor`), so a member of a founder's account and a founder impersonating never pass; see `permissions.md` section 4. |
| Invitee to the join page and `/api/team/accept` (2026-09-29) | The invite token in the link's URL fragment (read by the page's script, sent in a JSON body, never a query string); name, password | Only the token's SHA-256 is stored (`invite:{hash}`, 7 days); single use under `inviteuse:{hash}`; the invite (record and index entry), the owner (existence, suspension, seat limit) and the inviter re-read under `lock:team:{owner}`, and the address under `lock:login:{email}`; the preview applies the same inviter check; per-IP rate limits for preview and accept. |
| Member login to the owner's account (2026-09-29) | A session naming the member's own login | Re-resolved on every request (`currentUser`); the member acts in the owner's workspace under the role table; its password, sessions and reset links are its own and never the owner's. |

## 6. Environments (2026-10-08)

Spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-08-staging-smooth-releases-design.md` sections 3 and 4; the steps from branch to live are `docs/system/flows.md` ("Releases: staging, release pull request, rollback"), the lock is flows section 51. Sections 1, 2.6 and 5 above were written when the site ran on Vercel and were brought over to Hostinger on 2026-10-10 (rules 987-990); thakeen.com runs on Hostinger (webpack build since 2026-10-08). Once the Vercel project is disconnected, pull requests get no preview deploy, and CI builds the way Hostinger does (Node 22, `npm run build:hostinger`, rule 1017); the host itself (its env vars, its Node process) is proven only by staging.

Two Node.js web apps on one Hostinger Business order (the plan includes five, so staging adds no hosting cost). Status on 2026-10-13: the `production` branch exists (tip `dff95f25`, release pull request #336, tag `live-2026-10-12-1`) and thakeen.com deploys from it, so a merge to `main` does not reach customers; the staging app is unverified from the repository (the 2026-10-11 study found staging.thakeen.com answering 404, with no Git link set). Setup order for staging: the app is created with Git auto-deploy off and no build; auto-deploy (branch `main`) is turned on, and the first build started, only after the code pull request that carries the lock has merged into `main` and `APP_ENV=staging` and the other staging variables are set (plan Tasks 12 and 13), because a build of a `main` without the lock would serve an open copy of the site.

| | Live | Staging |
|---|---|---|
| Address | `https://thakeen.com` | `https://staging.thakeen.com` (`APP_ORIGIN` is its own origin, so links, robots and the access redirect stay on it) |
| Git auto-deploy branch | `production` (since 2026-10-08) | `main`, switched on after the lock has merged (see the status above) |
| What moves it | The founder's merge of a release pull request `main` to `production` | Every merge into `main` |
| Build | `npm run build:hostinger` (`next build --webpack`), Node 22, npm, output `.next` | The same settings, the same build. CI builds the same way since rule 1017 (`setup-node` 22, `npm run build:hostinger`); since rule 1207 the end-to-end server starts that one build with `APP_ENV=test` at runtime instead of building again (`e2e/web-server.ts`; a local run still builds with the same script) |
| `APP_ENV` | Unset | `staging`: still production for rules 430 and 431 (never the memory store, never the model mock), and it turns the lock on |
| Access | Public | Locked: a bare 404 on every path but the access page, its route and `/robots.txt`, unless the request carries the `thakeen_staging` cookie (`STAGING_ACCESS_KEY` decides who gets in; rules 674 and 675) |
| Indexing | `robots.txt` and sitemap as in 2.1 | Disallow everything, empty sitemap, `X-Robots-Tag: noindex, nofollow` on every response the proxy handles (the static assets, `/_next/image` and `/favicon.ico` do not pass through it; rule 677) |
| Redis | The live Upstash database, customer data | A new, empty Upstash database (`KV_REST_API_URL`, `KV_REST_API_TOKEN`); test accounts made by Claude; customer data is never copied in |
| Anthropic | The live key | Its own key with a low monthly limit set in the Anthropic console: real chat and agents, bounded spend, visible apart from live |
| Secrets | The live values | New values for every secret in `variables.md` (`CRON_SECRET`, `CONNECT_SECRET` and the rest): nothing signed or encrypted on staging is valid on live, and a staging `CRON_SECRET` can never call a live cron |
| Crons | The schedules of 2.6, run by hPanel crons (read-only list checked 2026-10-08: the eight schedules now in `crons.json`, then `vercel.json`, plus `*/15 * * * *` for `/api/cron/secretary`) | None scheduled; when a release touches a cron, Claude calls that one route by hand with the staging `CRON_SECRET` and the access cookie |
| `GET /api/health` | Public, shows the release commit | The same route behind the lock; the scripts send the cookie |

Left unset on staging: `RESEND_API_KEY` and `MAIL_FROM` (`mailReady()` is false, so staging sends no email), payment, connector and media provider credentials (added one by one, as test credentials, only when a change needs them), and `TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY` and `TURNSTILE_SECRET_KEY` (unset means not checked; the lock already keeps strangers out). `docs/system/variables.md` is the authoritative list of names; the founder copies each from the live app into hPanel and gives it the staging value above. Values are entered by the founder in hPanel, never in chat and never committed.

Health and monitoring (rules 1011-1016): `GET /api/health` is still `{ ok, commit, built }`; `?store=1` adds a one-key Redis read (`store: { ok, ms }`, 503 when it fails) and `?crons=1` (cron bearer) adds the heartbeat state of the eight cron routes from `src/lib/cron-heartbeat.ts`. Each cron route writes `cronbeat:{job}` when it finishes; `GET /api/admin/overview` carries the ages as `crons` and the Usage page shows them. The external uptime monitor is a founder step (flows section 62).

Request time limit (finding B-004, not measured): the routes' `maxDuration` (60 to 300 s) is read only by Vercel. On Hostinger the CDN, LiteSpeed and the Node host decide how long a request or a stream may run, and nothing has measured it; the deadlines (`deadlineFor`, `src/lib/media/keys.ts`) still assume `maxDuration`. The staging test is `docs/release/rollback.md` section 6. Whether the CDN purges prerendered pages (`s-maxage=31536000`) on a new build is not measured either (F-023, section 5 there).

Build identity: `next.config.ts` inlines `BUILD_COMMIT` and `BUILD_TIME`, and `GET /api/health` answers them. Both apps are built from a Git checkout, so `commit` is expected to be a real sha; if the build area has no `.git` it is `unknown` and the host's build record (`hosting_nodejs_list-builds`, `options.source_options.commit.hash`) names the commit instead (spec section 12).

Admin workspace view and suspension end dates (2026-10-17, branch `claude/wave2-admin-team-suspension-853778`, rules 2070-2083; flows.md section 84) add one route file and one handler: `GET /api/admin/users/{email}/workspace` (on the `handler()` wrapper with `auth: "admin"`, then `staffMay(consoleRole(req), "workspace.view")`, then `workspaceView`, then the audit row, then the answer). `src/lib/roles.ts` gains `STAFF_MAY` / `staffMay` (console permissions by staff role, fail closed) and `src/lib/auth.ts` gains `consoleRole(req)` (the stored staff role for a staff session, `owner` for the key, the key header and the founder's customer session). `team.ts` gains `peekInvites` (read only), `devices.ts` gains `lastSignIn` (read only). The account sheet gains a sixth tab, Workspace (`src/components/admin/WorkspacePanel.tsx`). No new Redis key and no new environment variable.
